===== migrations/m20260101_000000_search_documents.rs =====
//! The site index of the `search` module: one row per thing a visitor can
//! find. `brocanth_search::documents::upsert` writes a row; /search reads
//! them.
//!
//! Written by `brocanth create`, not by `migrate make`: the generated
//! `tsvector` column and the GIN indexes are not in a model. Run
//! `migrate sync` once, so migrations/mod.rs lists this file.

use brocanth_model::sea_orm_migration::prelude::*;

pub struct Migration;

impl MigrationName for Migration {
    fn name(&self) -> &str {
        "m20260101_000000_search_documents"
    }
}

#[async_trait::async_trait]
impl MigrationTrait for Migration {
    async fn up(&self, manager: &SchemaManager) -> Result<(), DbErr> {
        manager.get_connection().execute_unprepared(UP).await?;
        Ok(())
    }

    async fn down(&self, manager: &SchemaManager) -> Result<(), DbErr> {
        manager.get_connection().execute_unprepared(DOWN).await?;
        Ok(())
    }
}

const UP: &str = r#"CREATE TABLE "search_documents" (
    "id" bigserial PRIMARY KEY,
    "kind" text NOT NULL,
    "key" text NOT NULL,
    "title" text NOT NULL,
    "body" text NOT NULL DEFAULT '',
    "url" text NOT NULL,
    UNIQUE ("kind", "key")
);
CREATE EXTENSION IF NOT EXISTS pg_trgm;
ALTER TABLE "search_documents" ADD COLUMN "search" tsvector GENERATED ALWAYS AS (
    setweight(to_tsvector('english', coalesce("title", '')), 'A') ||
    setweight(to_tsvector('english', coalesce("body", '')), 'C')
) STORED;
CREATE INDEX "search_documents_search_idx" ON "search_documents" USING gin ("search");
CREATE INDEX "search_documents_title_trgm_idx" ON "search_documents" USING gin ("title" gin_trgm_ops);

"#;

const DOWN: &str = r#"DROP TABLE "search_documents";
"#;

===== src/assets.rs =====
//! Every static file this binary serves.
//!
//! Two directories, two rules. `assets/` is **fingerprinted** -- `site.css`
//! is published at `/css/site.<hash>.css`, so it can be cached forever and a
//! new build invalidates it by having a different name. `public/` is served
//! **verbatim**, because `robots.txt`, `favicon.ico` and anything linked to
//! from outside must keep the name they were given.
//!
//! Write a reference with `assets::url`, never by hand:
//!
//! ```ignore
//! <link rel="stylesheet" href={assets::url("css/site.css")}>
//! ```
//!
//! A name nothing publishes panics there rather than 404ing in a browser.
//!
//! Embedded, like the templates and the route table, so `serve` works from
//! any directory and nothing can go missing after the build passed. A file
//! added where there was none needs `touch src/assets.rs`, because nothing
//! referenced it yet.

brocanth_assets::assets!("assets", "public");
===== src/bin/migrate.rs =====
//! The `migrate` tool: write migrations from the models in `src/models/`.
//!
//! ```text
//! cargo run --bin migrate -- ids              # an id on each model and field
//! cargo run --bin migrate -- make add_posts   # a migration for what changed
//! cargo run --bin migrate -- sync             # list every file in migrations/mod.rs
//! ```
//!
//! A development tool, and a binary of its own: it parses Rust source, and
//! the server has no reason to link a parser. It applies nothing. Apply
//! with the application's own `migrate up`.

fn main() -> std::process::ExitCode {
    brocanth_migrate::tool::main()
}
===== src/commands/admin.rs =====
//! `admin eject` and `admin diff` -- shipped admin screens, copied into
//! this project and compared with the shipped ones.
//!
//! The work is the `brocanth` CLI's. This command runs
//! `brocanth admin eject <screen> [--model <key>]` or
//! `brocanth admin diff [<screen>] [--model <key>]` in the project root and
//! exits with its status, so `cargo run -- admin eject list` and
//! `brocanth admin eject list` do the same thing. The application does not
//! link the code that writes source.
//!
//! This file is yours. See `docs/admin-customization.md`, level 8.

use std::ffi::OsStr;
use std::path::Path;
use std::process;

use brocanth_command::{anyhow, clap, cmd, Command, Context};

/// Work on the admin: `eject` copies a shipped screen into this project,
/// `diff` compares the copies with the shipped screens.
#[cmd]
#[derive(clap::Args)]
pub struct Admin {
    #[command(subcommand)]
    pub action: Action,
}

#[derive(clap::Subcommand)]
pub enum Action {
    /// Copy a shipped screen into this project and install it over the
    /// default. `brocanth admin eject --help` lists the screens.
    Eject {
        /// The screen to copy, such as `list` or `layout`.
        screen: String,
        /// Install the copy for this model only. list, detail, form and
        /// delete take one.
        #[arg(long, value_name = "KEY")]
        model: Option<String>,
    },
    /// Compare ejected screens with the shipped ones of the installed
    /// brocanth-admin. Run it after an upgrade.
    Diff {
        /// The screen to compare. Without one, every ejected copy.
        screen: Option<String>,
        /// Compare the copy for this model only.
        #[arg(long, value_name = "KEY", requires = "screen")]
        model: Option<String>,
    },
}

impl Command for Admin {
    fn run(self, ctx: &mut Context) -> anyhow::Result<()> {
        let status = brocanth(OsStr::new("brocanth"), ctx.root(), &self.action.args())?;
        if !status.success() {
            // `brocanth` printed the error and its help line.
            process::exit(status.code().unwrap_or(1));
        }
        Ok(())
    }
}

impl Action {
    /// The `brocanth` arguments this action stands for.
    fn args(&self) -> Vec<String> {
        match self {
            Action::Eject { screen, model } => {
                let mut args = vec!["admin".to_string(), "eject".to_string(), screen.clone()];
                if let Some(model) = model {
                    args.extend(["--model".to_string(), model.clone()]);
                }
                args
            }
            Action::Diff { screen, model } => {
                let mut args = vec!["admin".to_string(), "diff".to_string()];
                args.extend(screen.clone());
                if let Some(model) = model {
                    args.extend(["--model".to_string(), model.clone()]);
                }
                args
            }
        }
    }
}

/// Runs `program` with `args` in `root`. A program that is not installed
/// is an error that says how to install it.
fn brocanth(program: &OsStr, root: &Path, args: &[String]) -> anyhow::Result<process::ExitStatus> {
    let name = program.to_string_lossy();
    process::Command::new(program)
        .args(args)
        .current_dir(root)
        .status()
        .map_err(|e| match e.kind() {
            std::io::ErrorKind::NotFound => anyhow::anyhow!(
                "`{name}` is not installed or not on PATH\n\
                 help: install the brocanth CLI: `cargo install --git \
                 https://github.com/DHHwasRight/brocanth brocanth-cli`, or \
                 `cargo install --path crates/brocanth-cli` in a brocanth checkout"
            ),
            _ => anyhow::anyhow!("could not run `{name}`: {e}"),
        })
}

#[cfg(test)]
mod tests {
    use super::*;

    #[test]
    fn eject_passes_the_screen_and_the_model_through() {
        let action = Action::Eject {
            screen: "list".to_string(),
            model: Some("post".to_string()),
        };
        assert_eq!(action.args(), ["admin", "eject", "list", "--model", "post"]);
        let action = Action::Eject {
            screen: "layout".to_string(),
            model: None,
        };
        assert_eq!(action.args(), ["admin", "eject", "layout"]);
    }

    #[test]
    fn diff_passes_the_screen_and_the_model_through() {
        let action = Action::Diff {
            screen: Some("list".to_string()),
            model: Some("post".to_string()),
        };
        assert_eq!(action.args(), ["admin", "diff", "list", "--model", "post"]);
        let action = Action::Diff {
            screen: None,
            model: None,
        };
        assert_eq!(action.args(), ["admin", "diff"]);
    }

    #[test]
    fn a_missing_cli_says_how_to_install_it() {
        let error = brocanth(
            OsStr::new("brocanth-that-is-not-installed"),
            Path::new("."),
            &[],
        )
        .expect_err("no such program");
        let message = error.to_string();
        assert!(
            message.contains("not installed or not on PATH"),
            "{message}"
        );
        assert!(
            message.contains("help: install the brocanth CLI"),
            "{message}"
        );
    }
}
===== src/commands/api.rs =====
//! This application, from the outside.
//!
//! `cargo run -- api --help` lists every operation, because the list is
//! `crate::pages::described()` -- the same table the OpenAPI document is
//! built from, read off the handler signatures while this crate compiled.
//! There is nothing to regenerate: add a page and the client grows a
//! subcommand.
//!
//! ```text
//! $ cargo run -- api signup-post --handle ada-lovelace --age 36
//! {"id":"0191a3f0-0000-7000-8000-000000000000","handle":"ada-lovelace"}
//! ```
//!
//! The flags come from the request body's own schema, and their help text
//! is the sentence each field carries. Nothing here re-checks a rule: the
//! server is the authority, and a client that validated too would be a
//! second opinion that can disagree with it.

use brocanth_command::{anyhow, clap, cmd, Command, Context};

/// Call this application's own API.
#[cmd]
#[derive(clap::Args)]
pub struct Api {
    /// The operation and its flags. `api --help` lists them.
    #[arg(trailing_var_arg = true, allow_hyphen_values = true, num_args = 0..)]
    pub rest: Vec<String>,
}

impl Command for Api {
    fn run(self, _ctx: &mut Context) -> anyhow::Result<()> {
        let routes = crate::pages::described();
        let wire = brocanth_client::Reqwest::new().map_err(anyhow::Error::msg)?;

        let mut argv: Vec<std::ffi::OsString> = vec!["api".into()];
        argv.extend(self.rest.into_iter().map(std::ffi::OsString::from));

        let answered =
            brocanth_client::run("api", &routes, &wire, argv).map_err(anyhow::Error::msg)?;

        println!("{}", answered.body);
        // The status is the exit code, so `api ... && something` means what
        // it looks like in a shell. The body is still printed, because a
        // 422 from this framework carries every fault and that is the part
        // worth reading.
        if !answered.ok() {
            std::process::exit(1);
        }
        Ok(())
    }
}
===== src/commands/check.rs =====
//! `check` -- what is wrong before it is live.
//!
//! Reads the configuration of one environment the way the application does,
//! `brocanth.toml`, and `src/` as text. Nothing is built and nothing
//! connects, so it runs in CI. `--deploy` adds the production checks; the
//! list is in `brocanth_config::check`. `brocanth check` runs this command
//! after the checks only the CLI can make.
//!
//! Ordinary project code, like `serve`: rename it or delete it, and delete
//! its line in `src/commands/mod.rs`.

use brocanth_command::{anyhow, clap, cmd, Command, Context};
use brocanth_config::check::{self, Options};

/// Check the configuration and the source; --deploy adds the production checks.
#[cmd]
#[derive(clap::Args)]
pub struct Check {
    /// Add the production checks: secrets, cookies, CSRF, the admin, the database
    #[arg(long)]
    deploy: bool,
    /// The environment to load [default: production with --deploy, else $<APP>_ENV or development]
    #[arg(long, short, value_name = "ENV")]
    env: Option<String>,
    /// Silence one finding by its id, such as deploy/hsts. Repeat for more
    #[arg(long, value_name = "ID")]
    allow: Vec<String>,
    /// Exit 1 on a warning too
    #[arg(long)]
    strict: bool,
}

impl Command for Check {
    fn run(self, ctx: &mut Context) -> anyhow::Result<()> {
        let options = Options {
            deploy: self.deploy,
            env: self.env,
            allow: self.allow,
        };
        let report = check::run(crate::app::APP, ctx.root(), &options)?;
        eprint!("{report}");
        if report.failed(self.strict) {
            std::process::exit(1);
        }
        Ok(())
    }
}
===== src/commands/createsuperuser.rs =====
//! `createsuperuser` -- an operator account that passes every check of the
//! admin.
//!
//! Opens the accounts at `[auth] url` and makes the account with the
//! address a superuser. It creates the account first when there is none,
//! with the password in the variable that `[superuser] password_env` names.
//! An account that exists keeps its password. Run it twice and nothing
//! changes.
//!
//! Ordinary project code, like `serve`: rename it or delete it, and delete
//! its line in `src/commands/mod.rs`.

use std::sync::Arc;

use brocanth_auth::{
    ensure_superuser, Accounts, Passwords, PgStore, SuperuserOutcome, SystemClock,
};
use brocanth_command::{anyhow, clap, cmd, Command, Context};

/// Make the account with this address a superuser, creating it if needed
#[cmd]
#[derive(clap::Args)]
pub struct Createsuperuser {
    /// The account's address
    #[arg(long, value_name = "ADDRESS")]
    email: String,
}

impl Command for Createsuperuser {
    fn run(self, ctx: &mut Context) -> anyhow::Result<()> {
        std::env::set_current_dir(ctx.root())?;
        let (auth, superuser) = settings()?;
        let name = superuser.password_env;
        let password = std::env::var(&name).unwrap_or_default();
        let runtime = tokio::runtime::Builder::new_current_thread()
            .enable_all()
            .build()?;
        let outcome = runtime.block_on(async {
            let store = Arc::new(PgStore::connect(&auth.url).await?);
            store.migrate().await?;
            let accounts = Accounts::new(
                Arc::clone(&store),
                Arc::new(SystemClock),
                Arc::new(Passwords::recommended()?),
            )?;
            anyhow::Ok(ensure_superuser(&accounts, store.as_ref(), &self.email, &password).await)
        })?;
        match outcome {
            Ok(SuperuserOutcome::Created(_)) => println!("created superuser {}", self.email),
            Ok(SuperuserOutcome::Promoted(_)) => println!("{} is now a superuser", self.email),
            Ok(SuperuserOutcome::Unchanged(_)) => println!("{} is a superuser already", self.email),
            // A new account with no password: say where it goes.
            Err(e) if password.is_empty() => anyhow::bail!(
                "{e}\nhelp: a new account needs a password; export {name}=<a long passphrase>"
            ),
            Err(e) => return Err(e.into()),
        }
        Ok(())
    }
}

/// `[auth]` and `[superuser]`, through the loader the server uses.
fn settings() -> anyhow::Result<(
    crate::plugins::auth::Settings,
    crate::plugins::superuser::Settings,
)> {
    let loaded = brocanth_config::Loader::new(crate::app::APP).load()?;
    Ok((loaded.section("auth")?, loaded.section("superuser")?))
}
===== src/commands/css.rs =====
//! `css` -- build assets/css/site.css from assets/css/_tailwind.css.
//!
//!     brocanth css          once, minified
//!     brocanth css -W       again on each change
//!
//! Ordinary project code, like `serve`: rename it, give it more flags, or
//! delete it. It is here rather than in a shell script because this project
//! already has a command line, and a second way to run things is a second
//! thing to keep in step.
//!
//! The Tailwind CLI comes out of `node_modules` and there is no fallback to
//! `npx`. A fallback downloads some other version and hides the one fact
//! worth knowing, which is that nobody ran `npm install`.

use std::path::Path;
use std::process::Command as Child;

use brocanth_command::{anyhow, clap, cmd, Command, Context};

/// Build the stylesheet from the Tailwind input.
#[cmd]
#[derive(clap::Args)]
pub struct Css {
    /// Rebuild on each change instead of once.
    #[arg(short = 'W', long)]
    pub watch: bool,
}

const INPUT: &str = "assets/css/_tailwind.css";
const OUTPUT: &str = "assets/css/site.css";
const CLI: &str = "node_modules/.bin/tailwindcss";

impl Command for Css {
    fn run(self, ctx: &mut Context) -> anyhow::Result<()> {
        // The project root and not the working directory: `brocanth css`
        // runs from wherever you happen to be, and every path below is
        // written relative to the project.
        let root = ctx.root();
        let cli = root.join(CLI);
        if !cli.is_file() {
            anyhow::bail!(
                "the Tailwind CLI is absent at {}\n       run `npm install` in {} first",
                cli.display(),
                root.display()
            );
        }

        let mut child = Child::new(&cli);
        child
            .current_dir(root)
            .args(["--input", INPUT, "--output", OUTPUT]);
        if self.watch {
            child.arg("--watch");
        } else {
            child.arg("--minify");
        }

        let status = child.status()?;
        if !status.success() {
            // No message beyond this: the CLI has already written its own
            // on the stderr this process inherited.
            anyhow::bail!("the Tailwind CLI failed");
        }

        // `--watch` only ever returns when it was interrupted, and the
        // output it leaves behind is whatever the last change produced.
        // Checking it, or touching the module below, would both be about a
        // build that is over.
        if self.watch {
            return Ok(());
        }

        let written = root.join(OUTPUT);
        let bytes = std::fs::metadata(&written)?.len();
        if bytes == 0 {
            anyhow::bail!("the build wrote no CSS to {}", written.display());
        }

        touch(&root.join("src/assets.rs"))?;
        println!("built {OUTPUT} ({bytes} bytes)");
        Ok(())
    }
}

/// Make cargo notice a stylesheet it has already compiled in.
///
/// The CSS is embedded at compile time, so a new one is a new binary, and
/// nothing rebuilds until the module that embeds it looks newer than the
/// artefact. Rewriting its own bytes is the portable way to say so: there is
/// no `utimes` in `std`, and a dependency for one call is not worth it.
fn touch(path: &Path) -> anyhow::Result<()> {
    let same = std::fs::read(path)?;
    std::fs::write(path, same)?;
    Ok(())
}
===== src/commands/dbshell.rs =====
//! `dbshell` -- the database's own client, on this application's database.
//!
//! Reads `[db] url` the way the server does and replaces itself with `psql`
//! or `sqlite3` on it. `--print` shows the command line; a password goes in
//! `PGPASSWORD` and never on it. See `brocanth_db::shell`.
//!
//! Ordinary project code, like `serve`: rename it or delete it, and delete
//! its line in `src/commands/mod.rs`.

use std::ffi::OsString;

use brocanth_command::{anyhow, clap, cmd, Command, Context};

/// Open psql or sqlite3 on the database in `[db] url`
#[cmd]
#[derive(clap::Args)]
pub struct Dbshell {
    /// The environment whose `[db] url` to open [default: $<APP>_ENV, or development]
    #[arg(long, short, value_name = "ENV")]
    env: Option<String>,
    /// Print the command line instead of running it. The password is not printed
    #[arg(long)]
    print: bool,
    /// Passed to psql or sqlite3 unchanged, after `--`
    #[arg(last = true, value_name = "CLIENT ARGS")]
    rest: Vec<OsString>,
}

impl Command for Dbshell {
    fn run(self, ctx: &mut Context) -> anyhow::Result<()> {
        let url = url(self.env.as_deref(), ctx.root())?;
        brocanth_db::shell::run(&url, ctx.root(), self.print, &self.rest)?;
        Ok(())
    }
}

/// `[db] url`, through the loader the server uses, so the files,
/// `$<APP>_ENV` and `$<APP>_DB__URL` agree with what the server sees.
fn url(env: Option<&str>, root: &std::path::Path) -> anyhow::Result<String> {
    let mut loader = brocanth_config::Loader::new(crate::app::APP).cwd(root);
    if let Some(env) = env {
        loader = loader.env(env);
    }
    Ok(loader
        .load()?
        .section::<crate::plugins::db::Settings>("db")?
        .url)
}
===== src/commands/migrate.rs =====
//! `migrate` -- apply the migrations in `migrations/` to `[db] url`.
//!
//! `up`, `down [N]` and `status` run in this binary, because the
//! migrations are compiled into it. Where there is no `brocanth` -- a
//! container, a release job -- run the binary itself: `<binary> migrate up`.
//! `brocanth migrate up` hands over to this command too.
//!
//! `ids`, `make <NAME>` and `sync` write source, so they run the `migrate`
//! tool, `src/bin/migrate.rs`, through cargo. The tool is a binary of its
//! own so this one links no Rust parser.

use std::collections::BTreeSet;

use brocanth_command::{anyhow, clap, cmd, Command, Context};
use brocanth_db::{Db, DbConfig};
use brocanth_model::sea_orm_migration::{MigrationStatus, MigratorTrait};
use brocanth_model::Orm;

use crate::migrations::Migrator;

/// Apply, roll back or list the migrations in migrations/
#[cmd]
#[derive(clap::Args)]
pub struct Migrate {
    #[command(subcommand)]
    action: Action,
}

#[derive(clap::Subcommand)]
enum Action {
    /// Apply each pending migration, oldest first
    Up,
    /// Roll back the newest N applied migrations
    Down {
        /// How many migrations to roll back
        #[arg(value_name = "N", default_value_t = 1)]
        n: u32,
    },
    /// List each migration as applied or pending
    Status,
    /// Write an id on each model and field in src/models that has none
    Ids,
    /// Write a migration for what changed in src/models since the last one
    Make {
        /// The name of the migration, in snake case: add_posts, rename_title
        #[arg(value_name = "NAME")]
        name: String,
        /// Write the snapshot and no migration
        #[arg(long)]
        snapshot_only: bool,
    },
    /// List every migrations/m*.rs file in migrations/mod.rs
    Sync,
}

impl Command for Migrate {
    fn run(self, ctx: &mut Context) -> anyhow::Result<()> {
        // The config files resolve from the project root, wherever the
        // command started.
        std::env::set_current_dir(ctx.root())?;
        match self.action {
            Action::Ids => tool(&["ids"]),
            Action::Sync => tool(&["sync"]),
            Action::Make {
                name,
                snapshot_only,
            } => {
                let mut args = vec!["make", name.as_str()];
                if snapshot_only {
                    args.push("--snapshot-only");
                }
                tool(&args)
            }
            Action::Up => apply(Apply::Up),
            Action::Down { n } => apply(Apply::Down(n)),
            Action::Status => apply(Apply::Status),
        }
    }
}

/// What `up`, `down` and `status` do to the database.
enum Apply {
    Up,
    Down(u32),
    Status,
}

fn apply(what: Apply) -> anyhow::Result<()> {
    unlisted()?;
    let config = database()?;
    tokio::runtime::Runtime::new()?.block_on(async {
        // The pool the server opens, and the ORM over it, as the `model`
        // plugin builds it.
        let pool = Db::connect(&config).await?;
        let orm = Orm::from_db(&pool);
        let db = orm.connection();
        let before = Migrator::get_migration_with_status(db).await?;
        let named = |status: MigrationStatus| -> Vec<String> {
            before
                .iter()
                .filter(|m| m.status() == status)
                .map(|m| m.name().to_string())
                .collect()
        };
        match what {
            Apply::Up => {
                let pending = named(MigrationStatus::Pending);
                Migrator::up(db, None).await?;
                if pending.is_empty() {
                    println!("Nothing to apply: each migration is applied.");
                }
                for name in pending {
                    println!("applied      {name}");
                }
            }
            Apply::Down(n) => {
                let applied = named(MigrationStatus::Applied);
                Migrator::down(db, Some(n)).await?;
                if applied.is_empty() {
                    println!("Nothing to roll back: no migration is applied.");
                }
                for name in applied.iter().rev().take(n as usize) {
                    println!("rolled back  {name}");
                }
            }
            Apply::Status => {
                if before.is_empty() {
                    println!("No migration in migrations/.");
                }
                for m in &before {
                    let status = match m.status() {
                        MigrationStatus::Applied => "applied",
                        MigrationStatus::Pending => "pending",
                    };
                    println!("{status:<12} {}", m.name());
                }
            }
        }
        Ok(())
    })
}

/// Runs the `migrate` tool with these arguments, from the project root.
fn tool(args: &[&str]) -> anyhow::Result<()> {
    let status = std::process::Command::new("cargo")
        .args(["run", "--quiet", "--bin", "migrate", "--"])
        .args(args)
        .status()
        .map_err(|e| {
            anyhow::anyhow!(
                "cannot run cargo: {e}\n\
                 help: run `cargo run --bin migrate -- {}` where cargo is installed",
                args.join(" ")
            )
        })?;
    if !status.success() {
        std::process::exit(status.code().unwrap_or(1));
    }
    Ok(())
}

/// Refuses when a file in `migrations/` is not in the list this binary was
/// built with: it would be skipped without a word. A `.sql` file is the
/// same case: the SQL migrations of a project with `db` and no `model`.
fn unlisted() -> anyhow::Result<()> {
    let mut sql: Vec<String> = std::fs::read_dir("migrations")
        .map(|dir| {
            dir.flatten()
                .map(|entry| entry.file_name().to_string_lossy().into_owned())
                .filter(|name| name.ends_with(".sql"))
                .collect()
        })
        .unwrap_or_default();
    sql.sort();
    if !sql.is_empty() {
        anyhow::bail!(
            "migrations/ holds SQL migrations ({}), and this command applies only the \
             migrations of `model`\n\
             help: the SQL files made tables that are already there. Record them with \
             `migrate make <name> --snapshot-only`, then move the .sql files out of \
             migrations/, for example to migrations/sql/",
            sql.join(", ")
        );
    }
    let built: BTreeSet<String> = Migrator::migrations()
        .iter()
        .map(|m| m.name().to_string())
        .collect();
    let mut missing: Vec<String> = std::fs::read_dir("migrations")
        .map(|dir| {
            dir.flatten()
                .filter_map(|entry| {
                    let name = entry.file_name().to_string_lossy().into_owned();
                    let stem = name.strip_suffix(".rs")?.to_string();
                    (stem.starts_with('m') && stem != "mod" && !built.contains(&stem))
                        .then_some(stem)
                })
                .collect()
        })
        .unwrap_or_default();
    missing.sort();
    if missing.is_empty() {
        return Ok(());
    }
    anyhow::bail!(
        "this binary does not contain {}: migrations/mod.rs does not list it\n\
         help: run `migrate sync` (or `cargo run --bin migrate -- sync`), then run this \
         command again",
        missing.join(", ")
    )
}

/// The pool's configuration: `[db]`, through the loader the server uses, so
/// the files, `$<APP>_ENV` and `$<APP>_DB__URL` agree with what the server
/// sees.
fn database() -> anyhow::Result<DbConfig> {
    let config = brocanth_config::Loader::new(crate::app::APP).load()?;
    Ok(config
        .section::<crate::plugins::db::Settings>("db")?
        .config())
}
===== src/commands/routes.rs =====
//! Every route this application answers.
//!
//! `brocanth routes` builds this project and runs this command, so the
//! listing is the binary's own answer rather than a second reading of the
//! directory. The rows are `crate::pages::handlers()`, read off `src/pages`
//! while the crate compiled: a route is a file, so there is nothing to
//! regenerate and nothing that can fall behind.
//!
//! ```text
//! $ brocanth routes
//! VERB    PATTERN        HANDLER                      TAKES   GIVES
//! GET     /              pages::index::handle         -       -
//! GET     /about         pages::about::handle         -       -
//! POST    /signup        pages::signup::handle_post   Signup  Created
//! ```
//!
//! `TAKES` and `GIVES` are the body a handler reads and the `Json<T>` it
//! returns, from `crate::pages::described()`. A page that writes
//! `-> Result<impl IntoResponse, Error>` says neither, and prints `-`: the
//! wrapper is the opt-in, and these columns report it rather than ask for
//! it.
//!
//! An argument keeps the rows that contain it, in any column, so
//! `routes signup` is the one page and `routes POST` is every route that
//! writes.
//!
//! These are the pages. A plugin that mounts its own routes through a
//! `RouteSource` would not be in the list, and nothing this project has
//! mounts one -- so today the two are the same table. Say so here if that
//! stops being true.

use brocanth_command::{anyhow, clap, cmd, Command, Context};

/// List the routes this application answers.
#[cmd]
#[derive(clap::Args)]
pub struct Routes {
    /// Keep only the rows containing this text
    pub filter: Option<String>,
}

impl Command for Routes {
    fn run(self, _ctx: &mut Context) -> anyhow::Result<()> {
        let (header, mut rows) = table();
        if let Some(filter) = &self.filter {
            rows.retain(|row| row.iter().any(|cell| cell.contains(filter)));
        }
        if rows.is_empty() {
            // An application with no page and a filter that matched nothing
            // both print nothing, and only one of the two is the reader's
            // own doing.
            match &self.filter {
                Some(filter) => println!("no route matches `{filter}`"),
                None => println!("this application answers no route"),
            }
            return Ok(());
        }

        let mut widths: Vec<usize> = header.iter().map(|title| title.chars().count()).collect();
        for row in &rows {
            for (column, cell) in row.iter().enumerate() {
                widths[column] = widths[column].max(cell.chars().count());
            }
        }

        print_row(&widths, header.iter().copied());
        for row in &rows {
            print_row(&widths, row.iter().map(String::as_str));
        }
        Ok(())
    }
}

/// One line, every cell padded to its column but the last.
///
/// The last is left alone, because trailing spaces are invisible until
/// something pipes this into a diff.
fn print_row<'a>(widths: &[usize], cells: impl Iterator<Item = &'a str>) {
    let mut line = String::new();
    for (column, cell) in cells.enumerate() {
        if column > 0 {
            line.push_str("  ");
        }
        if column + 1 == widths.len() {
            line.push_str(cell);
        } else {
            line.push_str(&format!("{cell:<width$}", width = widths[column]));
        }
    }
    println!("{line}");
}

/// The header, and one row per route.
///
/// Five columns, because this project has `input`: `described()` read every
/// handler's signature while the crate compiled, so what a route takes and
/// gives is known here and worth a column.
fn table() -> (Vec<&'static str>, Vec<Vec<String>>) {
    let described = crate::pages::described();
    let rows = crate::pages::handlers()
        .iter()
        .map(|(verb, pattern, handler)| {
            let shape = described
                .iter()
                .find(|row| row.verb == *verb && row.path == *pattern);
            let takes = shape
                .and_then(|row| row.takes.as_ref())
                .and_then(|node| node.name())
                .unwrap_or("-");
            let gives = shape
                .and_then(|row| row.gives.as_ref())
                .and_then(|node| node.name())
                .unwrap_or("-");
            vec![
                verb.as_str().to_string(),
                (*pattern).to_string(),
                format!("pages::{handler}"),
                takes.to_string(),
                gives.to_string(),
            ]
        })
        .collect();
    (vec!["VERB", "PATTERN", "HANDLER", "TAKES", "GIVES"], rows)
}

pub fn About(title: &str, verb: &str, path: &str) -> Element {

        and nothing else, so a DELETE to it is a 405 carrying

===== src/pages/account/code.rs =====
//! `/account/code` -- the second factor, after the password passed.
//!
//! The password's proof waits in the `auth_pending` cookie, sealed with
//! the session key and good for a few minutes. Six digits are a TOTP code;
//! anything longer is a recovery code.

use brocanth_auth::AuthError;
use brocanth_middleware::Level;

use crate::pages::{html, views, Ctx, Error, Response};
use crate::plugins::auth_screens::{self as screens, csrf_field, see_other, PENDING};

#[derive(zod::Entry)]
pub struct Presented {
    #[check(zod::Text::new().min(6).max(64))]
    code: String,
}

pub fn handle(ctx: &mut Ctx) -> Result<Response, Error> {
    again(ctx, "", 200)
}

pub async fn handle_post(ctx: &mut Ctx) -> Result<Response, Error> {
    let auth = screens::services(ctx)?;
    let Some(proof) =
        screens::cookie(ctx, PENDING).and_then(|sealed| auth.sessions().unseal_proof(&sealed))
    else {
        screens::flash(ctx, Level::Error, "That took too long. Sign in again.");
        return Ok(see_other("/account/signin"));
    };
    let Some(form) = screens::form::<Presented>(ctx)? else {
        return again(ctx, "Enter the code your app shows.", 422);
    };
    let code: String = form.code.chars().filter(|c| !c.is_whitespace()).collect();
    let mfa = auth.mfa()?;
    let account = proof.account().clone();
    let second = if code.len() == 6 && code.bytes().all(|b| b.is_ascii_digit()) {
        mfa.verify_totp(&account, &code).await
    } else {
        mfa.verify_recovery_code(&account, &code)
            .await
            .map(|accepted| accepted.proof)
    };
    let second = match second {
        Ok(second) => second,
        Err(AuthError::InvalidCredentials) => {
            return again(ctx, "That code is wrong or already used.", 422)
        }
        Err(other) => return Err(other.into()),
    };
    screens::admit(ctx, &auth, proof.and(second)?).await
}

fn again(ctx: &mut Ctx, error: &str, status: u16) -> Result<Response, Error> {
    let csrf = csrf_field(ctx);
    let mut page = html!(ctx, views::account::screens::Code { csrf: &csrf, error })?;
    page.status = status;
    Ok(page)
}
===== src/pages/account/forgot.rs =====
//! `/account/forgot` -- a reset link by mail, if the address has an account.

use brocanth_auth::accounts::ResetOutcome;
use brocanth_middleware::Level;

use crate::pages::{html, views, Ctx, Error, IntoResponse, Response};
use crate::plugins::auth_screens::{self as screens, csrf_field, see_other};

#[derive(zod::Entry)]
pub struct Address {
    #[check(zod::Text::new().min(1).max(254))]
    email: String,
}

pub fn handle(ctx: &mut Ctx) -> Result<impl IntoResponse, Error> {
    let csrf = csrf_field(ctx);
    html!(ctx, views::account::screens::Forgot { csrf: &csrf })
}

/// The same answer whether or not the address has an account.
pub async fn handle_post(ctx: &mut Ctx) -> Result<Response, Error> {
    let auth = screens::services(ctx)?;
    let links = screens::screens(ctx)?;
    if let Some(form) = screens::form::<Address>(ctx)? {
        let requested = auth.accounts().request_password_reset(&form.email).await?;
        if let ResetOutcome::Issue { token, .. } = requested.outcome {
            let link = links.link(&format!("/account/reset/{}", token.secret()));
            let text = format!(
                "Choose a new password with this link:\n\n{link}\n\n\
                 If you did not ask for it, ignore this mail: your password stays.\n"
            );
            screens::mail(ctx, &form.email, "Reset your password", text).await?;
        }
    }
    screens::flash(
        ctx,
        Level::Info,
        "If that address has an account, a link to reset its password is on its way.",
    );
    Ok(see_other("/account/signin"))
}
===== src/pages/account/reset/[token].rs =====
//! `/account/reset/{token}` -- a new password, from the link in the mail.

use brocanth_auth::AuthError;
use brocanth_middleware::Level;

use crate::pages::{html, views, Ctx, Error, Response};
use crate::plugins::auth_screens::{self as screens, csrf_field, see_other};

#[derive(zod::Entry)]
pub struct NewPassword {
    #[check(zod::Text::new().min(12).max(1024))]
    password: String,
}

pub fn handle(ctx: &mut Ctx) -> Result<Response, Error> {
    again(ctx, "", 200)
}

/// A changed password ends every session of the account, which the crate
/// does by itself, so the person signs in again.
pub async fn handle_post(ctx: &mut Ctx) -> Result<Response, Error> {
    let auth = screens::services(ctx)?;
    let token = ctx.require("token")?.to_string();
    let Some(form) = screens::form::<NewPassword>(ctx)? else {
        return again(ctx, "Choose a password of 12 characters or more.", 422);
    };
    match auth.accounts().reset_password(&token, &form.password).await {
        Ok(account) => {
            crate::plugins::auth_lockout::lockout(&auth)?
                .clear(&account)
                .await?;
            screens::flash(
                ctx,
                Level::Success,
                "Your password is changed. Sign in with it.",
            );
            Ok(see_other("/account/signin"))
        }
        Err(AuthError::InvalidToken) => {
            screens::flash(
                ctx,
                Level::Error,
                "This link is spent or too old. Ask for a new one.",
            );
            Ok(see_other("/account/forgot"))
        }
        Err(other) => Err(other.into()),
    }
}

fn again(ctx: &mut Ctx, error: &str, status: u16) -> Result<Response, Error> {
    let csrf = csrf_field(ctx);
    let action = ctx.path.clone();
    let mut page = html!(
        ctx,
        views::account::screens::Reset {
            csrf: &csrf,
            action: &action,
            error,
        }
    )?;
    page.status = status;
    Ok(page)
}
===== src/pages/account/screens.rsx =====
// The markup of the account screens. One file, so the forms read alike;
// each page in this directory names its own component.
//
// `csrf` is the hidden input the CSRF hook minted for this request. It is
// HTML already, so it goes in with `{! }`. Nothing else here is raw.

fn Problem(text: &str) -> Element {
    if !text.is_empty() {
        <p role="alert">{text}</p>
    }
}

pub fn Signup(csrf: &str, email: &str, error: &str) -> Element {
    <section>
        <h1>Create an account</h1>
        <Problem text={error} />
        <form method="post" action="/account/signup">
            {! csrf}
            <label>Email <input type="email" name="email" value={email} autocomplete="email" required></label>
            <label>Password <input type="password" name="password" autocomplete="new-password" minlength="12" required></label>
            <button>Create the account</button>
        </form>
        <p><a href="/account/signin">I have an account</a></p>
    </section>
}

pub fn Signin(csrf: &str, email: &str, error: &str) -> Element {
    <section>
        <h1>Sign in</h1>
        <Problem text={error} />
        <form method="post" action="/account/signin">
            {! csrf}
            <label>Email <input type="email" name="email" value={email} autocomplete="username" required></label>
            <label>Password <input type="password" name="password" autocomplete="current-password" required></label>
            <button>Sign in</button>
        </form>
        <p><a href="/account/forgot">I forgot my password</a></p>
        <p><a href="/account/signup">Create an account</a></p>
    </section>
}

pub fn Signout(csrf: &str) -> Element {
    <section>
        <h1>Sign out</h1>
        <form method="post" action="/account/signout">
            {! csrf}
            <button>Sign out</button>
        </form>
    </section>
}

pub fn Forgot(csrf: &str) -> Element {
    <section>
        <h1>Reset your password</h1>
        <form method="post" action="/account/forgot">
            {! csrf}
            <label>Email <input type="email" name="email" autocomplete="email" required></label>
            <button>Send me a link</button>
        </form>
    </section>
}

pub fn Reset(csrf: &str, action: &str, error: &str) -> Element {
    <section>
        <h1>Choose a new password</h1>
        <Problem text={error} />
        <form method="post" action={action}>
            {! csrf}
            <label>New password <input type="password" name="password" autocomplete="new-password" minlength="12" required></label>
            <button>Change the password</button>
        </form>
    </section>
}

pub fn Verify(csrf: &str, action: &str) -> Element {
    <section>
        <h1>Confirm your address</h1>
        <form method="post" action={action}>
            {! csrf}
            <button>Confirm my address</button>
        </form>
    </section>
}

pub fn Code(csrf: &str, error: &str) -> Element {
    <section>
        <h1>Enter your code</h1>
        <Problem text={error} />
        <form method="post" action="/account/code">
            {! csrf}
            <label>Code from your app, or a recovery code <input name="code" autocomplete="one-time-code" required></label>
            <button>Continue</button>
        </form>
    </section>
}

pub fn Totp(csrf: &str, secret: &str, uri: &str, error: &str, codes: &[String]) -> Element {
    <section>
        <h1>Two-step sign-in</h1>
        if !codes.is_empty() {
            <p>Two-step sign-in is on. Keep these recovery codes somewhere safe. Each works once, and this page will not show them again.</p>
            <ul>
                for code in codes {
                    <li><code>{code}</code></li>
                }
            </ul>
        } else {
            <Problem text={error} />
            if !secret.is_empty() {
                <p>Add this account to your authenticator app: <a href={uri}>open it in the app</a>, or type the key <code>{secret}</code>.</p>
            }
            <form method="post" action="/account/totp">
                {! csrf}
                <label>The code the app shows <input name="code" inputmode="numeric" autocomplete="one-time-code" required></label>
                <button>Turn it on</button>
            </form>
            <p><a href="/account/totp">Start again with a new key</a></p>
        }
    </section>
}
===== src/pages/account/signin.rs =====
//! `/account/signin` -- a password, then a second factor if the account has
//! one.

use brocanth_auth::Attempt;

use crate::pages::{html, views, Ctx, Error, Response};
use crate::plugins::auth_screens::{self as screens, csrf_field};

#[derive(zod::Entry)]
pub struct Credentials {
    #[check(zod::Text::new().min(1).max(254))]
    email: String,
    #[check(zod::Text::new().min(1).max(1024))]
    password: String,
}

/// One sentence for a wrong password, an unknown address and an account
/// with no password: which one it was is what an attacker wants.
const WRONG: &str = "That email and password do not match an account.";

pub fn handle(ctx: &mut Ctx) -> Result<Response, Error> {
    again(ctx, "", "", 200)
}

pub async fn handle_post(ctx: &mut Ctx) -> Result<Response, Error> {
    let auth = screens::services(ctx)?;
    let Some(form) = screens::form::<Credentials>(ctx)? else {
        return again(ctx, "", "Enter your email and your password.", 422);
    };
    let lockout = crate::plugins::auth_lockout::lockout(&auth)?;
    let attempt = lockout
        .login(auth.accounts(), &form.email, &form.password)
        .await?;
    let proof = match attempt {
        Attempt::Admitted(signed) => signed.into_proof(),
        Attempt::Refused => return again(ctx, &form.email, WRONG, 422),
        Attempt::JustLocked { account, .. } => {
            screens::mail_locked(ctx, &account, lockout.policy().lock_for()).await?;
            return again(ctx, &form.email, WRONG, 422);
        }
    };
    screens::admit(ctx, &auth, proof).await
}

fn again(ctx: &mut Ctx, email: &str, error: &str, status: u16) -> Result<Response, Error> {
    let csrf = csrf_field(ctx);
    let mut page = html!(
        ctx,
        views::account::screens::Signin {
            csrf: &csrf,
            email,
            error
        }
    )?;
    page.status = status;
    Ok(page)
}
===== src/pages/account/signout.rs =====
//! `/account/signout` -- a button, because a sign-out is a POST: a link
//! that signs out is a link any page can make a browser follow.

use brocanth_middleware::Level;

use crate::pages::{html, views, Ctx, Error, IntoResponse, Response};
use crate::plugins::auth_screens::{self as screens, csrf_field, see_other};

pub fn handle(ctx: &mut Ctx) -> Result<impl IntoResponse, Error> {
    let csrf = csrf_field(ctx);
    html!(ctx, views::account::screens::Signout { csrf: &csrf })
}

pub async fn handle_post(ctx: &mut Ctx) -> Result<Response, Error> {
    let auth = screens::services(ctx)?;
    let cleared = match screens::signed_in(ctx, &auth).await {
        Some(session) => auth.sessions().revoke(&session.id).await?,
        None => auth.sessions().clearing_cookie(),
    };
    screens::flash(ctx, Level::Info, "You are signed out.");
    Ok(see_other("/").with_header("set-cookie", cleared.to_string()))
}
===== src/pages/account/signup.rs =====
//! `/account/signup` -- a new account, and a mail to confirm the address.

use brocanth_auth::accounts::RegistrationNotice;
use brocanth_auth::AuthError;
use brocanth_middleware::Level;

use crate::pages::{html, views, Ctx, Error, Response};
use crate::plugins::auth_screens::{self as screens, csrf_field, see_other};

#[derive(zod::Entry)]
pub struct NewAccount {
    #[check(zod::Text::new().min(3).max(254))]
    email: String,
    #[check(zod::Text::new().min(12).max(1024))]
    password: String,
}

pub fn handle(ctx: &mut Ctx) -> Result<Response, Error> {
    again(ctx, "", "", 200)
}

/// The same answer for a new address and a taken one: a stranger must not
/// learn who has an account. The mail says which it was, to its owner.
pub async fn handle_post(ctx: &mut Ctx) -> Result<Response, Error> {
    let auth = screens::services(ctx)?;
    let links = screens::screens(ctx)?;
    let Some(form) = screens::form::<NewAccount>(ctx)? else {
        return again(
            ctx,
            "",
            "Enter an email and a password of 12 characters or more.",
            422,
        );
    };
    let accepted = match auth.accounts().register(&form.email, &form.password).await {
        Ok(accepted) => accepted,
        Err(AuthError::InvalidEmail(_)) => {
            return again(ctx, &form.email, "This is not an email address.", 422)
        }
        Err(other) => return Err(other.into()),
    };
    let (subject, text) = match accepted.notice {
        RegistrationNotice::Created { verification, .. } => (
            "Confirm your address",
            format!(
                "Confirm your address with this link:\n\n{}\n",
                links.link(&format!("/account/verify/{}", verification.secret()))
            ),
        ),
        RegistrationNotice::AlreadyRegistered { .. } => (
            "Somebody tried to create an account",
            format!(
                "Somebody tried to create an account with this address, which has one.\n\
                 If it was you, reset your password:\n\n{}\n",
                links.link("/account/forgot")
            ),
        ),
    };
    screens::mail(ctx, &form.email, subject, text).await?;
    screens::flash(
        ctx,
        Level::Info,
        "Check your inbox to confirm your address, then sign in.",
    );
    Ok(see_other("/account/signin"))
}

fn again(ctx: &mut Ctx, email: &str, error: &str, status: u16) -> Result<Response, Error> {
    let csrf = csrf_field(ctx);
    let mut page = html!(
        ctx,
        views::account::screens::Signup {
            csrf: &csrf,
            email,
            error
        }
    )?;
    page.status = status;
    Ok(page)
}
===== src/pages/account/totp.rs =====
//! `/account/totp` -- turn on two-step sign-in with an authenticator app.
//!
//! The GET draws a new key and stores it unconfirmed; the POST confirms it
//! with a code from the app and shows the recovery codes, once.

use brocanth_auth::{AccountHolder, AuthError};
use brocanth_middleware::Level;

use crate::pages::{html, views, Ctx, Error, Response};
use crate::plugins::auth_screens::{self as screens, csrf_field, see_other};

#[derive(zod::Entry)]
pub struct Confirm {
    #[check(zod::Text::new().min(6).max(16))]
    code: String,
}

pub async fn handle(ctx: &mut Ctx) -> Result<Response, Error> {
    let auth = screens::services(ctx)?;
    let links = screens::screens(ctx)?;
    let Some(session) = screens::signed_in(ctx, &auth).await else {
        screens::flash(ctx, Level::Info, "Sign in first.");
        return Ok(see_other("/account/signin"));
    };
    let holder = AccountHolder::of(&session)?;
    let label = screens::email_of(&auth, &session).await?;
    let enrolment = auth
        .mfa()?
        .begin_totp_enrolment(&holder, links.issuer(), &label)
        .await?;
    let secret = enrolment.secret.to_base32();
    page(ctx, &secret, &enrolment.uri, "", &[], 200)
}

pub async fn handle_post(ctx: &mut Ctx) -> Result<Response, Error> {
    let auth = screens::services(ctx)?;
    let Some(session) = screens::signed_in(ctx, &auth).await else {
        screens::flash(ctx, Level::Info, "Sign in first.");
        return Ok(see_other("/account/signin"));
    };
    let holder = AccountHolder::of(&session)?;
    let Some(form) = screens::form::<Confirm>(ctx)? else {
        return page(
            ctx,
            "",
            "",
            "Enter the six digits your app shows.",
            &[],
            422,
        );
    };
    match auth
        .mfa()?
        .confirm_totp_enrolment(&holder, form.code.trim())
        .await
    {
        Ok(recovery) => {
            let codes: Vec<String> = recovery
                .codes()
                .iter()
                .map(|code| code.as_str().to_string())
                .collect();
            page(ctx, "", "", "", &codes, 200)
        }
        Err(AuthError::InvalidCredentials) => page(
            ctx,
            "",
            "",
            "That code does not match. Check the clock of your device, or start again.",
            &[],
            422,
        ),
        Err(other) => Err(other.into()),
    }
}

fn page(
    ctx: &mut Ctx,
    secret: &str,
    uri: &str,
    error: &str,
    codes: &[String],
    status: u16,
) -> Result<Response, Error> {
    let csrf = csrf_field(ctx);
    let mut page = html!(
        ctx,
        views::account::screens::Totp {
            csrf: &csrf,
            secret,
            uri,
            error,
            codes,
        }
    )?;
    page.status = status;
    Ok(page)
}
===== src/pages/account/verify/[token].rs =====
//! `/account/verify/{token}` -- the address is confirmed by a click, not
//! by the GET: a mail scanner follows every link it sees.

use brocanth_auth::AuthError;
use brocanth_middleware::Level;

use crate::pages::{html, views, Ctx, Error, IntoResponse, Response};
use crate::plugins::auth_screens::{self as screens, csrf_field, see_other};

pub fn handle(ctx: &mut Ctx) -> Result<impl IntoResponse, Error> {
    let csrf = csrf_field(ctx);
    let action = ctx.path.clone();
    html!(
        ctx,
        views::account::screens::Verify {
            csrf: &csrf,
            action: &action,
        }
    )
}

pub async fn handle_post(ctx: &mut Ctx) -> Result<Response, Error> {
    let auth = screens::services(ctx)?;
    let token = ctx.require("token")?.to_string();
    match auth.accounts().verify_email(&token).await {
        Ok(_) => screens::flash(ctx, Level::Success, "Your address is confirmed."),
        Err(AuthError::InvalidToken) => screens::flash(
            ctx,
            Level::Error,
            "This link is spent or too old. Sign in to ask for a new one.",
        ),
        Err(other) => return Err(other.into()),
    }
    Ok(see_other("/account/signin"))
}
===== src/pages/error.rsx =====
// The page for every failure but a 404: a page that returned an error, a
// 500 included. The layouts above this file wrap it. An `error.rsx` in a
// directory below answers for that directory.
//
// A 5xx has no message here: the cause is in the log, and a debug build
// shows it on a page of its own. A 4xx has the message the page gave.

use crate::pages::Caught;

pub fn ErrorPage(caught: &Caught) -> Element {
    <h1>{caught.status} {caught.reason}</h1>
    if let Some(message) = &caught.message {
        <p>{message}</p>
    } else {
        <p>Something went wrong on our side. Try again in a moment.</p>
    }
    <p><a href="/">Back to the home page</a></p>
}
===== src/pages/index.rsx =====
use crate::pages::views::link::Link;

// The page, and only the page. The document around it is layout.rsx, and
// this file does not know it exists.

pub fn Index(title: &str) -> Element {
    <h1>{title}</h1>
    <p>
        This file is <code>src/pages/index.rsx</code>, beside the handler
        in <code>index.rs</code>, and it is compiled into the binary. Edit it
        and cargo rebuilds; break it and the build stops on the line you broke.
    </p>
    <p>
        An interpolated value is escaped on the way out, always. Writing
        markup verbatim needs a different and greppable hole, so an injection
        is something you can search for rather than something you have to
        remember.
    </p>
    <ul>
        <li><Link to="/about">/about</Link></li>
        <li><Link to={&crate::pages::routes::users::id::path(7)}>User 7</Link></li>
    </ul>
}
===== src/pages/layout.rsx =====
// The document every page in this directory, and every directory below it,
// is rendered inside. A page never names it: which layout wraps a page is a
// property of where the page is, which is the rule the Crystal page_router
// uses for layout.cr.
//
// `meta` is the page's head, which `html!` fills with `ctx.head()`: the
// title, the description and the share tags the page set with
// `ctx.meta()`, and the `[meta]` settings for what it left out. `{! meta }`
// writes the tags unescaped because they are markup; every value inside
// them is escaped already. Only the layout that writes `<head>` asks for
// `meta`.

use brocanth_meta::Head;

use crate::pages::TITLE;

pub fn Layout(layout: &str, meta: &Head, children: Element) -> Element {
    <!DOCTYPE html>
    <html lang={meta.lang_tag()}>
        <head>
            <meta charset="utf-8">
            <meta name="viewport" content="width=device-width, initial-scale=1">
            <title>{meta.title()}</title>
            {! meta }
            <link rel="stylesheet" href={crate::assets::url("css/site.css")}>
            <script src={crate::assets::url("dpswap.min.js")} defer></script>
        </head>
        <body class="min-h-screen bg-white text-slate-900">
            <header><a href="/">{TITLE}</a></header>
            <div id="flash" role="status"></div>
            <main dp-layout={layout} id="outlet">{children}</main>
        </body>
    </html>
}
===== src/pages/link.rsx =====
// An anchor that swaps instead of reloading the document.
//
// Written as a real <a href> and enhanced afterwards. With JavaScript off,
// to a crawler, and on the first load of any page it is a link and the
// browser does what it always did -- which is why `to` is required and why
// there is no version of this that renders a <span> with a click handler.
//
// `to` takes what the route helpers return:
//
//     <Link to={routes::users::id::path(7)}>User 7</Link>
//
// never a string. A route that does not exist is then a compile error
// rather than a 404 somebody finds later.

pub fn Link(to: &str, children: Element) -> Element {
    <a href={to} dp-boost>{children}</a>
}
===== src/pages/not-found.rsx =====
// The page for a 404: a path nothing answers, or a page that returned
// `Error::new(404, ..)`. The layouts above this file wrap it, as they wrap a
// page. A `not-found.rsx` in a directory below answers for that directory.
//
// `caught` is what `pages!` gives an error page: the status, the reason
// phrase, the message of a 4xx, and the path.

use crate::pages::Caught;

pub fn NotFound(caught: &Caught) -> Element {
    <h1>Page not found</h1>
    <p>Nothing answers at <code>{caught.path}</code>.</p>
    if let Some(message) = &caught.message {
        <p>{message}</p>
    }
    <p><a href="/">Back to the home page</a></p>
}
===== src/pages/openapi.json.rs =====
//! `/openapi.json` -- because the file is called `openapi.json.rs`.
//!
//! The route is the file name, as it is for every page: the last dot is the
//! extension and what is left is the path, so this file answers
//! `/openapi.json` and renaming it moves the document.
//!
//! The body was rendered once, at startup, by `src/plugins/openapi/mod.rs`.
//! This page hands it out and does nothing else.

use crate::pages::{Ctx, Error, IntoResponse};

/// GET `/openapi.json`.
///
/// `no-cache` rather than a long max-age: the document changes when this
/// application is redeployed, and a client holding a stale one is the exact
/// failure a derived document exists to prevent.
pub fn handle(_ctx: &mut Ctx) -> Result<impl IntoResponse, Error> {
    let body = crate::plugins::openapi::json().ok_or_else(|| {
        Error::internal("the openapi plugin is not registered, so there is no document to serve")
    })?;
    Ok((
        body.to_string(),
        200,
        vec![
            ("content-type".to_string(), "application/json".to_string()),
            ("cache-control".to_string(), "no-cache".to_string()),
        ],
    ))
}

    })?;

        body.to_string(),
        200,

            ("content-type".to_string(), "application/yaml".to_string()),
            ("cache-control".to_string(), "no-cache".to_string()),
        ],
    ))
}
===== src/pages/parts/pager.jt =====
// The links of a page, as JSON. Not a page: the route table reads `.rs`
// files only, so this file adds no route.
//
//     "items": [ for p in &posts { Post(p) } ],
//     "pages": PageLinks(&pager),

/// An offset page: where it is, and the links on either side.
pub fn PageLinks(pager: &brocanth_pagination::Pager) -> Json {
    "page": pager.page.number(),
    "per_page": pager.page.per_page(),
    "total": pager.page.total(),
    "total_pages": pager.page.total_pages(),
    "previous"?: pager.previous.as_deref(),
    "next"?: pager.next.as_deref(),
}

/// A keyset page: the first page, the previous one and the next one.
pub fn KeysetLinks(pager: &brocanth_pagination::KeysetPager) -> Json {
    "first"?: pager.first.as_deref(),
    "previous"?: pager.previous.as_deref(),
    "next"?: pager.next.as_deref(),
}
===== src/pages/parts/pager.rsx =====
// Two pagers, for any list. Not a page: the route table reads `.rs` files
// only, so this file adds no route.
//
//     let pager = brocanth_pagination::Pager::new(page, &ctx.links());
//     <PageNav pager={&pager} />
//
// A link is an ordinary `<a href>`, so the pager works with no script.

/// An offset pager: the summary, previous, the numbered pages, next. Nothing
/// when there is one page.
pub fn PageNav(pager: &brocanth_pagination::Pager) -> Element {
    if pager.is_needed() {
        <nav aria-label="Pages">
            <span>{pager.summary()}</span>
            if let Some(href) = &pager.previous {
                <a href={href} rel="prev">Previous</a>
            }
            for link in &pager.pages {
                if link.current {
                    <span aria-current="page">{link.number}</span>
                } else {
                    <a href={&link.href}>{link.number}</a>
                }
            }
            if let Some(href) = &pager.next {
                <a href={href} rel="next">Next</a>
            }
        </nav>
    }
}

/// A keyset pager: the first page, the previous one and the next one. A
/// keyset page has no numbers.
pub fn KeysetNav(pager: &brocanth_pagination::KeysetPager) -> Element {
    <nav aria-label="Pages">
        if let Some(href) = &pager.first {
            <a href={href} rel="first">First</a>
        }
        if let Some(href) = &pager.previous {
            <a href={href} rel="prev">Previous</a>
        }
        if let Some(href) = &pager.next {
            <a href={href} rel="next">Next</a>
        }
    </nav>
}
===== src/pages/realtime/events.rs =====
//! `GET /realtime/events?topics=posts/*,posts/42` -- the broker's frames, as
//! server-sent events.
//!
//! ```js
//! const events = new EventSource("/realtime/events?topics=posts/*");
//! events.addEventListener("posts/*", (e) => console.log(e.data));
//! events.addEventListener("lagged", () => location.reload());
//! ```
//!
//! The topics are in the query because an `EventSource` sends no body and
//! no headers of its own; the cookie goes with it, so a session reads as on
//! any other page. Every topic passes `authorise` before it is filed, and
//! one refusal refuses the request: a stream that quietly follows fewer
//! topics than it asked for is a bug nobody sees.
//!
//! The transport is `crate::plugins::realtime_sse`.

use brocanth_realtime::{Gate, Subscription, Topic};

use crate::pages::{Ctx, Error, IntoResponse, Response};
use crate::plugins::realtime::Broadcast;
use crate::plugins::realtime_sse::Frames;

/// Collections any visitor may follow: every row, every change.
///
/// Empty, so nothing is public until somebody says so here.
const PUBLIC: &[&str] = &[];

/// Whether this request may follow `topic`, and gated by what.
///
/// The one place a topic is authorised, and the place to edit. A public
/// collection takes `Gate::everyone()`. Rows that a policy decides take the
/// policy: `from_policy` gates `posts/*` with the list rule and `posts/42`
/// with the view rule, and the broker inverts the rule into an index key, so
/// a write is a map lookup and not a check per subscriber.
///
/// ```ignore
/// let subject = /* who is asking: the session's user id */;
/// return Ok(Subscription::from_policy(topic, &PostPolicy, &subject));
/// ```
fn authorise(_ctx: &Ctx, topic: Topic) -> Result<Subscription, Error> {
    if PUBLIC.contains(&topic.collection_name()) {
        return Ok(Subscription::new(topic, Gate::everyone()));
    }
    Err(Error::new(
        403,
        format!("no rule lets this request follow {topic}; add one to `authorise` in src/pages/realtime/events.rs"),
    ))
}

pub fn handle(ctx: &mut Ctx) -> Result<impl IntoResponse, Error> {
    let mut subscriptions = Vec::new();
    for topic in topics(&ctx.query)? {
        subscriptions.push(authorise(ctx, topic)?);
    }
    let broker = &ctx
        .provided::<Broadcast, _>()
        .ok_or_else(|| Error::internal("the realtime plugin put no broker on the context"))?
        .broker;

    // A browser that reconnects sends the id of the last frame it read. The
    // broker keeps no history to replay, so the honest answer to "did I miss
    // anything" is yes whenever a frame was published since.
    let missed = ctx
        .header("last-event-id")
        .is_some_and(|seen| seen.trim().parse::<u64>().ok() != Some(broker.last_frame_id()));

    // At the cap the broker refuses rather than evicting somebody. The
    // browser retries on its own; `retry-after` says when.
    let client = broker
        .connect()
        .map_err(|e| Error::new(503, e.to_string()).with_header("retry-after", "5"))?;
    for subscription in subscriptions {
        client
            .subscribe(subscription)
            .map_err(|e| Error::new(400, e.to_string()))?;
    }
    Ok(Response::events(Frames::new(client, missed)))
}

/// Every `topics=` in the query, split at commas and parsed.
fn topics(query: &str) -> Result<Vec<Topic>, Error> {
    let mut out = Vec::new();
    for pair in query.split('&') {
        let Some(("topics", value)) = pair.split_once('=') else {
            continue;
        };
        for text in decoded(value)?.split(',').filter(|t| !t.is_empty()) {
            let topic =
                Topic::parse(text).map_err(|e| Error::new(400, format!("topic {text:?}: {e}")))?;
            out.push(topic);
        }
    }
    if out.is_empty() {
        return Err(Error::new(
            400,
            "name what to follow: ?topics=posts/* for a collection, posts/42 for a row",
        ));
    }
    Ok(out)
}

/// A query value with its `%XX` and `+` undone.
fn decoded(value: &str) -> Result<String, Error> {
    let bad = || {
        Error::new(
            400,
            format!("the query value {value:?} is not percent-encoded UTF-8"),
        )
    };
    let mut bytes = Vec::with_capacity(value.len());
    let mut rest = value.bytes();
    while let Some(byte) = rest.next() {
        bytes.push(match byte {
            b'+' => b' ',
            b'%' => {
                let hex = [rest.next().ok_or_else(bad)?, rest.next().ok_or_else(bad)?];
                let hex = std::str::from_utf8(&hex).map_err(|_| bad())?;
                u8::from_str_radix(hex, 16).map_err(|_| bad())?
            }
            other => other,
        });
    }
    String::from_utf8(bytes).map_err(|_| bad())
}

#[cfg(test)]
mod tests {
    use brocanth::routing::Verb;
    use brocanth_server::Request;

    use super::*;

    async fn status(target: &str) -> u16 {
        let app = crate::app::build().expect("the application builds");
        let (path, query) = target.split_once('?').unwrap_or((target, ""));
        // As a browser's `EventSource` asks.
        let mut request = Request::new(Verb::Get, path).with_header("accept", "text/event-stream");
        request.query = query.to_string();
        crate::app::handle(&app, request).await.status
    }

    #[test]
    fn topics_are_read_from_the_query_encoded_or_not() {
        let read = topics("topics=posts/*,posts%2F42&x=1&topics=users%2F*").expect("three");
        let names: Vec<String> = read.iter().map(ToString::to_string).collect();
        assert_eq!(names, ["posts/*", "posts/42", "users/*"]);
        assert!(
            topics("").is_err(),
            "no topic is a 400, not a silent stream"
        );
        assert!(topics("topics=posts").is_err(), "a topic has a slash");
    }

    #[tokio::test]
    async fn a_request_without_topics_is_a_bad_request() {
        assert_eq!(status("/realtime/events").await, 400);
    }

    #[tokio::test]
    async fn a_topic_no_rule_allows_is_refused() {
        assert_eq!(status("/realtime/events?topics=posts/*").await, 403);
    }
}
===== src/pages/realtime/socket.rs =====
//! `GET /realtime/socket?topics=posts/*,posts/42` -- the broker's frames
//! over a WebSocket.
//!
//! ```js
//! const socket = new WebSocket(`ws://${location.host}/realtime/socket?topics=posts/*`);
//! socket.onmessage = (e) => console.log(e.data); // "id:1\nevent:posts/*\ndata:..."
//! socket.onopen = () => socket.send("subscribe posts/42");
//! ```
//!
//! Three checks run before the connection switches, and each refusal is a
//! status the browser can read:
//!
//! 1. The handshake: a WebSocket, version 13. Else 426.
//! 2. The `Origin`. A browser sends the site's cookies with a WebSocket from
//!    any page, and no CORS rule applies, so the Origin check is this
//!    route's CSRF check: the page must be on this host, or in
//!    `ALLOWED_ORIGINS`. Else 403.
//! 3. Every topic in the URL passes `authorise`, and one refusal refuses
//!    the socket. Else 403.
//!
//! A topic the browser asks for later (`subscribe <topic>`) passes the same
//! `authorise`. The loop is `crate::plugins::realtime_websocket`.

use brocanth_realtime::{Gate, Subscription, Topic};
use brocanth_server::websocket::Handshake;

use crate::pages::{Ctx, Error, IntoResponse, Response};
use crate::plugins::realtime::Broadcast;
use crate::plugins::realtime_websocket::{self, Filed};

/// Collections any visitor may follow: every row, every change.
///
/// Empty, so nothing is public until somebody says so here.
const PUBLIC: &[&str] = &[];

/// Sites other than this host whose pages may open a socket, spelled as a
/// browser sends its `Origin`: `"https://app.example.com"`.
const ALLOWED_ORIGINS: &[&str] = &[];

/// Who is asking: what `authorise` needs, read from the request once.
///
/// The socket outlives the request, so `authorise` cannot read the request
/// when the browser subscribes later. Put here what it decides on: the
/// session's user id, a role.
pub struct Viewer;

fn viewer(_ctx: &Ctx) -> Viewer {
    Viewer
}

/// Whether `viewer` may follow `topic`, and gated by what.
///
/// The one place a topic is authorised, and the place to edit. A public
/// collection takes `Gate::everyone()`. Rows that a policy decides take the
/// policy: `from_policy` gates `posts/*` with the list rule and `posts/42`
/// with the view rule, and the broker inverts the rule into an index key, so
/// a write is a map lookup and not a check per subscriber.
///
/// ```ignore
/// return Ok(Subscription::from_policy(topic, &PostPolicy, &viewer.user_id));
/// ```
fn authorise(_viewer: &Viewer, topic: Topic) -> Result<Subscription, String> {
    if PUBLIC.contains(&topic.collection_name()) {
        return Ok(Subscription::new(topic, Gate::everyone()));
    }
    Err(format!(
        "no rule lets this socket follow {topic}\n\
         help: add one to `authorise` in src/pages/realtime/socket.rs"
    ))
}

pub fn handle(ctx: &mut Ctx) -> Result<impl IntoResponse, Error> {
    let handshake = Handshake::read(|name| ctx.header(name), ALLOWED_ORIGINS)
        .map_err(|refused| Error::new(refused.status(), refused.to_string()))?;
    let viewer = viewer(ctx);
    let mut subscriptions = Vec::new();
    for topic in topics(&ctx.query)? {
        let name = topic.to_string();
        let subscription = authorise(&viewer, topic).map_err(|why| Error::new(403, why))?;
        subscriptions.push((name, subscription));
    }
    let broker = &ctx
        .provided::<Broadcast, _>()
        .ok_or_else(|| Error::internal("the realtime plugin put no broker on the context"))?
        .broker;

    // A browser that reconnects sends the id of the last frame it read. The
    // broker keeps no history to replay, so the honest answer to "did I miss
    // anything" is yes whenever a frame was published since.
    let missed = value(&ctx.query, "last")
        .is_some_and(|seen| seen.trim().parse::<u64>().ok() != Some(broker.last_frame_id()));

    // At the cap the broker refuses rather than evicting somebody.
    let client = broker
        .connect()
        .map_err(|e| Error::new(503, e.to_string()).with_header("retry-after", "5"))?;
    let mut filed = Filed::new();
    for (name, subscription) in subscriptions {
        let subscribed = client
            .subscribe(subscription)
            .map_err(|e| Error::new(400, e.to_string()))?;
        filed.insert(name, subscribed.id);
    }

    let headers = handshake.headers().into_iter().collect();
    let rule: realtime_websocket::Authorise = Box::new(move |topic| authorise(&viewer, topic));
    let stream = handshake
        .switch(move |socket| realtime_websocket::run(socket, client, rule, filed, missed));
    Ok(Response {
        status: 101,
        headers,
        stream: Some(stream),
        ..Default::default()
    })
}

/// Every `topics=` in the query, split at commas and parsed. None is
/// allowed: the browser can subscribe after the socket opens.
fn topics(query: &str) -> Result<Vec<Topic>, Error> {
    let mut out = Vec::new();
    for pair in query.split('&') {
        let Some(("topics", value)) = pair.split_once('=') else {
            continue;
        };
        for text in decoded(value)?.split(',').filter(|t| !t.is_empty()) {
            let topic =
                Topic::parse(text).map_err(|e| Error::new(400, format!("topic {text:?}: {e}")))?;
            out.push(topic);
        }
    }
    Ok(out)
}

/// The first value of `name` in the query, as sent.
fn value<'q>(query: &'q str, name: &str) -> Option<&'q str> {
    query
        .split('&')
        .filter_map(|pair| pair.split_once('='))
        .find(|(key, _)| *key == name)
        .map(|(_, value)| value)
}

/// A query value with its `%XX` and `+` undone.
fn decoded(value: &str) -> Result<String, Error> {
    let bad = || {
        Error::new(
            400,
            format!("the query value {value:?} is not percent-encoded UTF-8"),
        )
    };
    let mut bytes = Vec::with_capacity(value.len());
    let mut rest = value.bytes();
    while let Some(byte) = rest.next() {
        bytes.push(match byte {
            b'+' => b' ',
            b'%' => {
                let hex = [rest.next().ok_or_else(bad)?, rest.next().ok_or_else(bad)?];
                let hex = std::str::from_utf8(&hex).map_err(|_| bad())?;
                u8::from_str_radix(hex, 16).map_err(|_| bad())?
            }
            other => other,
        });
    }
    String::from_utf8(bytes).map_err(|_| bad())
}

#[cfg(test)]
mod tests {
    use std::net::SocketAddr;
    use std::sync::Arc;
    use std::time::Duration;

    use brocanth_server::websocket::{self, Incoming};
    use brocanth_server::{Server, Stopper};

    use super::*;

    /// The whole application on a real port.
    async fn serve() -> (SocketAddr, Stopper) {
        let app = Arc::new(crate::app::build().expect("the application builds"));
        let server = Server::bind("127.0.0.1:0")
            .await
            .expect("a port")
            .with_streams(app.core.streams().clone());
        let addr = server.local_addr().expect("an address");
        let stopper = server.stopper();
        tokio::spawn(server.run(move |request| {
            let app = Arc::clone(&app);
            async move { crate::app::handle(&app, request).await }
        }));
        (addr, stopper)
    }

    #[test]
    fn topics_are_read_from_the_query_encoded_or_not() {
        let read = topics("topics=posts/*,posts%2F42&x=1").expect("two");
        let names: Vec<String> = read.iter().map(ToString::to_string).collect();
        assert_eq!(names, ["posts/*", "posts/42"]);
        assert!(topics("").expect("none is allowed").is_empty());
        assert!(topics("topics=posts").is_err(), "a topic has a slash");
        assert_eq!(value("a=1&last=7", "last"), Some("7"));
    }

    #[tokio::test]
    async fn a_socket_opens_and_a_topic_no_rule_allows_is_refused_over_it() {
        let (addr, stopper) = serve().await;
        let origin = format!("http://{addr}");
        let mut socket = websocket::connect(addr, "/realtime/socket", Some(&origin))
            .await
            .expect("this host may open a socket");
        socket.send_text("subscribe posts/*").await.expect("sent");
        let answer = tokio::time::timeout(Duration::from_secs(5), socket.recv())
            .await
            .expect("an answer in time");
        let Incoming::Text(answer) = answer else {
            panic!("a text answer, not {answer:?}");
        };
        assert!(
            answer.starts_with("event:refused\ndata:posts/*: "),
            "{answer}"
        );
        socket.close(websocket::NORMAL, "done").await;
        stopper.stop();
    }

    #[tokio::test]
    async fn a_page_on_another_site_is_refused_before_the_switch() {
        let (addr, stopper) = serve().await;
        let refused = websocket::connect(addr, "/realtime/socket", Some("https://evil.example"))
            .await
            .expect_err("a cross-site socket");
        assert_eq!(refused.status, Some(403));
        stopper.stop();
    }

    #[tokio::test]
    async fn a_topic_in_the_url_no_rule_allows_is_refused_before_the_switch() {
        let (addr, stopper) = serve().await;
        let refused = websocket::connect(addr, "/realtime/socket?topics=posts/*", None)
            .await
            .expect_err("no rule allows posts");
        assert_eq!(refused.status, Some(403));
        stopper.stop();
    }
}
===== src/pages/robots.txt.rs =====
//! `/robots.txt` -- because the file is called `robots.txt.rs`.
//!
//! The text was rendered once, at startup, by `src/plugins/robots/mod.rs`.
//! This page hands it out and does nothing else.

use crate::pages::{Ctx, Error, IntoResponse};

/// GET `/robots.txt`.
///
/// An hour of cache: crawlers keep the file for a day anyway, and an hour
/// is how long a changed rule takes to reach one that asked just before.
pub fn handle(_ctx: &mut Ctx) -> Result<impl IntoResponse, Error> {
    let body = crate::plugins::robots::text().ok_or_else(|| {
        Error::internal("the robots plugin is not registered, so there is no robots.txt to serve")
    })?;
    Ok((
        body.to_string(),
        200,
        vec![
            (
                "content-type".to_string(),
                "text/plain; charset=utf-8".to_string(),
            ),
            ("cache-control".to_string(), "max-age=3600".to_string()),
        ],
    ))
}

#[cfg(test)]
mod tests {
    use brocanth::routing::Verb;
    use brocanth_server::Request;

    /// What this project answered the day it was generated. Change the
    /// `[robots]` settings and this line with them.
    const GENERATED: &str = "User-agent: *\nDisallow: /\n";

    #[tokio::test]
    async fn the_application_answers_robots_txt_as_text() {
        let app = crate::app::build().expect("the application builds");
        let response = crate::app::handle(&app, Request::new(Verb::Get, "/robots.txt")).await;
        assert_eq!(response.status, 200);

        );

    }
