===== src/pages/search.rs =====
//! `/search` -- the site search, over `search_documents`.
//!
//! `?q=words&page=2`. The term is read by Postgres' `websearch_to_tsquery`:
//! words, `"a phrase"`, `-not`, `or`. A typo in a title still matches, by
//! trigram. The markup is in `search.rsx` beside this file.

use brocanth_search::documents::{self, Document, DocumentColumn, Hit};
use brocanth_search::pagination::{query, Pager};
use brocanth_search::policy::{Dialect, Pred};

use crate::pages::{html, views, Ctx, Error, IntoResponse};

/// Results per page: `per_page` in `src/modules/search.rs`.
const PER_PAGE: u64 = crate::modules::search::MODULE.per_page;

/// GET. An empty `q` shows the form alone.
pub async fn handle(ctx: &mut Ctx) -> Result<impl IntoResponse, Error> {
    let term = query::param(&ctx.query, "q").unwrap_or_default();
    let params = ctx.page_params()?;
    // The rule. Every row of the site index is public: index only what any
    // visitor may read. To hide rows, write the rule here. It goes into the
    // `WHERE` of the page and of the count, so a hidden row is never ranked.
    let rule = Pred::<DocumentColumn>::Always;
    let condition = rule.condition::<Document>(Dialect::Postgres)?;
    let hits = documents::index()
        .search::<Hit>(ctx.db(), &condition, &term, params.page, PER_PAGE)
        .await?;
    let pager = Pager::new(hits.page, &ctx.links());
    html!(
        ctx,
        views::search::Search {
            term: &term,
            hits: &hits.items,
            pager: &pager,
        }
    )
}
===== src/pages/search.rsx =====
// The site search. Every value is escaped on the way out: the term came
// from the request and the rows from whoever wrote the index.

use brocanth_search::documents::Hit;
use brocanth_search::pagination::Pager;

use crate::pages::views::parts::pager::PageNav;

pub fn Search(term: &str, hits: &[Hit], pager: &Pager) -> Element {
    <h1>Search</h1>
    <form method="get" action="/search" role="search">
        <input type="search" name="q" value={term} aria-label="Search" />
        <button type="submit">Search</button>
    </form>
    if !term.trim().is_empty() {
        if hits.is_empty() {
            <p>Nothing matches “{term}”.</p>
        } else {
            <p>{pager.summary()}</p>
            <ol>
                for hit in hits {
                    <li>
                        <a href={&hit.url}>{&hit.title}</a>
                        <p>{hit.excerpt(200)}</p>
                    </li>
                }
            </ol>
            <PageNav pager={pager} />
        }
    }
}

#[cfg(test)]
mod tests {
    use brocanth::routing::Verb;
    use brocanth_server::Request;

    #[tokio::test]
    async fn the_application_answers_sitemap_xml_with_its_pages() {
        let app = crate::app::build().expect("the application builds");
        let response = crate::app::handle(&app, Request::new(Verb::Get, "/sitemap.xml")).await;
        assert_eq!(response.status, 200);
        assert_eq!(
            response.header("content-type"),
            Some("application/xml; charset=utf-8")
        );
        let body = response.body.bytes().expect("a whole body");
        let xml = std::str::from_utf8(body).expect("utf-8");
        assert!(xml.starts_with("<?xml"), "{xml}");
        // The index page, at the base URL of the development file.
        assert!(xml.contains("/</loc>"), "{xml}");
        // No route with a parameter, and not this file itself.
        assert!(!xml.contains('{'), "{xml}");
        assert!(!xml.contains("sitemap.xml"), "{xml}");
        // The admin, wherever src/modules/admin.rs mounts it.
        let admin = format!("{}</loc>", crate::modules::admin::MODULE.mount_at);
        assert!(!xml.contains(&admin), "{xml}");
    }
}
===== src/pages/status.jt =====
// The body of `/status`. A template is a function whose body is JSON with
// holes, and the macro compiles it into straight-line writes: every static
// run below is one memcpy, and the commas are resolved while this file is
// compiled rather than counted when a request arrives.
//
// Take parameters by reference. A `&str` is borrowed into the template and
// nothing is copied to render it.

pub fn Status(name: &str, version: &str, parts: &[&str]) -> Json {
    "name": name,
    "version": version,
    "ok": true,
    "parts": [ for p in parts { p } ],
}
===== src/pages/status.rs =====
//! `/status` -- because the file is called `status.rs`.
//!
//! The answer is JSON, and its shape is in `status.jt` beside this file.
//! Nothing here builds a struct for the response: the template is the
//! shape, and it is compiled into the binary rather than parsed per
//! request.

use crate::pages::{payload, render_json, Ctx, Error, IntoResponse};

/// GET. The verb comes off the function name, the same as every other page.
pub fn handle(_ctx: &mut Ctx) -> Result<impl IntoResponse, Error> {
    render_json(payload::status::Status(
        env!("CARGO_PKG_NAME"),
        env!("CARGO_PKG_VERSION"),
        &["pages", "router"],
    ))
}

#[cfg(test)]
mod tests {
    use super::*;

    #[test]
    fn the_template_is_the_shape_of_the_answer() {
        // Rendered without a request, because a template takes values and
        // not a context. Edit `status.jt` and this is what changes.
        let body = jt::to_string(&payload::status::Status("api", "0.1.0", &["pages"]));
        assert_eq!(
            body,
            r#"{"name":"api","version":"0.1.0","ok":true,"parts":["pages"]}"#
        );
    }

    #[test]
    fn the_answer_says_it_is_json() {
        let answered = render_json(payload::status::Status("api", "0.1.0", &[])).expect("renders");
        assert!(answered
            .headers
            .iter()
            .any(|(name, value)| name == "content-type" && value == "application/json"));
    }
}

// Two layouts wrap this one: the root, and users/layout.rsx beside it.
// Neither is named here or anywhere else -- `html!` reads the directory
// tree. Delete users/layout.rsx and this page is wrapped in one.

pub fn User(id: u64) -> Element {
    <h1>User {id}</h1>
    <p>
        The <code>id</code> came out of the path. A component takes typed
        parameters, so this one is a <code>u64</code> the whole way
        from <code>src/pages/users/[id].rs</code>, never a string that
        might not be a number.
    </p>
}
===== src/pages/users/layout.rsx =====
// Wraps every page under src/pages/users/, inside the root layout rather
// than instead of it. Nothing declares the stacking: `html!` reads it off
// the directory tree.
//
// `layout` is the URL prefix this layout covers, filled in by `html!`, and
// `dp-layout` is the marker dp-swap walks to find the deepest layer two
// pages share. It goes on the element wrapping exactly the children and
// nothing else: anything inside it is replaced along with them, so a
// sidebar in here would lose its scroll position on every navigation.

pub fn Layout(layout: &str, children: Element) -> Element {
    <section class="users">
        <nav><a href="/">home</a></nav>
        <div dp-layout={layout}>{children}</div>
    </section>
}
===== src/plugins/admin/mod.rs =====
//! The backoffice, at `/admin`.
//!
//! Written once by `brocanth create`. This file is yours.
//!
//! It ships locked, and two answers are yours to give:
//!
//! 1. **Who may open a screen.** The policy is `DenyAll` until
//!    `AdminConfig::with_policy` names another, so every screen answers 403.
//! 2. **Who is asking.** [`principal`] returns an anonymous principal until
//!    it reads your user off the request.
//!
//! It reads rows through the ORM the `model` plugin publishes. A model is in
//! the admin when `models.rs` lists it, and it needs a row policy:
//! `AdminConfig::resource(..)`, one per model. A model with no resource
//! answers 403 on every screen. `brocanth_admin::rows` has the worked
//! example.
//!
//! The routes are in the one table `src/pages/mod.rs` builds:
//! `pages::mounted` calls [`mount`] with the prefix. A page at `/admin` is
//! therefore a build error, not a race.

mod models;

use std::sync::{Arc, Mutex, OnceLock, PoisonError};

use brocanth::plugin_api::Plugin;
use brocanth::plugin_context::NoContext;
use brocanth::routing::{BuildError, RouterBuilder, Verb};
use brocanth_admin::policy::Principal;
use brocanth_admin::{Admin, AdminConfig, Fixture, Submission};

use crate::pages::{Ctx, Handler, Response};

/// The admin's titles, its "View site" link and its theme: `[admin]` in
/// the config file, `$<APP>_ADMIN__<KEY>`, or `--admin.<key>`.
///
/// It derives `Deserialize` because this application reads it from
/// `[admin]` in a TOML file. Drop the config plugin and the derive goes
/// with it: `Plugin::Config` carries no bounds the core imposes.
#[derive(Debug, serde::Deserialize)]
#[serde(default, deny_unknown_fields)]
pub struct Settings {
    /// The end of every `<title>`, and the header and the index heading
    /// when those are not set.
    pub title: String,
    /// What the top of the sidebar says.
    pub header: Option<String>,
    /// The index page's heading.
    pub index_title: Option<String>,
    /// Where the sidebar's "View site" link points. No link when unset.
    pub site_url: Option<String>,
    /// A stylesheet every screen links after the shipped one. More than one
    /// is `AdminConfig::stylesheet` again, in [`mount`].
    pub stylesheet: Option<String>,
}

impl Default for Settings {
    fn default() -> Self {
        Self {
            title: "My Blog admin".to_string(),
            header: None,
            index_title: None,
            site_url: None,
            stylesheet: None,
        }
    }
}

/// The settings this run chose, kept for [`mount`].
///
/// `mount` runs when the route table is built, after the plugins are, and
/// takes no argument it could carry them in. A test that builds the table
/// without the application gets the defaults.
static CHOSEN: OnceLock<Settings> = OnceLock::new();

/// Holds the admin's settings. It puts nothing on the context.
pub struct AdminSettings;

impl Plugin for AdminSettings {
    type Config = Settings;
    type Provides = NoContext;
    const NAME: &'static str = "admin";

    fn new(config: Settings) -> Self {
        // The first application built in this process decides.
        let _ = CHOSEN.set(config);
        Self
    }
}

/// Every screen of the admin, under `prefix`.
///
/// Four routes: the index and everything under it, for GET and for POST.
/// The admin parses its own paths, so the table gives it the whole path and
/// does not split it into twelve routes.
pub fn mount(routes: &mut RouterBuilder<Handler>, prefix: &str) -> Result<(), BuildError> {
    let defaults = Settings::default();
    let settings = CHOSEN.get().unwrap_or(&defaults);
    let mut config = AdminConfig::new().titled(&settings.title).at(prefix);
    if let Some(header) = &settings.header {
        config = config.headed(header);
    }
    if let Some(heading) = &settings.index_title {
        config = config.index_titled(heading);
    }
    if let Some(href) = &settings.site_url {
        config = config.site_url(href);
    }
    if let Some(href) = &settings.stylesheet {
        config = config.stylesheet(href);
    }
    // The tabs other modules add, such as analytics. Each one is on or off in
    // the module file of the module that adds it: `integrate.admin`.
    config = crate::plugins::admin_panels(config);
    // Built by the first request, over the ORM: see `OverOrm`.
    let admin = Arc::new(OverOrm::new(config));
    let rest = format!("{}/{{*rest}}", prefix.trim_end_matches('/'));
    for pattern in [prefix, rest.as_str()] {
        routes.route(pattern, Verb::Get, screen(Arc::clone(&admin)))?;
        routes.route(pattern, Verb::Post, screen(Arc::clone(&admin)))?;
    }
    Ok(())
}

/// The admin over the ORM, built once, by the first request.
///
/// `mount` runs when the route table is built, and the plugins start after
/// that: the `db` plugin opens the pool in `before_start`, and the `model`
/// plugin wraps it. So the admin cannot be built in `mount`. The first
/// request after the start carries the ORM on its context, and the admin is
/// built from it then. A row policy for a model that is not listed therefore
/// panics on that first request, not at startup.
///
/// One per route table. The table `src/app.rs` builds only ever sees a
/// request the application dispatched. A table a test builds sees a `Ctx`
/// built by hand, which has no ORM: that admin is built over an empty
/// source, with the same configuration, and shows no rows.
struct OverOrm {
    config: Mutex<Option<AdminConfig>>,
    built: OnceLock<Arc<Admin>>,
}

impl OverOrm {
    fn new(config: AdminConfig) -> Self {
        Self {
            config: Mutex::new(Some(config)),
            built: OnceLock::new(),
        }
    }

    /// The admin, built by the first request that asks.
    fn for_request(&self, ctx: &Ctx) -> Arc<Admin> {
        Arc::clone(self.built.get_or_init(|| {
            let config = self
                .config
                .lock()
                .unwrap_or_else(PoisonError::into_inner)
                .take()
                .expect("the admin is built once");
            match ctx.provided::<brocanth_model::Orm, _>() {
                Some(orm) => Arc::new(Admin::new(
                    Arc::new(models::Models::new(orm.connection().clone())),
                    config,
                )),
                None => Arc::new(Admin::new(Arc::new(Fixture::new()), config)),
            }
        }))
    }
}

/// One handler for every screen. The admin reads the path and decides.
///
/// The admin is async because a database is, and so is a handler in this
/// table: the answer is awaited and holds no thread while it waits.
fn screen(admin: Arc<OverOrm>) -> Handler {
    Box::new(move |ctx: &mut Ctx| {
        let admin = Arc::clone(&admin);
        Box::pin(async move {
            let ctx = &*ctx;
            let admin = admin.for_request(ctx);
            // HEAD reaches the GET handler, and the admin knows two verbs.
            let method = match ctx.verb {
                Verb::Post => "POST",
                _ => "GET",
            };
            let form = submission(ctx);
            let staff = crate::plugins::superuser::staff(ctx).await?;
            let who = principal(staff.as_ref());
            // `?` on the admin's own failure: a data source that failed, or
            // a template that did not render. A refusal is not one: it is a
            // page.
            let answer = admin
                .dispatch(who, method, &ctx.path, &ctx.query, &form)
                .await?;
            Ok(match answer {
                brocanth_admin::Response::Page { status, html } => {
                    Response::new(status, stamped(ctx, html))
                }
                brocanth_admin::Response::Redirect { status, location } => {
                    Response::empty(status).with_header("location", location)
                }
            })
        })
    })
}

/// The page with the CSRF token in every form that posts.
///
/// The admin renders its own forms and does not know the token, so the
/// field goes in here. Without a token on the request -- a test that built
/// the context by hand -- the page goes out unchanged.
fn stamped(ctx: &Ctx, html: String) -> String {
    match ctx.hooked::<brocanth_middleware::CsrfToken>() {
        Some(token) => token.stamp_forms(&html),
        None => html,
    }
}

/// Who is asking, as the admin's policy sees it: the signed-in member of
/// staff, or anonymous.
///
/// `StaffPolicy` reads it, behind `integrate.admin` in
/// `src/modules/superuser.rs`. A policy of your own can read it too:
/// `principal.downcast::<brocanth_auth::Staff>()`.
fn principal(staff: Option<&brocanth_auth::Staff>) -> Principal<'_> {
    match staff {
        Some(staff) => Principal::new(staff),
        None => Principal::anonymous(),
    }
}

/// The form a POST sent. Anything that is not urlencoded is an empty form,
/// so the screen shows its errors and writes nothing.
fn submission(ctx: &Ctx) -> Submission {
    let urlencoded = ctx
        .content_type()
        .is_some_and(|t| t.starts_with("application/x-www-form-urlencoded"));
    match ctx.body_text() {
        Some(body) if urlencoded => Submission::from_urlencoded(body),
        _ => Submission::new(),
    }
}

#[cfg(test)]
mod tests {
    use brocanth::routing::{Dispatch, Verb};

    use crate::pages::Ctx;

    /// The admin is in the table, where src/modules/admin.rs mounts it, and
    /// it answers 403 until somebody opens it.
    #[tokio::test]
    async fn the_admin_is_mounted_and_ships_locked() {
        let at = crate::modules::admin::MODULE.mount_at;
        let router = crate::pages::router().expect("the table builds");
        let Dispatch::Found { handler, matched } = router.dispatch(Verb::Get, at) else {
            panic!("{at} is not in the table");
        };
        let mut ctx = Ctx::new(Verb::Get, at, matched.params());
        let answer = handler(&mut ctx).await.expect("the admin answers");
        assert_eq!(answer.status, 403);
    }
}
===== src/plugins/admin/models.rs =====
//! The models the admin shows: one line per entity.
//!
//! This file is yours. A model is in the admin when it is listed here, and
//! not because it compiled: a screen with a delete button on it shows what
//! somebody chose. Each line is the module path SeaORM's registry reports,
//! and the entity:
//!
//! ```ignore
//! "my_app::models::post" => crate::models::post::Entity,
//! ```
//!
//! A listed model still answers 403 until it has a row policy:
//! `AdminConfig::resource(..)` in `mount`.

brocanth_admin_model::admin_source! {
    /// Every model this admin shows, over the ORM's connection.
    pub struct Models;
}
===== src/plugins/audit/mod.rs =====
//! The change history: who is asking, and the sink a page writes through.
//!
//! Written once by `brocanth create`. This file is yours.
//!
//! A write through the model layer is recorded when it is published to the
//! sink below:
//!
//! ```ignore
//! post.insert_published(ctx.orm(), &crate::plugins::audit::sink(ctx)).await?;
//! ```
//!
//! The history is the `brocanth_audit` table of the `[db]` database. With
//! the `admin` module, the History tab at `/admin/history` lists it, every
//! record has a History link, and every admin write is recorded with the
//! values before and after.

use brocanth_audit::{By, Trail};

use crate::pages::Ctx;

/// A sink that records each change as made by whoever made this request.
///
/// Records nothing on a `Ctx` built by hand, which has no plugin behind it.
#[allow(dead_code)]
pub fn sink(ctx: &Ctx) -> By {
    match ctx.provided::<Trail, _>() {
        Some(trail) => trail.sink(actor(ctx)),
        None => By::nothing(),
    }
}

/// Who is asking, as the history names them.
///
/// Nobody until this reads your user off the request. With a user on the
/// context, this is one line:
///
/// ```ignore
/// ctx.provided::<CurrentUser, _>().map(|user| user.email.clone())
/// ```
#[allow(dead_code)]
fn actor(_ctx: &Ctx) -> Option<String> {
    None
}
===== src/plugins/auth/mod.rs =====
//! Accounts and sessions, as this application registers them.
//!
//! One interface, no providers yet, and a local account path that does not
//! leak who has one: `register`, `login`, `request_password_reset` and
//! `verify_email` answer the same shape and take the same time whether or not
//! the address exists, because the expensive hash runs on both paths.
//!
//! `brocanth-auth` is in `Cargo.toml` because this file is here, and through
//! no other route. Delete the registration in `src/plugins/mod.rs` and
//! delete this file, and the application has no accounts and links nothing
//! to hash a password with.
//!
//! # Signing somebody in
//!
//! Everything is behind one context item, and a handler in an application
//! that never registered this plugin does not compile:
//!
//! ```ignore
//! use brocanth_auth::mfa::{Admission, Enrolled, FactorPolicy, FactorSet};
//!
//! let auth = ctx.provided::<Services, _>().expect("a dispatched request");
//!
//! // 1. The credential. `login` answers the same shape and takes the same
//! //    time whether or not the address has an account.
//! let who = auth.accounts().login(email, password).await?;
//!
//! // 2. What this account could prove. Nothing: `Mfa` needs a store trait
//! //    this application does not implement, so the enrolment is empty and
//! //    the constructor is named so that a reviewer stops on it. The day
//! //    there is an `MfaStore`, `Mfa::second_factors` answers this properly
//! //    and this line goes.
//! let enrolled =
//!     Enrolled::without_asking_the_store(who.account().id.clone(), FactorSet::empty());
//!
//! // 3. The policy, which consumes the proof. `Admitted` has no public
//! //    constructor, so this step cannot be skipped -- a sign-in that
//! //    forgot the second factor is a program that does not compile rather
//! //    than one that ships.
//! let Admission::Admit(admitted) =
//!     FactorPolicy::default().admit(&enrolled, who.into_proof())
//! else {
//!     return Err(Error::new(401, "sign in"));
//! };
//!
//! // 4. The session, which takes the admission by value.
//! let started = auth.sessions().begin_with_factors(admitted, None).await?;
//! // `started.cookie` is the `Set-Cookie` header to send back.
//! ```
//!
//! **A page cannot do that yet.** A handler here is
//! `Box<dyn Fn(&mut Ctx) -> Result<Response, Error>>`, which is synchronous,
//! and every method above is `async fn`. What runs today is a test, a
//! `manage` subcommand, and anything else of yours that already has a
//! runtime. The sign-in form arrives with the core's request hooks, which are
//! also what the two middleware offers below are waiting for.
//!
//! # Three decisions this file makes, each in one line
//!
//! They are type aliases so that changing one changes one line and nothing
//! else in the project changes type.
//!
//! 1. **`Store = PgStore`.** Accounts and sessions are rows in the
//!    `brocanth_auth_*` tables of `[auth] url`, so they survive a restart and
//!    a deploy. The pool is built in `before_start` with no connection, and
//!    the readiness check that `serve` awaits before it listens runs
//!    `migrate` once (it is idempotent and takes an advisory lock). To review
//!    the schema in a migration file instead, copy `PgStore::schema_sql()`
//!    into one and delete the `migrate` call.
//! 2. **`Http = StubHttp`.** There are no providers, so nothing in this
//!    binary ever performs a provider request, and a client that answers "no
//!    stubbed route" is a louder failure than one that opens a socket. It is
//!    also the line that keeps reqwest, rustls and their trees out of the lock
//!    file. Adding a provider changes it; see below.
//! 3. **`Passwords::recommended()`, not a setting.** The hash is argon2id and
//!    there is no choice to make. The one question a deployment gets to answer
//!    is cost, and it is a named constructor rather than three numbers in a
//!    file, because three numbers in a file is how an application ends up
//!    hashing at a cost nobody chose.
//!
//! # Adding a provider
//!
//! Three steps, and the flow code never learns the provider's name.
//!
//! 1. In `Cargo.toml`, `brocanth-auth = { ..., features = ["reqwest-client"] }`.
//! 2. Here, `pub type Http = brocanth_auth::http_client::ReqwestClient;`.
//! 3. Here, in `configure`, push one `ConfiguredProvider`:
//!
//! ```ignore
//! use brocanth_auth::oauth::ClientConfig;
//! use brocanth_auth::plugin::ConfiguredProvider;
//! use brocanth_auth::providers::catalog::GOOGLE;
//!
//! providers: vec![ConfiguredProvider {
//!     provider: GOOGLE,
//!     client: ClientConfig::new(
//!         std::env::var("GOOGLE_CLIENT_ID")?,
//!         std::env::var("GOOGLE_CLIENT_SECRET")?,
//!         "https://example.test/auth/google/callback",
//!     ),
//! }]
//! ```
//!
//! A provider is data -- endpoints, scopes, and a mapping from its profile
//! JSON to a common identity -- so the second one is another entry in that
//! `vec!` and no new code anywhere. `brocanth_auth::providers::catalog` has
//! the ones that are already written down.
//!
//! Two things change with the first provider. `same_site` below must become
//! `Lax`, because the callback is a cross-site top-level navigation and
//! `Strict` drops the cookie on the way back. And a matching email still does
//! not link an account to a provider: `Linking` returns a refusal the
//! application answers with a page that asks, because "this provider says
//! alice@example.com" is not Alice consenting, and several real products have
//! shipped that account takeover.
//!
//! # What is not here
//!
//! Second factors, one-time codes and login alerts. Each needs a store trait
//! of its own -- `MfaStore`, `OtpStore`, `SignInLocationStore` -- and
//! `brocanth-auth` deliberately keeps all three off `Auth`, so that an
//! application wanting a password form does not have to implement a TOTP
//! table. An application that wants them builds them here, from the same
//! `Arc<Store>`, and puts them on the context itself: one line each, in this
//! file, where the decision is visible.

use std::sync::Arc;

use brocanth::plugin_api::{Lifecycle, Plugin, PluginError};
use brocanth::plugin_context::{ContextBuilder, ProvidesContext};
use brocanth::plugin_shutdown::ShutdownCx;
use brocanth_auth::http_client::StubHttp;
use brocanth_auth::plugin::AuthConfig;
use brocanth_auth::session::{SameSite, SessionConfig, SigningKey};
use brocanth_auth::{Clock as _, Passwords, PgStore, SystemClock};

/// Where identities, sessions and single-use tokens live.
///
/// Postgres, at `[auth] url`. See the file header.
pub type Store = PgStore;

/// How this application reaches a provider.
///
/// It does not. There are no providers, so nothing calls this.
pub type Http = StubHttp;

/// Where the time comes from.
pub type Clock = SystemClock;

/// Everything a handler needs, behind one `Arc`: registration, login, the
/// token service, sessions and identity linking.
///
/// An alias rather than a wrapper, because `brocanth_auth::Auth` is already a
/// `ContextItem`. Cloning is cheap and the clone shares the provider key
/// cache, which is why there is one of these and not one per request.
pub type Services = brocanth_auth::Auth<Store, Http, Clock>;

/// What this plugin is built from.
///
/// Five values a file can carry. The store, the client, the clock and the
/// hasher are built below, because none of them is a thing a TOML file can
/// say.
///
/// It derives `Deserialize` because this application reads it from
/// `[auth]` in a TOML file. Drop the config plugin and the derive goes
/// with it: `Plugin::Config` carries no bounds the core imposes.
#[derive(Debug, serde::Deserialize)]
#[serde(default, deny_unknown_fields)]
pub struct Settings {
    /// The Postgres database the accounts and sessions live in.
    pub url: String,
    /// The *name* of the environment variable holding the session signing
    /// key, not the key. 32 bytes, base64url.
    pub signing_key_env: String,
    /// The session cookie's name. `__Host-` in production: it is the only
    /// prefix a browser enforces, and what it enforces is `Secure`, a path of
    /// `/` and no `Domain` -- so a subdomain takeover cannot set a session
    /// cookie for the parent.
    pub cookie_name: String,
    /// Whether the cookie is `Secure`. False only on plain-HTTP localhost,
    /// and then the name above must lose its `__Host-` prefix as well.
    pub secure: bool,
    /// Seconds of inactivity before the session is dead.
    pub idle_timeout: i64,
    /// Seconds from creation before it is dead however much it was used.
    pub absolute_timeout: i64,
}

impl Default for Settings {
    fn default() -> Self {
        Self {
            url: "postgres://localhost/development".to_string(),
            signing_key_env: "MY_BLOG_SESSION_KEY".to_string(),
            cookie_name: "__Host-session".to_string(),
            secure: true,
            idle_timeout: 8 * 60 * 60,
            absolute_timeout: 30 * 24 * 60 * 60,
        }
    }
}

pub struct Auth {
    settings: Settings,
    /// Built at `before_start`, so this is an `Option` for exactly the length
    /// of the build.
    services: Option<Services>,
    /// The store, kept for the sweep.
    store: Option<Arc<Store>>,
    /// The sweep, stopped at shutdown.
    sweeper: Option<tokio::task::JoinHandle<()>>,
}

impl Plugin for Auth {
    type Config = Settings;
    type Provides = ProvidesContext<Services>;
    const NAME: &'static str = "auth";

    fn new(config: Settings) -> Self {
        Self {
            settings: config,
            services: None,
            store: None,
            sweeper: None,
        }
    }

    fn configure(&mut self, _lc: &mut Lifecycle<'_>) -> Result<(), PluginError> {
        if self.settings.url.is_empty() {
            return Err(PluginError::new(
                "[auth] url is required; accounts live in a Postgres database",
            ));
        }
        Ok(())
    }

    fn before_start(&mut self, lc: &mut Lifecycle<'_>) -> Result<(), PluginError> {
        if self.settings.cookie_name.is_empty() {
            return Err(PluginError::new("[auth] cookie_name must not be empty"));
        }
        // A `__Host-` cookie is `Secure` by definition, so a configuration
        // where the two disagree produces a cookie no browser stores and a
        // login that silently never takes. Caught here, at startup, where it
        // can still be answered.
        if self.settings.cookie_name.starts_with("__Host-") && !self.settings.secure {
            return Err(PluginError::new(
                "[auth] a __Host- cookie is Secure by definition; set secure = true, \
                 or drop the prefix for plain-HTTP development",
            ));
        }

        let name = self.settings.signing_key_env.clone();
        let signing_key = match std::env::var(&name) {
            // Rejected rather than stretched. Padding a four-character secret
            // to 32 bytes is how a deployment ends up with a forgeable cookie
            // and no warning anywhere.
            Ok(raw) => SigningKey::parse(&raw)
                .map_err(|e| PluginError::new(format!("[auth] ${name}: {e}")))?,
            // This project's own tests build the application with no secret
            // in the environment. The key is fixed and public, and `cfg(test)`
            // keeps it out of every binary.
            Err(_) if cfg!(test) => SigningKey::from_bytes([7; 32]),
            Err(_) => {
                return Err(PluginError::new(format!(
                    "[auth] ${name} is not set. It is 32 random bytes, base64url:\n  \
                     export {name}=$(head -c 32 /dev/urandom | base64 | tr '+/' '-_' | tr -d '=')\n\
                     There is no default: a key invented at startup would log every session \
                     out on each restart and differ between the instances of a fleet."
                )));
            }
        };

        let passwords = Passwords::recommended()
            .map_err(|e| PluginError::new(format!("[auth] argon2id: {e}")))?;

        let session = SessionConfig {
            cookie_name: self.settings.cookie_name.clone(),
            secure: self.settings.secure,
            idle_timeout: self.settings.idle_timeout,
            absolute_timeout: self.settings.absolute_timeout,
            // Strict, and not the crate's own `Lax` default. `Lax` is there
            // for the cross-site top-level navigation an OAuth callback is,
            // and this application has no provider. Add one and this line
            // has to become `Lax` or the cookie is dropped on the way back.
            same_site: SameSite::Strict,
            // Path `/`, and thirty minutes for an impersonated session. Both
            // are the crate's defaults and both are arguments rather than
            // numbers; `SessionConfig` carries them.
            ..SessionConfig::default()
        };

        // The hooks are synchronous and opening a pool is not, so the pool
        // opens lazily and nothing here waits. Waiting would hang a
        // current-thread runtime, where this thread drives the I/O. The pool
        // spawns its upkeep task, so it needs the runtime entered.
        let runtime = tokio::runtime::Handle::try_current().map_err(|e| {
            PluginError::new(format!(
                "[auth] needs a Tokio runtime at startup to open its pool ({e})"
            ))
        })?;
        let store = {
            let _entered = runtime.enter();
            PgStore::connect_lazy(&self.settings.url)
                .map_err(|e| PluginError::new(format!("[auth] {e}")))?
        };
        let store = Arc::new(store);
        self.store = Some(Arc::clone(&store));
        // Ready when the database answers. The first check also creates the
        // tables, and the first check is the one `serve` awaits before it
        // listens: an unreachable database stops the start there.
        let probe = Arc::clone(&store);
        let migrated = Arc::new(tokio::sync::OnceCell::new());
        lc.ready_when(move || {
            let probe = Arc::clone(&probe);
            let migrated = Arc::clone(&migrated);
            async move {
                migrated
                    .get_or_try_init(|| probe.migrate())
                    .await
                    .map_err(|e| format!("creating the auth tables: {e}"))?;
                probe.ping().await.map_err(|e| e.to_string())
            }
        });

        self.services = Some(
            brocanth_auth::Auth::build(AuthConfig {
                store,
                http: Arc::new(Http::new()),
                clock: Arc::new(SystemClock),
                signing_key,
                session,
                passwords: Arc::new(passwords),
                // Add one here. The file header has the three lines.
                providers: Vec::new(),
            })
            .map_err(|e| PluginError::new(format!("[auth] {e}")))?,
        );
        // In the services too, for a plugin that reads the accounts at
        // startup, such as `superuser`.
        if let Some(services) = &self.services {
            lc.services_mut().insert(services.clone());
        }
        Ok(())
    }

    fn start(&mut self, _lc: &mut Lifecycle<'_>) -> Result<(), PluginError> {
        // Dead sessions, spent tokens and expired one-time codes, every
        // fifteen minutes. Every read checks expiry already, so this only
        // keeps the tables small. Each instance runs it, and the deletes do
        // not mind.
        let Some(store) = self.store.clone() else {
            return Ok(());
        };
        let idle_timeout = self.settings.idle_timeout;
        self.sweeper = Some(tokio::spawn(async move {
            let mut every = tokio::time::interval(std::time::Duration::from_secs(15 * 60));
            loop {
                every.tick().await;
                if let Err(e) = store.sweep(SystemClock.now(), idle_timeout).await {
                    crate::plugins::say!(warn, "[auth] sweep: {e}"; error = %e);
                }
            }
        }));
        Ok(())
    }

    fn shutdown(&mut self, _sd: &mut ShutdownCx<'_>) -> Result<(), PluginError> {
        if let Some(sweeper) = self.sweeper.take() {
            sweeper.abort();
        }
        Ok(())
    }

    fn provide(&self, cx: &mut ContextBuilder) {
        // `before_start` runs before the server is handed the application, so
        // the services are here by the time any request builds a context.
        if let Some(services) = &self.services {
            cx.insert(services.clone());
        }
    }
}
===== src/plugins/auth_lockout.rs =====
//! Account lockout: after too many wrong passwords, an account refuses
//! password sign-in for a while. The policy is in
//! `src/modules/auth_lockout.rs`.
//!
//! Sign in through `lockout(&auth)?.login(auth.accounts(), email, password)`
//! and render `Attempt::Refused` and `Attempt::JustLocked` alike: a page
//! that says "locked" tells a stranger the address has an account. On
//! `JustLocked`, mail the owner. After a password reset, call `clear`.

use brocanth_auth::{AuthError, Lockout, LockoutPolicy};

use crate::plugins::auth::{Clock, Services, Store};

/// The policy in `src/modules/auth_lockout.rs`.
///
/// # Errors
/// [`AuthError::Misconfigured`] when an option is out of its range.
pub fn policy() -> Result<LockoutPolicy, AuthError> {
    let max = crate::modules::auth_lockout::MODULE.max_failures;
    let window = crate::modules::auth_lockout::MODULE.window_secs;
    let lock_for = crate::modules::auth_lockout::MODULE.lock_for_secs;
    LockoutPolicy::new(
        u32::try_from(max).unwrap_or(u32::MAX),
        i64::try_from(window).unwrap_or(i64::MAX),
        i64::try_from(lock_for).unwrap_or(i64::MAX),
    )
}

/// Password sign-in that counts failures, over the auth store.
///
/// # Errors
/// [`AuthError::Misconfigured`] when an option is out of its range.
pub fn lockout(auth: &Services) -> Result<Lockout<Store, Clock>, AuthError> {
    Ok(auth.lockout(policy()?))
}

#[cfg(test)]
mod tests {
    #[test]
    fn the_policy_in_the_module_file_is_valid() {
        if let Err(e) = super::policy() {
            panic!("{e}");
        }
    }
}
===== src/plugins/auth_oauth/mod.rs =====
//! Sign-in with a provider: GitHub, Google, Microsoft or GitLab.
//!
//! Written once by `brocanth create`. This file is yours.
//!
//! The routes, under `mount_at` in `src/modules/auth_oauth.rs`:
//!
//! | Route | Does |
//! |---|---|
//! | `GET /oauth/<provider>` | a page with one button, "Continue with GitHub" |
//! | `POST /oauth/<provider>` | a signed flow cookie, then a redirect to the provider |
//! | `GET /oauth/<provider>/callback` | the provider's answer: a session, or a page that asks |
//! | `GET`, `POST /oauth/<provider>/link` | connects the provider to the signed-in account |
//!
//! A sign-in page of yours links each provider with a form that posts to
//! `/oauth/<provider>`. A plain link to the same path shows the button page.
//!
//! # Linking
//!
//! A provider joins an existing account only by a POST from a session that
//! is signed in as that account. The callback never links by itself: "the
//! provider says alice@example.com" is not Alice's consent. A person whose
//! address already has an account signs in with the password first, then
//! confirms at `/oauth/<provider>/link`.
//!
//! # Another provider
//!
//! `brocanth_auth::providers::catalog` has Discord, Slack and Facebook too.
//! Add a field pair to `Settings` and a line to `Settings::configured`.
//! Apple needs a client secret that you sign with a private key, and its
//! callback is a cross-site POST: add its callback path to the CSRF
//! exemptions first.

use std::sync::{Arc, OnceLock};

use brocanth::plugin_api::{Lifecycle, Plugin, PluginError};
use brocanth::plugin_context::NoContext;
use brocanth::routing::{BuildError, RouterBuilder, Verb};
use brocanth_auth::http_client::ReqwestClient;
use brocanth_auth::oauth::web::{OauthAnswer, OauthRequest, OauthRoutes};
use brocanth_auth::oauth::ClientConfig;
use brocanth_auth::plugin::ConfiguredProvider;
use brocanth_auth::providers::{catalog, Provider};
use brocanth_auth::SystemClock;

use crate::pages::{Ctx, Error, Handler, Response};
use crate::plugins::auth::Services;

/// Your registration at each provider: `[auth_oauth]` in the config file,
/// or `$<APP>_AUTH_OAUTH__<KEY>`.
///
/// It derives `Deserialize` because this application reads it from
/// `[auth_oauth]` in a TOML file. Drop the config plugin and the derive goes
/// with it: `Plugin::Config` carries no bounds the core imposes.
#[derive(Debug, serde::Deserialize)]
#[serde(default, deny_unknown_fields)]
pub struct Settings {
    /// The providers to offer, by id.
    pub providers: Vec<String>,
    /// The scheme and host the providers send the browser back to.
    pub base_url: String,
    /// Where a person goes after a sign-in or a link.
    pub after_sign_in: String,
    /// Your password sign-in page, for a person who must sign in before a
    /// link. The flow adds `?next=<the link page>`.
    pub sign_in_page: Option<String>,
    /// The client id of the GitHub OAuth app.
    pub github_client_id: Option<String>,
    /// The client secret of the GitHub OAuth app. No flag: it is a secret.
    pub github_client_secret: Option<String>,
    /// The client id of the Google OAuth client.
    pub google_client_id: Option<String>,
    /// The client secret of the Google OAuth client. No flag: it is a secret.
    pub google_client_secret: Option<String>,
    /// The application (client) id of the Microsoft Entra app.
    pub microsoft_client_id: Option<String>,
    /// The client secret of the Microsoft Entra app. No flag: it is a secret.
    pub microsoft_client_secret: Option<String>,
    /// The application id of the GitLab application.
    pub gitlab_client_id: Option<String>,
    /// The secret of the GitLab application. No flag: it is a secret.
    pub gitlab_client_secret: Option<String>,
}

impl Default for Settings {
    fn default() -> Self {
        Self {
            providers: vec!["github".to_string()],
            base_url: "http://localhost:3000".to_string(),
            after_sign_in: "/".to_string(),
            sign_in_page: None,
            github_client_id: None,
            github_client_secret: None,
            google_client_id: None,
            google_client_secret: None,
            microsoft_client_id: None,
            microsoft_client_secret: None,
            gitlab_client_id: None,
            gitlab_client_secret: None,
        }
    }
}

impl Settings {
    /// Each provider in `providers`, with its registration. An error names
    /// the first one that has no id or no secret.
    fn configured(&self, prefix: &str) -> Result<Vec<ConfiguredProvider>, String> {
        let known: [(Provider, &Option<String>, &Option<String>); 4] = [
            (
                catalog::GITHUB,
                &self.github_client_id,
                &self.github_client_secret,
            ),
            (
                catalog::GOOGLE,
                &self.google_client_id,
                &self.google_client_secret,
            ),
            (
                catalog::MICROSOFT,
                &self.microsoft_client_id,
                &self.microsoft_client_secret,
            ),
            (
                catalog::GITLAB,
                &self.gitlab_client_id,
                &self.gitlab_client_secret,
            ),
        ];
        let mut out = Vec::new();
        for id in &self.providers {
            let Some((provider, client_id, secret)) = known
                .iter()
                .find(|(provider, _, _)| provider.id == id.as_str())
            else {
                return Err(format!(
                    "[auth_oauth] providers names `{id}`, which this file does not configure\n\
                     help: use github, google, microsoft or gitlab, or add the provider to \
                     `Settings` in src/plugins/auth_oauth/mod.rs"
                ));
            };
            let (Some(client_id), Some(secret)) = (client_id, secret) else {
                return Err(format!(
                    "[auth_oauth] `{id}` has no client id or no client secret\n\
                     help: set {id}_client_id and {id}_client_secret under [auth_oauth], or \
                     the variables $<APP>_AUTH_OAUTH__{upper}_CLIENT_ID and \
                     $<APP>_AUTH_OAUTH__{upper}_CLIENT_SECRET",
                    upper = id.to_uppercase()
                ));
            };
            out.push(ConfiguredProvider {
                provider: provider.clone(),
                client: ClientConfig::new(
                    client_id.clone(),
                    secret.clone(),
                    OauthRoutes::<ReqwestClient, SystemClock>::callback_url(
                        &self.base_url,
                        prefix,
                        id,
                    ),
                ),
            });
        }
        Ok(out)
    }
}

/// The settings this run chose, kept for [`mount`].
///
/// `mount` runs when the route table is built, after the plugins are, and
/// takes no argument it could carry them in. A test that builds the table
/// without the application gets the defaults, and so no provider.
static CHOSEN: OnceLock<Settings> = OnceLock::new();

/// Holds the settings and checks them at startup. It puts nothing on the
/// context: the routes read the auth plugin's services.
pub struct AuthOauth;

impl Plugin for AuthOauth {
    type Config = Settings;
    type Provides = NoContext;
    const NAME: &'static str = "auth_oauth";

    fn new(config: Settings) -> Self {
        // The first application built in this process decides.
        let _ = CHOSEN.set(config);
        Self
    }

    fn configure(&mut self, _lc: &mut Lifecycle<'_>) -> Result<(), PluginError> {
        let Some(settings) = CHOSEN.get() else {
            return Ok(());
        };
        // A provider without a secret is a button that answers an error. In
        // this project's own tests no secret is set, so nothing is checked.
        if cfg!(test) {
            return Ok(());
        }
        settings
            .configured(crate::modules::auth_oauth::MODULE.mount_at)
            .map(drop)
            .map_err(PluginError::new)
    }
}

type Routes = OauthRoutes<ReqwestClient, SystemClock>;

/// Every route of the flow, under `prefix`.
///
/// Two routes: everything under the prefix, for GET and for POST. The flow
/// reads the provider and the step from the path.
pub fn mount(routes: &mut RouterBuilder<Handler>, prefix: &str) -> Result<(), BuildError> {
    let defaults = Settings::default();
    let settings = CHOSEN.get().unwrap_or(&defaults);
    // `configure` refuses a provider without its registration, so this
    // drops one only when the application is not the one being started.
    let providers = settings.configured(prefix).unwrap_or_default();
    // Fails only when the TLS backend cannot start, which no request could
    // recover from.
    let http = ReqwestClient::with_default_timeout().expect("auth_oauth: an HTTP client");
    let mut flow = Routes::new(Arc::new(http), Arc::new(SystemClock), providers)
        .at(prefix)
        .after_sign_in(&settings.after_sign_in);
    if let Some(page) = &settings.sign_in_page {
        flow = flow.sign_in_page(page);
    }
    let flow = Arc::new(flow);
    let pattern = format!("{}/{{*rest}}", prefix.trim_end_matches('/'));
    routes.route(&pattern, Verb::Get, step(Arc::clone(&flow)))?;
    routes.route(&pattern, Verb::Post, step(flow))?;
    Ok(())
}

/// One handler for every step. The flow reads the path and decides.
fn step(flow: Arc<Routes>) -> Handler {
    Box::new(move |ctx: &mut Ctx| {
        let flow = Arc::clone(&flow);
        Box::pin(async move {
            let ctx = &*ctx;
            let Some(auth) = ctx.provided::<Services, _>() else {
                return Err(Error::new(
                    503,
                    "auth_oauth: the auth services are not on this request\n\
                     help: register the auth plugin in src/plugins/mod.rs",
                ));
            };
            let request = OauthRequest {
                post: ctx.verb == Verb::Post,
                path: &ctx.path,
                query: &ctx.query,
                body: ctx.body_text().unwrap_or(""),
                cookies: ctx.header("cookie").unwrap_or(""),
            };
            let answer = flow
                .answer(auth, request)
                .await
                .map_err(|e| Error::internal(format!("auth_oauth: {e}")))?;
            let (response, cookies) = match answer {
                OauthAnswer::Redirect { location, cookies } => (
                    Response::empty(303).with_header("location", location),
                    cookies,
                ),
                OauthAnswer::Page {
                    status,
                    page,
                    cookies,
                } => (Response::new(status, stamped(ctx, page.html())), cookies),
            };
            Ok(cookies.iter().fold(response, |response, cookie| {
                response.with_header("set-cookie", cookie.as_str())
            }))
        })
    })
}

/// The page with the CSRF token in every form that posts.
///
/// The flow renders its own forms and does not know the token. Without a
/// token on the request, a test that built the context by hand, the page
/// goes out unchanged.
fn stamped(ctx: &Ctx, html: String) -> String {
    match ctx.hooked::<brocanth_middleware::CsrfToken>() {
        Some(token) => token.stamp_forms(&html),
        None => html,
    }
}
===== src/plugins/auth_screens/mod.rs =====
//! The account screens' plugin, and what every screen under
//! `src/pages/account/` shares.
//!
//! The pages are yours: change the words in `src/pages/account/screens.rsx`
//! and the flow in each page. Keep three rules:
//!
//! 1. Answer alike for an address that has an account and one that has
//!    not. A different word is how a stranger learns who signed up.
//! 2. Build a link in mail from [`Screens::link`], never from the request.
//! 3. Every form carries [`csrf_field`].

use brocanth::plugin_api::{Lifecycle, Plugin, PluginError};
use brocanth::plugin_context::{ContextBuilder, ContextItem, ProvidesContext};
use brocanth_auth::{Admission, AuthStore, FactorPolicy, SessionRecord, Verified};
use brocanth_middleware::{Flash, Level};

use crate::pages::{Ctx, Error, Response, Sent};
use crate::plugins::auth::Services;
use crate::plugins::mail::Outbox;

/// The cookie that carries a sign-in waiting for its second factor.
pub const PENDING: &str = "auth_pending";

/// What this plugin is built from.
///
/// It derives `Deserialize` because this application reads it from
/// `[auth_screens]` in a TOML file. Drop the config plugin and the derive goes
/// with it: `Plugin::Config` carries no bounds the core imposes.
#[derive(Debug, serde::Deserialize)]
#[serde(default, deny_unknown_fields)]
pub struct Settings {
    /// The address links in mail start with: `https://example.com`.
    pub base_url: String,
    /// The name an authenticator app shows beside the code.
    pub issuer: String,
}

impl Default for Settings {
    fn default() -> Self {
        Self {
            base_url: "http://localhost:3000".to_string(),
            issuer: "My Blog".to_string(),
        }
    }
}

/// What a screen reads off the context.
#[derive(Clone, Debug)]
pub struct Screens {
    base_url: String,
    issuer: String,
}

impl ContextItem for Screens {}

impl Screens {
    /// An absolute link to `path` on this site, for a mail.
    pub fn link(&self, path: &str) -> String {
        format!("{}{path}", self.base_url.trim_end_matches('/'))
    }

    /// The name an authenticator app shows.
    pub fn issuer(&self) -> &str {
        &self.issuer
    }
}

pub struct AuthScreens {
    screens: Screens,
}

impl Plugin for AuthScreens {
    type Config = Settings;
    type Provides = ProvidesContext<Screens>;
    const NAME: &'static str = "auth_screens";

    fn new(config: Settings) -> Self {
        Self {
            screens: Screens {
                base_url: config.base_url,
                issuer: config.issuer,
            },
        }
    }

    fn before_start(&mut self, _lc: &mut Lifecycle<'_>) -> Result<(), PluginError> {
        let url = &self.screens.base_url;
        if !(url.starts_with("https://") || url.starts_with("http://")) {
            return Err(PluginError::new(format!(
                "[auth_screens] base_url is {url:?}, which is not an absolute address\n\
                 help: set it to the address people reach this site at, such as \
                 https://example.com"
            )));
        }
        Ok(())
    }

    fn provide(&self, cx: &mut ContextBuilder) {
        cx.insert(self.screens.clone());
    }
}

/// The auth services. A clone: one `Arc`, so a page can hold it across an
/// `.await` and still write to the context.
pub fn services(ctx: &Ctx) -> Result<Services, Error> {
    ctx.provided::<Services, _>().cloned().ok_or_else(|| {
        Error::new(
            500,
            "the auth services are not on this context\nhelp: register the auth plugin",
        )
    })
}

/// The base address and the issuer.
pub fn screens(ctx: &Ctx) -> Result<Screens, Error> {
    ctx.provided::<Screens, _>().cloned().ok_or_else(|| {
        Error::new(
            500,
            "the account screens' settings are not on this context\n\
             help: register the auth_screens plugin",
        )
    })
}

/// The hidden input a form carries for the CSRF check. HTML, so a
/// template writes it with `{! }`.
pub fn csrf_field(ctx: &Ctx) -> String {
    ctx.hooked::<brocanth_middleware::CsrfToken>()
        .map(|token| token.hidden_field())
        .unwrap_or_default()
}

/// The form, or `None` when a field broke its rule. A body nobody can read
/// is an error: no form sent it.
pub fn form<T: zod::Entry>(ctx: &Ctx) -> Result<Option<T>, Error> {
    match ctx.read::<T>() {
        Sent::Good(value) => Ok(Some(value)),
        Sent::Faulty(_) => Ok(None),
        Sent::Unreadable(why) => Err(Error::new(why.status(), why.to_string())),
    }
}

/// One cookie of the request, by name.
pub fn cookie(ctx: &Ctx, name: &str) -> Option<String> {
    ctx.headers()
        .filter(|(header, _)| header.eq_ignore_ascii_case("cookie"))
        .flat_map(|(_, line)| line.split(';'))
        .find_map(|pair| {
            let (key, value) = pair.split_once('=')?;
            (key.trim() == name).then(|| value.trim().to_string())
        })
        .filter(|value| !value.is_empty())
}

/// The session this request carries, if it is a live one.
pub async fn signed_in(ctx: &Ctx, auth: &Services) -> Option<SessionRecord> {
    let value = cookie(ctx, &auth.sessions().config().cookie_name)?;
    auth.sessions().load(&value).await.ok()
}

/// A message for the next page.
pub fn flash(ctx: &mut Ctx, level: Level, text: &str) {
    if let Some(flash) = ctx.hooked_mut::<Flash>() {
        flash.push(level, text);
    }
}

/// `303 See Other`: after a post, the browser asks for `location` with GET.
pub fn see_other(location: &str) -> Response {
    Response::empty(303).with_header("location", location)
}

/// A mail with a plain-text body, through the outbox.
pub async fn mail(ctx: &Ctx, to: &str, subject: &str, text: String) -> Result<(), Error> {
    let outbox = ctx.provided::<Outbox, _>().cloned().ok_or_else(|| {
        Error::new(
            500,
            "no outbox on this context\nhelp: register the mail plugin",
        )
    })?;
    let message = outbox
        .message()
        .to(brocanth_mail::Address::parse(to)?)
        .subject(subject)
        .text(text)
        .build()?;
    outbox.deliver_later(message).await?;
    Ok(())
}

/// The email of the account a session belongs to, for a label.
pub async fn email_of(auth: &Services, session: &SessionRecord) -> Result<String, Error> {
    let account = auth
        .store()
        .account(&session.account)
        .await
        .map_err(|e| Error::new(500, e.to_string()))?;
    Ok(account
        .and_then(|account| account.email)
        .map(|email| email.as_str().to_string())
        .unwrap_or_default())
}

/// The second half of every sign-in: ask the policy, then open a session,
/// or wait for a second factor, or refuse.
pub async fn admit(ctx: &mut Ctx, auth: &Services, proof: Verified) -> Result<Response, Error> {
    let enrolled = auth.mfa()?.second_factors(&proof).await?;
    let secure = if auth.sessions().config().secure {
        "; Secure"
    } else {
        ""
    };
    match FactorPolicy::recommended().admit(&enrolled, proof) {
        Admission::Admit(admitted) => {
            // The session the browser had, if any, is replaced: a new
            // identifier at sign-in is the defence against fixation.
            let before = cookie(ctx, &auth.sessions().config().cookie_name)
                .and_then(|value| auth.sessions().unseal(&value));
            let started = auth
                .sessions()
                .begin_with_factors(admitted, before.as_ref())
                .await?;
            flash(ctx, Level::Success, "You are signed in.");
            Ok(see_other(crate::modules::auth_screens::MODULE.after_sign_in)
                .with_header("set-cookie", started.cookie.to_string())
                .with_header(
                    "set-cookie",
                    format!("{PENDING}=; Path=/account; Max-Age=0; HttpOnly; SameSite=Strict{secure}"),
                ))
        }
        Admission::Incomplete { proof, .. } => {
            let ttl = crate::modules::auth_screens::MODULE.pending_secs;
            let sealed = auth.sessions().seal_proof(&proof, ttl as i64);
            Ok(see_other("/account/code").with_header(
                "set-cookie",
                format!(
                    "{PENDING}={sealed}; Path=/account; Max-Age={ttl}; HttpOnly; SameSite=Strict{secure}"
                ),
            ))
        }
        Admission::EnrolmentRequired { .. } | Admission::Insufficient => {
            flash(
                ctx,
                Level::Error,
                "This account needs a second factor that it does not have.",
            );
            Ok(see_other("/account/signin"))
        }
    }
}

/// Tells the owner of an account that just locked, with the way back in.
pub async fn mail_locked(
    ctx: &Ctx,
    account: &brocanth_auth::Account,
    lock_for: i64,
) -> Result<(), Error> {
    let Some(email) = &account.email else {
        return Ok(());
    };
    let link = screens(ctx)?.link("/account/forgot");
    let text = format!(
        "Somebody entered a wrong password for your account too many times, \
         so it refuses to sign in for {} minutes. Nobody signed in.\n\n\
         If it was you, wait, or choose a new password now:\n\n{link}\n",
        lock_for / 60
    );
    mail(ctx, email.as_str(), "Your account is locked", text).await
}
===== src/plugins/cache/mod.rs =====
//! The cache, as this application registers it.
//!
//! The cache is a Redis or Valkey server. It costs one more server to run,
//! back up and watch, and it is the right trade when the cache is a workload
//! of its own: many writes a second, reads sent to replicas, or a need to
//! evict under `maxmemory`. The server expires entries by itself, so there is
//! no sweep and no table to create.
//!
//! `brocanth-cache` is in `Cargo.toml` because this file is here, and through
//! no other route. Delete the registration in `src/plugins/mod.rs` and delete
//! this file, and the application caches nothing and links nothing to do it
//! with.
//!
//! The crate also ships `CachePlugin`, and this is not that. `CachePlugin` is
//! handed a cache that is already open, because a pool is made asynchronously
//! and `Plugin::new` is not async. An application whose cache is one setting
//! wants the shorter road, so this plugin opens the cache at startup and
//! publishes the same `CacheHandle`. Hand-built caches -- a second namespace
//! policy, a backend of your own -- are what `CachePlugin` is for, and
//! swapping to it is a change in this file alone.

use std::sync::Arc;

use brocanth::plugin_api::{Lifecycle, Plugin, PluginError};
use brocanth::plugin_context::{ContextBuilder, ProvidesContext};
use brocanth_cache::CacheHandle;
use brocanth_cache_redis::RedisCache;

/// What this plugin is built from.
///
/// It derives `Deserialize` because this application reads it from
/// `[cache]` in a TOML file. Drop the config plugin and the derive goes
/// with it: `Plugin::Config` carries no bounds the core imposes.
#[derive(Debug, serde::Deserialize)]
#[serde(default, deny_unknown_fields)]
pub struct Settings {
    /// The Redis or Valkey URL the cache lives in: one server, a primary,
    /// or Sentinel. Not Redis Cluster: see `brocanth_cache_redis`.
    pub url: String,
}

impl Default for Settings {
    fn default() -> Self {
        Self {
            url: "redis://localhost:6379".to_string(),
        }
    }
}

pub struct Cache {
    settings: Settings,
    /// Open at `before_start` and not before, so the field is an `Option` for
    /// exactly the length of the build.
    handle: Option<CacheHandle>,
}

impl Plugin for Cache {
    type Config = Settings;
    type Provides = ProvidesContext<CacheHandle>;
    const NAME: &'static str = "cache";

    fn new(settings: Settings) -> Self {
        Self {
            settings,
            handle: None,
        }
    }

    fn configure(&mut self, _lc: &mut Lifecycle<'_>) -> Result<(), PluginError> {
        if self.settings.url.is_empty() {
            return Err(PluginError::new(
                "[cache] url is required; there is no default cache\n\
                 help: set `url` under [cache] in the config file, or the variable for `cache.url`",
            ));
        }
        Ok(())
    }

    fn before_start(&mut self, lc: &mut Lifecycle<'_>) -> Result<(), PluginError> {
        // The connection opens on the first command, so nothing here waits.
        // Its task is spawned on the runtime, so it needs the runtime entered.
        let runtime = tokio::runtime::Handle::try_current().map_err(|e| {
            PluginError::new(format!(
                "[cache] needs a Tokio runtime at startup to connect ({e})"
            ))
        })?;
        let cache = {
            let _entered = runtime.enter();
            RedisCache::open(&self.settings.url)
                .map_err(|e| PluginError::new(format!("[cache] {e}")))?
        };
        let cache: Arc<dyn brocanth_cache::Cache> = Arc::new(cache);
        // Ready when one read answers. A miss is a pass: the question is
        // whether the server answers. `serve` awaits this once before it
        // listens, so an unreachable server stops the start there.
        let probe = Arc::clone(&cache);
        lc.ready_when(move || {
            let probe = Arc::clone(&probe);
            async move {
                let key = brocanth_cache::Namespace::new("brocanth.readyz")
                    .and_then(|ns| brocanth_cache::Key::new(ns, "probe"))
                    .map_err(|e| e.to_string())?;
                probe.get(&key).await.map(drop).map_err(|e| {
                    format!("{e}\nhelp: check `[cache] url`, and that the Redis server runs")
                })
            }
        });
        let handle = CacheHandle::new(cache);
        // The first cache this process opens is the one the admin's cache
        // tab reads: the admin is built before any plugin starts.
        handle.install();
        self.handle = Some(handle);
        Ok(())
    }

    fn provide(&self, cx: &mut ContextBuilder) {
        // `before_start` runs before the server is handed the application, so
        // the handle is here by the time any request builds a context.
        if let Some(handle) = &self.handle {
            cx.insert(handle.clone());
        }
    }
}
===== src/plugins/db/mod.rs =====
//! The database, as this application registers it.
//!
//! One Postgres pool, built at startup and shared by every request. It opens
//! no connection in the build; `serve` checks it once before it listens. A page
//! reads it with `ctx.db()`. Another plugin reads it from the application's
//! services: `lc.require_service::<brocanth_db::Db>()`, from its own
//! `before_start`, registered after this one.
//!
//! `brocanth-db` is in `Cargo.toml` because this file is here, and through no
//! other route. Delete the registration in `src/plugins/mod.rs` and this
//! file, and the application links no SQL driver.

use std::time::Duration;

use brocanth::plugin_api::{Lifecycle, Plugin, PluginError};
use brocanth::plugin_context::{ContextBuilder, ProvidesContext};
use brocanth::plugin_shutdown::ShutdownCx;
use brocanth_db::{Db, DbConfig, DbError};

/// What this plugin is built from: `[db]`.
///
/// A duration is a whole number of seconds, and 0 turns an optional one
/// off.
///
/// It derives `Deserialize` because this application reads it from
/// `[db]` in a TOML file. Drop the config plugin and the derive goes
/// with it: `Plugin::Config` carries no bounds the core imposes.
#[derive(Debug, serde::Deserialize)]
#[serde(default, deny_unknown_fields)]
pub struct Settings {
    /// `postgres://user:password@host:5432/database`.
    pub url: String,
    /// The most connections the pool opens.
    pub max_connections: u32,
    /// The connections the pool keeps open when idle.
    pub min_connections: u32,
    /// Seconds a statement waits for a connection, opening one included.
    pub acquire_timeout: u64,
    /// Seconds before an idle connection closes. 0 keeps it open.
    pub idle_timeout: u64,
    /// Seconds before the server cancels a statement. 0 is no limit.
    pub statement_timeout: u64,
    /// The schemas for a table name with no schema. Empty is the server's
    /// default.
    pub search_path: Vec<String>,
    /// Every transaction is read-only.
    pub read_only: bool,
    /// The name `pg_stat_activity` shows. Empty sends none.
    pub application_name: String,
    /// A PEM bundle to trust, with `verify-full`. `None` uses the system's
    /// roots.
    pub ca_file: Option<String>,
}

impl Default for Settings {
    fn default() -> Self {
        Self {
            url: "postgres://localhost/development".to_string(),
            max_connections: 10,
            min_connections: 0,
            acquire_timeout: 30,
            idle_timeout: 600,
            statement_timeout: 0,
            search_path: Vec::new(),
            read_only: false,
            application_name: "my-blog".to_string(),
            ca_file: None,
        }
    }
}

impl Settings {
    /// The pool's configuration, from these settings.
    pub fn config(&self) -> DbConfig {
        let seconds = |secs: u64| (secs > 0).then(|| Duration::from_secs(secs));
        let mut config = DbConfig::new(&self.url);
        config.max_connections = self.max_connections;
        config.min_connections = self.min_connections;
        config.acquire_timeout = Duration::from_secs(self.acquire_timeout);
        config.idle_timeout = seconds(self.idle_timeout);
        config.statement_timeout = seconds(self.statement_timeout);
        config.search_path = self.search_path.clone();
        config.read_only = self.read_only;
        config.application_name =
            Some(self.application_name.clone()).filter(|name| !name.is_empty());
        config.ca_file = self.ca_file.clone().map(Into::into);
        config
    }
}

pub struct Database {
    config: DbConfig,
    /// Open from `before_start` until `shutdown`.
    db: Option<Db>,
}

impl Plugin for Database {
    type Config = Settings;
    type Provides = ProvidesContext<Db>;
    const NAME: &'static str = "db";

    fn new(settings: Settings) -> Self {
        Self {
            config: settings.config(),
            db: None,
        }
    }

    fn configure(&mut self, _lc: &mut Lifecycle<'_>) -> Result<(), PluginError> {
        if self.config.url.is_empty() {
            return Err(PluginError::new(
                "[db] url is required; there is no default database\n\
                 help: set `url` under [db] in the config file, or the variable for `db.url`",
            ));
        }
        Ok(())
    }

    fn before_start(&mut self, lc: &mut Lifecycle<'_>) -> Result<(), PluginError> {
        // The hook is synchronous and connecting is not, so the pool opens
        // lazily and nothing here waits. Waiting would hang a current-thread
        // runtime, where this thread is the one that drives the I/O. The
        // pool spawns its upkeep task, so it needs the runtime entered.
        let runtime = tokio::runtime::Handle::try_current().map_err(|e| {
            PluginError::new(format!(
                "[db] needs a Tokio runtime at startup to open its pool ({e})"
            ))
        })?;
        let db = {
            let _entered = runtime.enter();
            Db::connect_lazy(&self.config).map_err(|e| PluginError::new(format!("[db] {e}")))?
        };
        // Ready while `SELECT 1` answers on a pooled connection. `serve`
        // awaits this once before it listens, so a wrong URL, password or
        // certificate stops the start there and not on the first request.
        let probe = db.clone();
        let url = self.config.redacted_url();
        lc.ready_when(move || {
            let probe = probe.clone();
            let url = url.clone();
            async move {
                probe.ping().await.map_err(|e| match e {
                    DbError::Connect { .. } => format!("connecting to {url}: {e}"),
                    other => other.to_string(),
                })
            }
        });
        // For a plugin registered after this one: the model layer wraps the
        // same pool, so there is one set of connections to size.
        lc.services_mut().insert(db.clone());
        self.db = Some(db);
        Ok(())
    }

    fn shutdown(&mut self, sd: &mut ShutdownCx<'_>) -> Result<(), PluginError> {
        let Some(db) = self.db.take() else {
            return Ok(());
        };
        // Without a runtime there is nothing to wait on: the connections
        // close when the pool drops.
        let Ok(runtime) = tokio::runtime::Handle::try_current() else {
            return Ok(());
        };
        let remaining = sd.remaining();
        // `close` waits for the connections in use to come back, so it
        // waits inside what is left of the grace period and no longer.
        let close = async move {
            match remaining {
                Some(left) => tokio::time::timeout(left, db.close()).await.is_ok(),
                None => {
                    db.close().await;
                    true
                }
            }
        };
        // On a current-thread runtime this thread drives the I/O, so a wait
        // here never ends. The close runs as a task, under the same limit.
        if runtime.runtime_flavor() == tokio::runtime::RuntimeFlavor::CurrentThread {
            runtime.spawn(close);
            return Ok(());
        }
        // On a multi-thread runtime the workers drive it, and a scoped
        // thread waits without blocking one of them.
        std::thread::scope(|scope| {
            scope
                .spawn(|| runtime.block_on(close))
                .join()
                .map_err(|_| PluginError::new("[db] the closing thread panicked"))
        })?
        .then_some(())
        .ok_or_else(|| PluginError::new("[db] connections were still in use at the deadline"))
    }

    fn provide(&self, cx: &mut ContextBuilder) {
        // `before_start` runs before the server is handed the application,
        // so the pool is here by the time a request builds a context.
        if let Some(db) = &self.db {
            cx.insert(db.clone());
        }
    }
}
===== src/plugins/errors/mod.rs =====
//! Written by `brocanth`. What a failure becomes.
//!
//! `pages!("src/pages", errors)` wraps every page in [`catching`]. A page
//! that returns an error reaches [`caught`] with the error pages nearest its
//! file: `not-found.rsx` for a 404, `error.rsx` for the rest, each inside
//! the layouts above it. A path no route matched reaches it too, through
//! `pages::not_found`, which `src/app.rs` calls.
//!
//! A 5xx is logged and told to every reporter. A debug build shows the
//! cause on a page of its own; a release build never does.

use std::sync::Arc;

use brocanth_errors::{Origin, Report, Reporters};

use crate::pages::{Ctx, Error, Handler, Response};

pub use brocanth_errors::Caught;

/// An error page: `not-found.rsx` or `error.rsx`, inside its layouts.
/// `pages!` writes one function per file.
pub type ErrorPage = fn(&mut Ctx, &Caught) -> Result<Response, Error>;

/// A page whose failure goes to [`caught`], with the error pages nearest
/// its file. `pages!` wraps every page in it.
pub fn catching(
    handler: Handler,
    not_found: Option<ErrorPage>,
    failed: Option<ErrorPage>,
) -> Handler {
    // Shared, because the future a request gets must not borrow the table.
    let handler = Arc::new(handler);
    Box::new(move |ctx| {
        let handler = Arc::clone(&handler);
        Box::pin(async move {
            let answered = (handler.as_ref())(&mut *ctx).await;
            match answered {
                Ok(response) => Ok(response),
                Err(error) => caught(ctx, error, not_found, failed),
            }
        })
    })
}

/// What a failure becomes.
///
/// An error that names its own `content-type` is for a program, and so is
/// a client that does not read HTML: both keep the plain answer, which
/// `Err` hands back to `src/app.rs`. Every other failure answers a page
/// with the error's status and headers.
pub fn caught(
    ctx: &mut Ctx,
    error: Error,
    not_found: Option<ErrorPage>,
    failed: Option<ErrorPage>,
) -> Result<Response, Error> {
    let reporters = ctx.provided::<Reporters, _>().cloned();
    if error.status >= 500 {
        if let Some(reporters) = &reporters {
            let method = ctx.verb.to_string();
            reporters.report(&Report {
                status: error.status,
                message: &error.message,
                method: &method,
                path: &ctx.path,
                origin: Origin::Page,
            });
        }
    }
    let typed = error
        .headers
        .iter()
        .any(|(name, _)| name.eq_ignore_ascii_case("content-type"));
    if typed || !brocanth_errors::accepts_html(ctx.header("accept")) {
        return Err(error);
    }

    let caught = Caught::new(error.status, &error.message, &ctx.path);
    if error.status >= 500 {
        let (status, message) = (error.status, &error.message);
        crate::plugins::say!(error, "error {status}: {message}"; status, %message);
        // A release binary never shows the cause, whatever the setting.
        if cfg!(debug_assertions) && reporters.as_ref().is_some_and(Reporters::details) {
            let method = ctx.verb.to_string();
            let page =
                brocanth_errors::development_page(&caught, &error.message, &method, ctx.headers());
            return Ok(answer(page, error));
        }
    }

    let page = if caught.is_not_found() {
        not_found
    } else {
        failed
    };
    let body = match page.map(|page| page(ctx, &caught)) {
        Some(Ok(rendered)) => rendered.body,
        Some(Err(broken)) => {
            // The error page failed too. The plain page cannot.
            let message = broken.message;
            crate::plugins::say!(error, "the error page failed: {message}"; %message);
            brocanth_errors::plain_page(&caught)
        }
        None => brocanth_errors::plain_page(&caught),
    };
    Ok(answer(body, error))
}

/// A page with the error's status, and the headers it carried: a
/// `www-authenticate` for a 401, a `retry-after` for a 429.
fn answer(body: String, error: Error) -> Response {
    let mut response = Response::new(error.status, body);
    response.headers = error.headers;
    response
}

#[cfg(test)]
mod tests {
    use std::sync::{Arc, Mutex};

    use brocanth::routing::Verb;
    use brocanth_errors::{Report, Reporter, Reporters};
    use brocanth_server::{Request, Response};

    /// A path no route answers, and one whose page fails with a 500.
    const MISSING: &str = "/en/nowhere";
    const BROKEN: &str = "/en/users/x";

    fn body(response: &Response) -> String {
        let bytes = response.body.bytes().expect("a whole body");
        String::from_utf8(bytes.to_vec()).expect("utf-8")
    }

    #[tokio::test]
    async fn a_path_no_route_matches_answers_an_html_404() {
        let app = crate::app::build().expect("the application builds");
        let request = Request::new(Verb::Get, MISSING).with_header("accept", "text/html");
        let response = crate::app::handle(&app, request).await;
        assert_eq!(response.status, 404);
        let body = body(&response);
        assert!(body.contains("Page not found"), "{body}");
    }

    #[tokio::test]
    async fn a_client_that_reads_json_keeps_the_plain_answer() {
        let app = crate::app::build().expect("the application builds");
        let request = Request::new(Verb::Get, MISSING).with_header("accept", "application/json");
        let response = crate::app::handle(&app, request).await;
        assert_eq!(response.status, 404);
        assert_eq!(body(&response), "Not Found");
    }

    struct Heard(Arc<Mutex<Vec<String>>>);

    impl Reporter for Heard {
        fn report(&self, report: &Report<'_>) {
            if let Ok(mut heard) = self.0.lock() {
                heard.push(format!("{} {}", report.status, report.path));
            }
        }
    }

    #[tokio::test]
    async fn a_failure_is_reported_and_shows_its_cause_only_in_a_debug_build() {
        let app = crate::app::build().expect("the application builds");
        let heard = Arc::new(Mutex::new(Vec::new()));
        let cx = app.core.context();
        let reporters: &Reporters = cx.get();
        reporters.add(Heard(Arc::clone(&heard)));

        // `/users/{id}` parses its parameter, so this fails with a 500. The
        // reporter hears the path the page answered.
        let request = Request::new(Verb::Get, BROKEN).with_header("accept", "text/html");
        let response = crate::app::handle(&app, request).await;
        assert_eq!(response.status, 500);
        assert_eq!(*heard.lock().expect("lock"), ["500 /users/x"]);
        let cause = "invalid digit found in string";
        assert_eq!(
            body(&response).contains(cause),
            cfg!(debug_assertions),
            "{}",
            body(&response)
        );
    }
}
===== src/plugins/flags/mod.rs =====
//! Feature flags: the list this application reads, and the call a page
//! makes.
//!
//! Written once by `brocanth create`. This file is yours.
//!
//! Declare a flag here as a constant, add it to [`ALL`], and read it in a
//! page with [`enabled`]:
//!
//! ```ignore
//! if crate::plugins::flags::enabled(ctx, crate::plugins::flags::NEW_CHECKOUT) {
//!     // the new flow
//! }
//! ```
//!
//! The constant, not a string: a misspelt flag does not compile. A flag
//! with no stored rule is off, so code can ship before anybody turns it on.
//!
//! The rules are rows in the `brocanth_flags` table of the `[db]` database.
//! The plugin reads them before the server listens and again every
//! `refresh_secs`, so a page never waits on the database for a flag. With
//! the `admin` module, the tab at `/admin/flags` turns each flag on for
//! everyone, for a percentage of signed-in users, or for a list of users
//! and groups.

use brocanth_flags::{Actor, Flag};

use crate::pages::Ctx;

/// The plugin, from the library. `[flags]` configures it.
pub use brocanth_flags::FlagsPlugin as Flags;

/// An example. Rename it, or delete it and its line in [`ALL`].
pub const NEW_CHECKOUT: Flag = Flag::new("new_checkout", "The checkout flow from the redesign.");

/// Every flag this application reads. The admin tab lists these, so a flag
/// can be turned on before anybody stored a rule for it.
#[allow(dead_code)]
pub const ALL: &[Flag] = &[NEW_CHECKOUT];

/// Whether `flag` is on for whoever made this request.
///
/// Off on a `Ctx` built by hand, which has no plugin behind it.
