#[allow(dead_code)]
pub fn enabled(ctx: &Ctx, flag: Flag) -> bool {
    ctx.provided::<brocanth_flags::Flags, _>()
        .is_some_and(|flags| flags.enabled(flag, &actor(ctx)))
}

/// Who is asking, as the flags see it.
///
/// Anonymous until this reads your user off the request, and an anonymous
/// request sees only a flag that is on for everyone. With a user on the
/// context, this is two lines:
///
/// ```ignore
/// ctx.provided::<CurrentUser, _>()
///     .map_or_else(Actor::anonymous, |user| Actor::new(user.id.to_string()).in_group("staff"))
/// ```
fn actor(_ctx: &Ctx) -> Actor {
    Actor::anonymous()
}
===== src/plugins/i18n/mod.rs =====
//! Written by `brocanth`. The catalogs in `locales/`, compiled by
//! `build.rs`, and the locale of a request.
//!
//! A page reads the locale and calls a key:
//!
//! ```ignore
//! use crate::plugins::i18n::{self, t};
//!
//! let locale = i18n::locale(ctx);
//! let title = t::welcome(locale);
//! ```

use brocanth_i18n::{Locales, Negotiated};

use crate::pages::Ctx;

// A key that no code calls yet is not a warning: a new project calls
// none. Remove the `allow` lines here to find what nothing uses.
#[allow(dead_code)]
mod catalog {
    include!(concat!(env!("OUT_DIR"), "/i18n.rs"));
}

#[allow(unused_imports)]
pub use catalog::t;
pub use catalog::Locale;

/// The plugin `src/plugins/mod.rs` registers. It negotiates in a request
/// hook, before the page runs.
pub type I18n = brocanth_i18n::I18nHook<Locale>;

/// The locale the `i18n` hook chose for this request.
///
/// The hook reads `?locale=`, then the cookie, then `Accept-Language`,
/// then the `default` setting, and the plugin puts the result on the
/// context: this compiles only while the plugin is registered. The
/// fallback is only for a `Ctx` built by hand in a test.
#[allow(dead_code)]
pub fn locale(ctx: &Ctx) -> Locale {
    ctx.provided::<Negotiated<Locale>, _>()
        .map_or(Locale::DEFAULT, |negotiated| negotiated.locale)
}

#[cfg(test)]
mod tests {
    use brocanth::routing::Verb;
    use brocanth_server::Request;

    use super::{t, Locale};

    async fn get(request: Request) -> brocanth_server::Response {
        let app = crate::app::build().expect("the application builds");
        crate::app::handle(&app, request).await
    }

    #[tokio::test]
    async fn a_page_answers_in_the_language_the_browser_asks_for() {
        let french = Request::new(Verb::Get, "/").with_header("accept-language", "fr-CH");
        let response = get(french).await;
        assert_eq!(response.header("content-language"), Some("fr"));
        let response = get(Request::new(Verb::Get, "/")).await;
        assert_eq!(response.header("content-language"), Some("en"));
    }

    #[tokio::test]
    async fn a_link_with_a_locale_wins_and_is_remembered() {
        let mut request = Request::new(Verb::Get, "/").with_header("accept-language", "en");
        request.query = "locale=fr".to_string();
        let response = get(request).await;
        assert_eq!(response.header("content-language"), Some("fr"));
        // Another module (csrf) may set a cookie of its own first.
        let cookies: Vec<&str> = response
            .headers()
            .filter(|(name, _)| name.eq_ignore_ascii_case("set-cookie"))
            .map(|(_, value)| value)
            .collect();
        assert!(
            cookies.iter().any(|c| c.starts_with("locale=fr;")),
            "{cookies:?}"
        );
    }

    #[test]
    fn every_locale_has_the_keys_with_its_own_plural_rules() {
        assert_eq!(t::welcome(Locale::Fr), "Bienvenue");
        assert_eq!(t::items(Locale::En, 0), "No items");
        assert_eq!(t::items(Locale::En, 1), "1 item");
        assert_eq!(t::items(Locale::Fr, 1), "1 article");
        assert_eq!(t::greeting(Locale::En, "Ada"), "Hello, Ada!");
    }
}
===== src/plugins/jobs/mod.rs =====
//! Background jobs, as this application registers them.
//!
//! A job is a struct that serialises, plus one `perform`. Enqueuing takes the
//! struct itself, so a payload a job cannot accept is a struct-literal error
//! rather than a decode failure on a worker at three in the morning.
//!
//! `brocanth-jobs` is in `Cargo.toml` because this file is here, and through
//! no other route. Delete the registration in `src/plugins/mod.rs` and
//! delete this file, and the application runs no jobs and links nothing to
//! run them with.
//!
//! # Enqueuing, and what does not work yet
//!
//! This plugin publishes the `Queue` on the per-request context, and a
//! handler reaches it through `Ctx`:
//!
//! ```ignore
//! let queue = ctx.provided::<Queue, _>().expect("a dispatched request");
//! queue.enqueue(Welcome { user_id: 7 }).await?;
//! ```
//!
//! `provided` compiles only because this plugin is registered. Drop it and
//! the line stops building and names `Queue`, which is the whole reason
//! the provider list lives in the application's type.
//!
//! Anything else holding the `Queue` can enqueue too -- `src/app.rs`, a
//! command under `src/commands/`:
//!
//! ```ignore
//! queue.enqueue(Welcome { user_id: 7 }).await?;
//! ```
//!
//! The note here used to say a handler could not reach it, because `Ctx`
//! carried no context. It does now.
//!
//! # Where job types go
//!
//! `Welcome` is here so that this file compiles into something that runs.
//! Move it, and whatever follows it, into `src/jobs/` once there is more
//! than one -- the only thing that must stay here is the `register` call.
//!
//! **Register every job type before anything enqueues it.** A worker claims
//! only the names it has registered, so a job whose name no worker knows
//! waits on its queue until a worker that knows it starts. During a rolling
//! deploy that means the old workers leave it alone, so ship the
//! registration before you ship the first enqueue.
//!
//! # Where the jobs live
//!
//! In this process's memory (`jobs-memory`), for development and tests.
//! A restart loses every job. `brocanth remove jobs-memory` moves the queue
//! to Postgres.
//!
//! The crate also ships `JobsPlugin`, and this is not that. `JobsPlugin` is
//! handed a queue that is already open, which is what an application that
//! builds its queue in `main` wants. This one builds it from the settings.

use brocanth::plugin_api::{Lifecycle, Plugin, PluginError};
use brocanth::plugin_context::{ContextBuilder, ProvidesContext};
use brocanth::plugin_shutdown::ShutdownCx;
use brocanth_jobs::{Job, JobContext, JobRegistry, Queue, Worker, WorkerHandle};
use serde::{Deserialize, Serialize};

// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
// A job
// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

/// Says hello, slowly, somewhere that is not a request.
#[derive(Serialize, Deserialize)]
pub struct Welcome {
    /// Who to welcome.
    pub user_id: i64,
}

impl Job for Welcome {
    const NAME: &'static str = "welcome";
    /// Which queue it waits on. The worker below claims from the queues the
    /// config names, so a job on a queue nobody claims waits for ever.
    const QUEUE: &'static str = "default";
    type Error = std::io::Error;

    async fn perform(&self, _cx: &JobContext) -> Result<(), Self::Error> {
        println!("welcome, user {}", self.user_id);
        Ok(())
    }
}

/// Every job type this application can run.
///
/// The one list that has to be kept: a worker claims a row by name and needs
/// the type to decode it. Adding a job is a type above and a line here.
fn registry() -> JobRegistry {
    let mut registry = JobRegistry::new();
    registry.register::<Welcome>();
    registry
}

// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
// The plugin
// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

/// What this plugin is built from.
///
/// It derives `Deserialize` because this application reads it from
/// `[jobs]` in a TOML file. Drop the config plugin and the derive goes
/// with it: `Plugin::Config` carries no bounds the core imposes.
#[derive(Debug, serde::Deserialize)]
#[serde(default, deny_unknown_fields)]
pub struct Settings {
    /// Run a worker in this process, as well as enqueuing from it.
    pub worker: bool,
    /// Which queues that worker claims from.
    pub queues: Vec<String>,
    /// How many jobs it runs at once.
    pub concurrency: usize,
}

impl Default for Settings {
    fn default() -> Self {
        Self {
            worker: true,
            queues: vec!["default".to_string()],
            concurrency: 5,
        }
    }
}

pub struct Jobs {
    settings: Settings,
    /// Opened at `before_start` and not before, so this is an `Option` for
    /// exactly the length of the build.
    queue: Option<Queue>,
    /// Kept so `shutdown` can wait for the worker to give back what it
    /// holds.
    worker: Option<WorkerHandle>,
}

/// What the worker keeps of the grace period to give back the jobs that did
/// not finish, so they are on the queue before the process exits.
const GIVE_BACK: std::time::Duration = std::time::Duration::from_secs(2);

impl Plugin for Jobs {
    type Config = Settings;
    type Provides = ProvidesContext<Queue>;
    const NAME: &'static str = "jobs";

    fn new(config: Settings) -> Self {
        Self {
            settings: config,
            queue: None,
            worker: None,
        }
    }

    fn configure(&mut self, _lc: &mut Lifecycle<'_>) -> Result<(), PluginError> {
        if self.settings.worker && self.settings.queues.is_empty() {
            return Err(PluginError::new(
                "[jobs] worker is true and queues is empty, so the worker would claim \
                 nothing; name a queue or set worker = false",
            ));
        }
        Ok(())
    }

    fn before_start(&mut self, lc: &mut Lifecycle<'_>) -> Result<(), PluginError> {
        // In this process's memory (`jobs-memory`): for development and
        // tests only. A restart loses every job, and `brocanth check
        // --deploy` refuses it.
        let queue = Queue::memory();

        // Ready while the queue answers, which in memory is always.
        let probe = queue.clone();
        lc.ready_when(move || {
            let probe = probe.clone();
            async move { probe.ping().await.map_err(|e| e.to_string()) }
        });

        // So the admin's jobs tab, which mounts before this runs, finds it.
        queue.install();

        // In the services too, for a plugin that runs a worker of its own on
        // this queue: the mail plugin sends `deliver_later` mail that way.
        // Every `before_start` runs before any `start`, so it finds it there.
        lc.services_mut().insert(queue.clone());
        self.queue = Some(queue);
        Ok(())
    }

    fn start(&mut self, lc: &mut Lifecycle<'_>) -> Result<(), PluginError> {
        // Here and not in `before_start`: `serve` runs this after its
        // startup gate, so the tables exist, and inside the runtime, so the
        // worker has somewhere to spawn. A build that never serves -- a test,
        // a command -- starts no worker. On Postgres an enqueue wakes the
        // worker (`LISTEN/NOTIFY`) rather than a poll.
        let (Some(queue), true) = (&self.queue, self.settings.worker) else {
            return Ok(());
        };
        let worker = Worker::builder(queue)
            .registry(registry())
            .queues(self.settings.queues.clone())
            .concurrency(self.settings.concurrency)
            .start()
            .map_err(|e| PluginError::new(format!("[jobs] starting the worker: {e}")))?;
        // At SIGTERM `serve` marks the application not ready at once, and
        // the worker stops claiming then, not after the listener drained.
        // The jobs already running have the grace period, less the time it
        // takes to give back the ones that do not finish: those go back to
        // the queue with their attempt, and another pod runs them.
        let grace = crate::plugins::shutdown_grace().map_err(PluginError::new)?;
        worker.stop_when(lc.readiness().draining(), grace.saturating_sub(GIVE_BACK));
        self.worker = Some(worker);
        Ok(())
    }

    fn begin_shutdown(&mut self, sd: &mut ShutdownCx<'_>) -> Result<(), PluginError> {
        // Nothing new from here, if the drain above has not stopped it yet.
        if let Some(worker) = &self.worker {
            let grace = sd.remaining().unwrap_or(crate::app::SHUTDOWN_GRACE);
            worker.stop(grace.saturating_sub(GIVE_BACK));
        }
        Ok(())
    }

    fn shutdown(&mut self, sd: &mut ShutdownCx<'_>) -> Result<(), PluginError> {
        let Some(worker) = self.worker.take() else {
            return Ok(());
        };
        let Ok(runtime) = tokio::runtime::Handle::try_current() else {
            return Ok(());
        };
        // On a current-thread runtime this thread drives the I/O, so a wait
        // here never ends. The worker stops by itself, by its deadline.
        if runtime.runtime_flavor() == tokio::runtime::RuntimeFlavor::CurrentThread {
            return Ok(());
        }
        // On a multi-thread runtime the workers drive it, and a scoped
        // thread waits without blocking one of them, inside what is left.
        let remaining = sd.remaining();
        let finished = async move {
            match remaining {
                Some(left) => tokio::time::timeout(left, worker.finished()).await.ok(),
                None => Some(worker.finished().await),
            }
        };
        let ended = std::thread::scope(|scope| {
            scope
                .spawn(|| runtime.block_on(finished))
                .join()
                .map_err(|_| PluginError::new("[jobs] the stopping thread panicked"))
        })?;
        match ended {
            Some(Ok(report)) => {
                let (finished, returned) = (report.finished, report.returned);
                crate::plugins::say!(
                    info,
                    "[jobs] worker stopped: {finished} finished, {returned} back on the queue";
                    finished,
                    returned
                );
                Ok(())
            }
            Some(Err(e)) => Err(PluginError::new(format!("[jobs] stopping the worker: {e}"))),
            None => Err(PluginError::new(
                "[jobs] the worker was still giving jobs back at the deadline",
            )),
        }
    }

    fn provide(&self, cx: &mut ContextBuilder) {
        // `before_start` runs before the server is handed the application,
        // so the queue is here by the time any request builds a context.
        if let Some(queue) = &self.queue {
            cx.insert(queue.clone());
        }
    }
}
===== src/plugins/mail/mod.rs =====
//! Mail, as this application sends it.
//!
//! A page reads the `Outbox` from the context and builds a message from it,
//! so `From` is always the configured sender:
//!
//! ```ignore
//! let outbox = ctx.provided::<Outbox, _>().expect("a dispatched request");
//! let message = outbox
//!     .message()
//!     .to(brocanth_mail::Address::parse("person@example.test")?)
//!     .subject("Welcome")
//!     .text("Hello")
//!     .build()?;
//! outbox.deliver_later(message).await?;
//! ```
//!
//! # Where mail goes
//!
//! `[mail] transport` decides: `log` writes each message to the log, `folder`
//! writes each one as an `.eml` file into `[mail] folder`, and `smtp` sends it
//! to `[mail] smtp_host`. Only `smtp` delivers, so a development file copied
//! to production sends nothing to real people, and the start says so.
//!
//! The SMTP password has no flag: every user on the host can read a process's
//! arguments. Set `$<APP>_MAIL__SMTP_PASSWORD`.
//!
//! # Later, on a queue
//!
//! With `jobs`, `deliver_later` puts the message on the `mail` queue and
//! returns. A worker that this plugin starts sends it, and retries it
//! when the relay refuses. It is a second worker, beside the one in
//! `src/plugins/jobs/mod.rs`, so a slow relay holds up no other job.
//! `[mail] worker = false` turns it off in a process that only serves.
use std::sync::{Arc, OnceLock};

use brocanth::plugin_api::{Lifecycle, Plugin, PluginError};
use brocanth::plugin_context::{ContextBuilder, ContextItem, ProvidesContext};
use brocanth::plugin_shutdown::ShutdownCx;
use brocanth_jobs::{Job, JobContext, Queue, Worker, WorkerHandle};
use brocanth_mail::{
    Address, Folder, Log, Mail, MailError, Message, MessageBuilder, Security, Smtp, SmtpConfig,
};
use serde::{Deserialize, Serialize};

/// What this plugin is built from.
///
/// It derives `Deserialize` because this application reads it from
/// `[mail]` in a TOML file. Drop the config plugin and the derive goes
/// with it: `Plugin::Config` carries no bounds the core imposes.
#[derive(Debug, serde::Deserialize)]
#[serde(default, deny_unknown_fields)]
pub struct Settings {
    /// `smtp`, `log` or `folder`.
    pub transport: String,
    /// The address every message comes from.
    pub from: String,
    /// The name shown beside it. Empty for none.
    pub from_name: String,
    /// Where `folder` writes.
    pub folder: String,
    /// The relay.
    pub smtp_host: String,
    /// The relay's port.
    pub smtp_port: u16,
    /// `tls`, `starttls`, or `plain` for a local catcher only.
    pub smtp_security: String,
    /// The relay's user name. Empty for none.
    pub smtp_username: String,
    /// The relay's password. Empty for none. A secret: no flag sets it.
    pub smtp_password: String,
    /// Run the worker that sends queued mail in this process.
    pub worker: bool,
}

impl Default for Settings {
    fn default() -> Self {
        Self {
            transport: "log".to_string(),
            from: "no-reply@example.com".to_string(),
            from_name: "My Blog".to_string(),
            folder: "tmp/mail".to_string(),
            smtp_host: "localhost".to_string(),
            smtp_port: 587,
            smtp_security: "starttls".to_string(),
            smtp_username: String::new(),
            smtp_password: String::new(),
            worker: true,
        }
    }
}

/// What a page sends mail through.
#[derive(Clone)]
pub struct Outbox {
    mail: Mail,
    transport: String,
    /// The queue, once the jobs plugin opened it. Empty in a context built
    /// before `before_start`, which sends at once.
    queue: Arc<OnceLock<Queue>>,
}

impl Outbox {
    /// A message from the configured sender.
    pub fn message(&self) -> MessageBuilder {
        self.mail.message()
    }

    /// Sends the message now, and waits for the transport to take it.
    ///
    /// `allow(dead_code)`: it is here for the first page that sends at once,
    /// and a project that only queues mail has none.
    #[allow(dead_code)]
    pub async fn send(&self, message: &Message) -> Result<(), MailError> {
        self.mail.send(message).await
    }

    /// Sends the message from a worker, after this request.
    ///
    /// The message goes on the `mail` queue, and the worker retries a send
    /// the relay refused. Before the queue is open -- a context built by
    /// hand -- it sends at once.
    pub async fn deliver_later(&self, message: Message) -> Result<(), DeliverError> {
        match self.queue.get() {
            Some(queue) => {
                queue.enqueue(DeliverMail { message }).await?;
                Ok(())
            }
            None => Ok(self.mail.send(&message).await?),
        }
    }

    /// Where mail goes: `smtp`, `log` or `folder`.
    #[allow(dead_code)]
    pub fn transport(&self) -> &str {
        &self.transport
    }
}

impl ContextItem for Outbox {}

/// What `deliver_later` can fail with: the queue refused the row, or there
/// was no queue and the relay refused the message.
#[derive(Debug)]
pub enum DeliverError {
    Queue(brocanth_jobs::Error),
    Mail(MailError),
}

impl std::fmt::Display for DeliverError {
    fn fmt(&self, f: &mut std::fmt::Formatter<'_>) -> std::fmt::Result {
        match self {
            Self::Queue(e) => write!(f, "the mail was not queued: {e}"),
            Self::Mail(e) => e.fmt(f),
        }
    }
}

impl std::error::Error for DeliverError {}

impl From<brocanth_jobs::Error> for DeliverError {
    fn from(e: brocanth_jobs::Error) -> Self {
        Self::Queue(e)
    }
}

impl From<MailError> for DeliverError {
    fn from(e: MailError) -> Self {
        Self::Mail(e)
    }
}

/// The mail the worker sends, set once when the plugin is built.
static SENDER: OnceLock<Mail> = OnceLock::new();

/// One message, sent from the `mail` queue.
///
/// The message is checked again when the worker reads it back: a row that
/// was edited in the table cannot carry a newline into a header.
#[derive(Serialize, Deserialize)]
pub struct DeliverMail {
    pub message: Message,
}

impl Job for DeliverMail {
    const NAME: &'static str = "mail.deliver";
    const QUEUE: &'static str = "mail";
    type Error = MailError;

    async fn perform(&self, _cx: &JobContext) -> Result<(), Self::Error> {
        let mail = SENDER
            .get()
            .ok_or_else(|| MailError::transport("the mail plugin was not built in this process"))?;
        mail.send(&self.message).await
    }
}

pub struct MailPlugin {
    settings: Settings,
    /// Built at `before_start`, so this is an `Option` for the length of the
    /// build.
    outbox: Option<Outbox>,
    /// The worker that sends queued mail, kept to stop it.
    worker: Option<WorkerHandle>,
}

impl MailPlugin {
    /// `[mail] from`, with `[mail] from_name` beside it.
    fn sender(&self) -> Result<Address, PluginError> {
        let s = &self.settings;
        if s.from_name.is_empty() {
            Address::parse(&s.from)
        } else {
            Address::named(&s.from_name, &s.from)
        }
        .map_err(|e| PluginError::new(format!("[mail] from: {e}\nhelp: set a plain address")))
    }

    /// The backend `[mail] transport` names.
    fn mailer(&self) -> Result<Arc<dyn brocanth_mail::Mailer>, PluginError> {
        let s = &self.settings;
        Ok(match s.transport.as_str() {
            "log" => Arc::new(Log::new(|message: &Message| {
                let to: Vec<&str> = message.to().iter().map(Address::address).collect();
                let to = to.join(", ");
                let subject = message.subject().as_str();
                let text = message.text();
                crate::plugins::say!(info, "mail to {to}: {subject}\n{text}"; %to, %subject);
            })),
            "folder" => Arc::new(Folder::new(&s.folder)),
            "smtp" => {
                let security = match s.smtp_security.as_str() {
                    "tls" => Security::ImplicitTls,
                    "starttls" => Security::StartTls,
                    "plain" => Security::PlaintextForLocalCatcherOnly,
                    other => {
                        return Err(PluginError::new(format!(
                            "[mail] smtp_security is `{other}`\n\
                             help: set it to `tls`, `starttls` or `plain`"
                        )))
                    }
                };
                let mut config = SmtpConfig::new(&s.smtp_host)
                    .with_security(security)
                    .with_port(s.smtp_port);
                if !s.smtp_username.is_empty() {
                    config = config.with_credentials(&s.smtp_username, &s.smtp_password);
                }
                Arc::new(
                    Smtp::connect(&config).map_err(|e| PluginError::new(format!("[mail] {e}")))?,
                )
            }
            other => {
                return Err(PluginError::new(format!(
                    "[mail] transport is `{other}`\nhelp: set it to `smtp`, `log` or `folder`"
                )))
            }
        })
    }
}

impl Plugin for MailPlugin {
    type Config = Settings;
    type Provides = ProvidesContext<Outbox>;
    const NAME: &'static str = "mail";

    fn new(config: Settings) -> Self {
        Self {
            settings: config,
            outbox: None,
            worker: None,
        }
    }

    fn before_start(&mut self, _lc: &mut Lifecycle<'_>) -> Result<(), PluginError> {
        let from = self.sender()?;
        // The SMTP pool spawns its upkeep on the runtime, so it needs the
        // runtime entered. It opens no connection here.
        let mailer = match tokio::runtime::Handle::try_current() {
            Ok(runtime) => {
                let _entered = runtime.enter();
                self.mailer()?
            }
            Err(_) => self.mailer()?,
        };
        if self.settings.transport != "smtp" {
            let transport = &self.settings.transport;
            crate::plugins::say!(
                warn,
                "[mail] transport is {transport}: nothing is delivered";
                %transport
            );
        }
        let mail = Mail::new(mailer, from);
        // The job reads the mail from here: a worker has no request
        // context. The first application built in this process decides.
        let _ = SENDER.set(mail.clone());
        self.outbox = Some(Outbox {
            mail,
            transport: self.settings.transport.clone(),
            queue: Arc::default(),
        });
        Ok(())
    }

    fn start(&mut self, lc: &mut Lifecycle<'_>) -> Result<(), PluginError> {
        // The jobs plugin publishes its queue in `before_start`, and every
        // `before_start` runs before any `start`.
        let Some(queue) = lc.services().get::<Queue>().cloned() else {
            return Err(PluginError::new(
                "[mail] the jobs plugin published no queue\n\
                 help: register the jobs plugin before the mail plugin",
            ));
        };
        if let Some(outbox) = &self.outbox {
            let _ = outbox.queue.set(queue.clone());
        }
        if !self.settings.worker {
            return Ok(());
        }
        let worker = Worker::builder(&queue)
            .register::<DeliverMail>()
            .queues(vec![DeliverMail::QUEUE.to_string()])
            .concurrency(2)
            .start()
            .map_err(|e| PluginError::new(format!("[mail] starting the worker: {e}")))?;
        let grace = crate::plugins::shutdown_grace().map_err(PluginError::new)?;
        worker.stop_when(lc.readiness().draining(), grace);
        self.worker = Some(worker);
        Ok(())
    }

    fn begin_shutdown(&mut self, sd: &mut ShutdownCx<'_>) -> Result<(), PluginError> {
        // A message that does not go out in time goes back on the queue, and
        // another process sends it.
        if let Some(worker) = &self.worker {
            worker.stop(sd.remaining().unwrap_or(crate::app::SHUTDOWN_GRACE));
        }
        Ok(())
    }

    fn provide(&self, cx: &mut ContextBuilder) {
        // `before_start` runs before the server is handed the application, so
        // the outbox is here by the time any request builds a context.
        if let Some(outbox) = &self.outbox {
            cx.insert(outbox.clone());
        }
    }
}

#[cfg(test)]
mod tests {
    use super::*;

    fn plugin(edit: impl FnOnce(&mut Settings)) -> MailPlugin {
        let mut settings = Settings::default();
        edit(&mut settings);
        MailPlugin::new(settings)
    }

    fn refusal(result: Result<impl Sized, PluginError>) -> String {
        match result {
            Ok(_) => panic!("a wrong setting was taken"),
            Err(e) => e.to_string(),
        }
    }

    /// The defaults are what a new project starts with: a sender that
    /// does not parse stops every start.
    #[tokio::test]
    async fn the_default_settings_start() {
        let mail = plugin(|_| {});
        if let Err(e) = mail.sender() {
            panic!("the default sender: {e}");
        }
        if let Err(e) = mail.mailer() {
            panic!("the default transport: {e}");
        }
    }

    /// Each transport with each security, with and without a user name.
    /// No connection opens: the relay is asked at the first send.
    #[tokio::test]
    async fn every_transport_and_every_security_start() {
        for transport in ["log", "folder", "smtp"] {
            for security in ["tls", "starttls", "plain"] {
                for username in ["", "relay-user"] {
                    let mail = plugin(|s| {
                        s.transport = transport.to_string();
                        s.smtp_security = security.to_string();
                        s.smtp_username = username.to_string();
                        s.smtp_password = "a password".to_string();
                    });
                    if let Err(e) = mail.mailer() {
                        panic!("{transport} with {security}: {e}");
                    }
                }
            }
        }
    }

    #[tokio::test]
    async fn a_wrong_value_stops_the_start_and_says_what_to_set() {
        let transport = refusal(plugin(|s| s.transport = "pigeon".to_string()).mailer());
        assert!(transport.contains("help:"), "{transport}");
        let security = refusal(
            plugin(|s| {
                s.transport = "smtp".to_string();
                s.smtp_security = "ssl".to_string();
            })
            .mailer(),
        );
        assert!(security.contains("help:"), "{security}");
        let from = refusal(plugin(|s| s.from = "no-reply@localhost".to_string()).sender());
        assert!(from.contains("help:"), "{from}");
        let name = plugin(|s| s.from_name = "Shop\r\nBcc: list@example.com".to_string());
        assert!(name.sender().is_err(), "a line break in the sender's name");
    }
}
===== src/plugins/meta/mod.rs =====
//! Written by `brocanth`. What every page says about itself in `<head>`.
//!
//! [`Meta`] holds the `[meta]` settings and puts a `brocanth_meta::Site` on
//! the context. A page sets its own title, description, image or canonical
//! URL with `ctx.meta()`, before it renders:
//!
//! ```ignore
//! ctx.meta().title("About").description("Who writes this site.");
//! html!(ctx, views::about::About { .. })
//! ```
//!
//! A layout that declares `meta: &Head` is given `ctx.head()`: the page's
//! values, and the site's for what the page left out. Its `Display` is the
//! tags, every value escaped.

use brocanth::plugin_api::{Lifecycle, Plugin, PluginError};
use brocanth::plugin_context::{ContextBuilder, ProvidesContext};
use brocanth_meta::Site;

/// What this plugin is built from.
///
/// It derives `Deserialize` because this application reads it from
/// `[meta]` in a TOML file. Drop the config plugin and the derive goes
/// with it: `Plugin::Config` carries no bounds the core imposes.
#[derive(Debug, serde::Deserialize)]
#[serde(default, deny_unknown_fields)]
pub struct Settings {
    /// The site's name: the title of a page with none, and `og:site_name`.
    pub name: String,
    /// How a page's title becomes the document's: `%s` is the page's own.
    pub title_template: Option<String>,
    /// The site's address, scheme and host. A canonical URL needs it.
    pub base_url: Option<String>,
    /// The description of a page that gives none.
    pub description: Option<String>,
    /// The `og:image` of a page that gives none.
    pub image: Option<String>,
    /// The site's account, for `twitter:site`: `@blog`.
    pub twitter: Option<String>,
}

impl Default for Settings {
    fn default() -> Self {
        Self {
            name: "My Blog".to_string(),
            title_template: Some("%s | My Blog".to_string()),
            base_url: None,
            description: None,
            image: None,
            twitter: None,
        }
    }
}

/// Puts the site's metadata on the context, where `ctx.head()` reads it.
pub struct Meta {
    site: Site,
}

impl Plugin for Meta {
    type Config = Settings;
    type Provides = ProvidesContext<Site>;
    const NAME: &'static str = "meta";

    fn new(config: Settings) -> Self {
        let mut site = Site::new(config.name);
        let set = |value: Option<String>| value.filter(|v| !v.is_empty());
        if let Some(template) = set(config.title_template) {
            site = site.title_template(template);
        }
        if let Some(url) = set(config.base_url) {
            site = site.base_url(url);
        }
        if let Some(text) = set(config.description) {
            site = site.description(text);
        }
        if let Some(url) = set(config.image) {
            site = site.image(url);
        }
        if let Some(handle) = set(config.twitter) {
            site = site.twitter(handle);
        }
        Self { site }
    }

    /// A template with no `%s` or a base URL with no scheme stops the
    /// start: every page would carry the wrong tag.
    fn configure(&mut self, _lc: &mut Lifecycle<'_>) -> Result<(), PluginError> {
        self.site
            .check()
            .map_err(|why| PluginError::new(format!("[meta] {why}")))
    }

    fn provide(&self, cx: &mut ContextBuilder) {
        cx.insert(self.site.clone());
    }
}

#[cfg(test)]
mod tests {
    use brocanth::routing::{Params, Verb};

    use super::*;
    use crate::pages::Ctx;

    #[test]
    fn a_page_title_goes_through_the_template() {
        let mut ctx = Ctx::new(Verb::Get, "/about", Params::empty());
        let site = Meta::new(Settings::default()).site;
        ctx.meta().title("About").description("Who writes this.");
        let head = brocanth_meta::Head::new(&site, ctx.hooked::<brocanth_meta::Meta>());
        assert_eq!(head.title(), "About | My Blog");
        assert!(head.to_string().contains("content=\"Who writes this.\""));
    }

    #[test]
    fn a_template_with_no_hole_stops_the_start() {
        let meta = Meta::new(Settings {
            title_template: Some("Same title everywhere".to_string()),
            ..Settings::default()
        });
        let why = meta.site.check().expect_err("refused");
        assert!(why.contains("help:"), "{why}");
    }

    #[tokio::test]
    async fn the_home_page_carries_the_site_title_and_its_tags() {
        let app = crate::app::build().expect("the application builds");
        let request = brocanth_server::Request::new(Verb::Get, "/en");
        let response = crate::app::handle(&app, request).await;
        let body = response.body.bytes().expect("a whole body");
        let body = std::str::from_utf8(body).expect("utf-8");
        assert!(body.contains("<title>My Blog</title>"), "{body}");
        let tag = r#"<link rel="alternate" hreflang="fr" href="/fr">"#;
        assert!(body.contains(tag), "{body}");
    }
}
===== src/plugins/model/mod.rs =====
//! The model layer.
//!
//! The ORM is recommended and absent. SeaORM is the recommendation, it is not a
//! dependency of the core and never will be, and it arrives through
//! `brocanth-model` -- which is in `Cargo.toml` because this file is here and
//! through no other route. An application that stores nothing pays nothing for
//! it: not a crate in the lock file, not a section in the config, not a byte.
//!
//! It opens no pool. The `db` plugin opens one and publishes it as
//! `brocanth_db::Db`; this plugin wraps that pool in SeaORM's connection, so an
//! entity query and a sea-query statement share connections, limits and
//! session settings. A page reads it with `ctx.orm()`.
//!
//! The entity modules go under `src/models/`, and you write them by hand: a
//! struct deriving `DeriveEntityModel` with `#[brocanth_model::validates(..)]`
//! above it, as `brocanth_model`'s own documentation sets out. There is no
//! `brocanth generate model` -- the CLI scaffolds a project and adds a module
//! to one, and a generator for a single entity is not written yet.

use brocanth::plugin_api::{Lifecycle, Plugin, PluginError};
use brocanth::plugin_context::{ContextBuilder, ProvidesContext};
use brocanth_model::{ModelRegistry, Orm};

/// What this plugin is built from: nothing. The URL and the pool are
/// `[db]`'s.
///
/// It derives `Deserialize` because this application reads it from
/// `[model]` in a TOML file. Drop the config plugin and the derive goes
/// with it: `Plugin::Config` carries no bounds the core imposes.
#[derive(Debug, Default, serde::Deserialize)]
#[serde(default, deny_unknown_fields)]
pub struct Settings {}

pub struct Model {
    /// Set in `before_start`, once the `db` plugin has opened the pool.
    orm: Option<Orm>,
}

impl Plugin for Model {
    type Config = Settings;
    type Provides = ProvidesContext<Orm>;
    const NAME: &'static str = "model";

    fn new(_config: Settings) -> Self {
        Self { orm: None }
    }

    fn before_start(&mut self, lc: &mut Lifecycle<'_>) -> Result<(), PluginError> {
        // The `db` plugin is registered first and opened the pool in its own
        // `before_start`. This wraps it and opens nothing.
        let orm = Orm::from_db(lc.require_service::<brocanth_db::Db>()?);
        // For another plugin: `lc.require_service::<Orm>()`.
        lc.services_mut().insert(orm.clone());
        self.orm = Some(orm);

        // The registry below is what would make a generator and an admin UI
        // tractable: every entity compiled into this binary can be enumerated,
        // with its columns, nullability and keys. It reads an inventory that
        // `DeriveEntityModel` fills, so it counts every model linked in,
        // whoever wrote it. The count is zero until the first entity module
        // exists under `src/models/`, and the line is here so that it is not
        // a surprise when it fills.
        let registry = ModelRegistry::new();
        let entities = registry.names().len();
        crate::plugins::say!(info, "[model] {entities} entities registered"; entities);
        Ok(())
    }

    fn provide(&self, cx: &mut ContextBuilder) {
        // Nothing before `before_start`, so `ctx.orm()` answers an error
        // rather than a connection to nowhere.
        if let Some(orm) = &self.orm {
            cx.insert(orm.clone());
        }
    }
}
===== src/plugins/observability/mod.rs =====
//! Observability, as this application registers it: a JSON or text line per request on stdout, `/up` and `/readyz`, Prometheus text on `/metrics`, an OTLP span per request.
//!
//! The core assumes no log layer and no probes. This file is where the
//! application chose them. Delete the registration and this file, and none
//! of it is compiled. Each piece is its own module: `brocanth add
//! observability-metrics` and the like.
//!
//! `serving.rs` beside this file reads the `Observer` this plugin
//! publishes: `serve` wraps its handler with it, and stops through it.

use std::time::Duration;

use brocanth::plugin_api::{Lifecycle, Plugin, PluginError};
use brocanth::plugin_context::NoContext;
use brocanth::plugin_shutdown::ShutdownCx;
use brocanth_observe::{LogFormat, ObserveConfig, ObservePlugin, Observer};

pub mod serving;

/// What this plugin is built from.
///
/// It derives `Deserialize` because this application reads it from
/// `[observability]` in a TOML file. Drop the config plugin and the derive goes
/// with it: `Plugin::Config` carries no bounds the core imposes.
#[derive(Debug, serde::Deserialize)]
#[serde(default, deny_unknown_fields)]
pub struct Settings {
    /// `json` or `text`. Empty: `json` in production, `text` elsewhere.
    pub format: String,
    /// A level (`info`) or per-target levels (`info,sqlx=warn`). `RUST_LOG`
    /// wins when it is set.
    pub level: String,
    /// Liveness. Empty turns it off.
    pub live_path: String,
    /// Readiness: every plugin's check. Empty turns it off.
    pub ready_path: String,
    /// Seconds to answer "not ready" after SIGTERM before the listener
    /// stops. A few seconds on Kubernetes; zero on a laptop.
    pub drain_delay_secs: u64,
    /// Prometheus text. Empty turns it off.
    pub metrics_path: String,
    /// When set, `/metrics` needs `Authorization: Bearer <token>`.
    pub metrics_token: String,
    /// When set, `/metrics` is served on this address and not on the main
    /// listener.
    pub metrics_bind: String,
    /// The OTLP/HTTP collector. Empty: `OTEL_EXPORTER_OTLP_ENDPOINT`.
    pub otlp_endpoint: String,
    /// The share of new traces kept, from 0.0 to 1.0.
    pub sample_ratio: f64,
}

impl Default for Settings {
    fn default() -> Self {
        Self {
            format: String::new(),
            level: "info".to_string(),
            live_path: "/up".to_string(),
            ready_path: "/readyz".to_string(),
            drain_delay_secs: 0,
            metrics_path: "/metrics".to_string(),
            metrics_token: String::new(),
            metrics_bind: String::new(),
            otlp_endpoint: String::new(),
            sample_ratio: 1.0,
        }
    }
}

/// `MY_APP_ENV`, the variable the config plugin reads too.
fn environment() -> String {
    let var = format!("{}_ENV", crate::app::APP.to_uppercase().replace('-', "_"));
    std::env::var(var).unwrap_or_else(|_| "development".to_string())
}

fn path(value: String) -> Option<String> {
    (!value.trim().is_empty()).then_some(value)
}

fn observer(settings: Settings) -> Result<Observer, String> {
    let mut config = ObserveConfig::new(crate::app::APP);
    config.log_format = LogFormat::resolve(Some(&settings.format), &environment())?;
    config.log_level = settings.level;
    config.live_path = path(settings.live_path);
    config.ready_path = path(settings.ready_path);
    config.drain_delay = Duration::from_secs(settings.drain_delay_secs);
    config.metrics_path = path(settings.metrics_path);
    config.metrics_token = path(settings.metrics_token);
    config.metrics_bind = path(settings.metrics_bind);
    config.tracing.enabled = true;
    config.tracing.endpoint = path(settings.otlp_endpoint);
    config.tracing.sample_ratio = settings.sample_ratio;
    Observer::new(config)
}

pub struct Observe {
    inner: Result<ObservePlugin, String>,
}

impl Observe {
    fn inner(&mut self) -> Result<&mut ObservePlugin, PluginError> {
        self.inner
            .as_mut()
            .map_err(|e| PluginError::new(format!("[observability] {e}")))
    }
}

impl Plugin for Observe {
    type Config = Settings;
    type Provides = NoContext;
    const NAME: &'static str = "observability";

    fn new(settings: Settings) -> Self {
        // `Plugin::new` cannot fail. A bad setting fails `register` instead,
        // with the line to write.
        Self {
            inner: observer(settings).map(ObservePlugin::new),
        }
    }

    fn register(&mut self, lc: &mut Lifecycle<'_>) -> Result<(), PluginError> {
        self.inner()?.register(lc)
    }

    fn after_config(&mut self, lc: &mut Lifecycle<'_>) -> Result<(), PluginError> {
        self.inner()?.after_config(lc)
    }

    fn before_start(&mut self, lc: &mut Lifecycle<'_>) -> Result<(), PluginError> {
        self.inner()?.before_start(lc)
    }

    fn shutdown(&mut self, sd: &mut ShutdownCx<'_>) -> Result<(), PluginError> {
        self.inner()?.shutdown(sd)
    }
}
===== src/plugins/observability/serving.rs =====
//! The `Observer` around the whole server: a server wrap.
//!
//! `src/plugins/mod.rs` composes it with any other wrap. It answers the
//! probes and `/metrics` itself, while their modules are here. Every other
//! request gets a request id, and the log line, the count and the span of
//! the modules chosen, under its route template.

use crate::plugins::{Handler, ServerWrap};

/// Names this wrap in `src/plugins/mod.rs`.
pub struct Serving;

impl ServerWrap for Serving {
    fn wrap(app: &std::sync::Arc<crate::app::App>, next: Handler) -> Handler {
        let routes = std::sync::Arc::clone(app);
        std::sync::Arc::new(observer(app).wrap_routed(
            move |request| next(request),
            move |request| {
                routes
                    .router
                    .at(&request.path)
                    .map(|found| found.template().to_string())
            },
        ))
    }

    /// `/metrics` on a second listener, when `[observability] metrics_bind`
    /// names one.
    fn side_listeners(
        app: &crate::app::App,
    ) -> impl std::future::Future<Output = Result<(), String>> {
        let observer = observer(app);
        async move {
            let Some(bind) = observer.config().metrics_bind.clone() else {
                return Ok(());
            };
            let server = brocanth_server::Server::bind(&bind)
                .await
                .map_err(|e| e.to_string())?;
            tokio::spawn(async move {
                let _ = observer.serve_admin(server).await;
            });
            Ok(())
        }
    }

    /// Readiness fails at once, so a load balancer stops sending requests,
    /// and the listener closes after the drain delay: `drain_delay_secs`
    /// with `observability-health`, zero without it.
    fn before_stop(app: &crate::app::App) -> impl std::future::Future<Output = ()> {
        let observer = observer(app);
        async move { observer.drain().await }
    }
}

/// The `Observer` the `observability` plugin published.
fn observer(app: &crate::app::App) -> brocanth_observe::Observer {
    app.core
        .services()
        .get::<brocanth_observe::Observer>()
        .cloned()
        .expect("`observability` is registered in `register!`")
}
===== src/plugins/openapi/mod.rs =====
//! The OpenAPI document, as this application derives it.
//!
//! The route table already knows every path, every verb and every path
//! parameter, and it knows them while this crate compiles, because the
//! filesystem is the route table. So the document is derived from
//! `crate::pages::router()` rather than written, and the two cannot drift:
//! add a file under `src/pages` and the document grows an operation.
//!
//! `brocanth-openapi` is in `Cargo.toml` because this file is here, and
//! through no other route. Delete the registration in `src/plugins/mod.rs`,
//! this file, `src/pages/openapi.json.rs` and `src/pages/openapi.yaml.rs`,
//! and the application describes nothing and links nothing to describe it
//! with.
//!
//! # What is derived, and what is not
//!
//! Every route, with its path parameters, an `operationId` and a 200. What a page takes and gives comes
//! from `pages::described()`, which `pages!` wrote off the handler
//! signatures: a second parameter is the request body, a `Json<T>`
//! return is the answer. Neither is written here, so neither can be
//! out of date.
//!
//! Anything else -- a summary, a second status, an example -- is added by
//! hand with `SpecBuilder::describe`, beside the lines below:
//!
//! ```ignore
//! .describe("/users/{id}", Verb::Get, |op| {
//!     op.summary("One user")
//! })
//! ```
//!
//! `/openapi.json` is hidden from the document it serves. The endpoint is a
//! row of the table it describes, and an endpoint that describes itself is
//! noise in every client somebody generates from this.
//!
//! # Why this is not `OpenApiPlugin`
//!
//! The crate also ships `OpenApiPlugin`, and this is not that. That one is
//! handed a document that is already built, which is right for an application
//! that writes its document in `main`. This one takes a title and a version,
//! because a new project has a title and a version. It still holds an
//! `OpenApiPlugin` and calls its hooks, so the two things only that type can
//! do still happen: an invalid document stops the build, and the document
//! reaches `Services` for anything else that wants to read it.

use std::sync::OnceLock;

use brocanth::plugin_api::{Lifecycle, Plugin, PluginError};
use brocanth::plugin_context::NoContext;
use brocanth::routing::Verb;
use brocanth_openapi::{Format, OpenApiConfig, OpenApiPlugin, Schema, SpecBuilder};

/// Where the document is served.
///
/// A constant and not a setting. The path is the name of the file in
/// `src/pages` that answers it, so moving the document is renaming that file
/// and this line, and a setting that disagreed with the file name would serve
/// a 404 and hide it in the document.
pub const ROUTE: &str = "/openapi.json";

/// The rendered document, both ways round.
///
/// Rendered once at `configure`, so a request costs a borrow rather than a
/// walk of the whole structure. A `OnceLock` because the page that serves it
/// is a plain function with no handle on this plugin.
static RENDERED: OnceLock<(String, String)> = OnceLock::new();

/// The document as JSON, or `None` when this plugin was never registered.
pub fn json() -> Option<&'static str> {
    RENDERED.get().map(|(json, _)| json.as_str())
}

/// The document as YAML, or `None` when this plugin was never registered.
///
/// Served by `src/pages/openapi.yaml.rs`, which is the file beside the
/// JSON one and reads this instead.
pub fn yaml() -> Option<&'static str> {
    RENDERED.get().map(|(_, yaml)| yaml.as_str())
}

/// What this plugin is built from.
///
/// It derives `Deserialize` because this application reads it from
/// `[openapi]` in a TOML file. Drop the config plugin and the derive goes
/// with it: `Plugin::Config` carries no bounds the core imposes.
#[derive(Debug, serde::Deserialize)]
#[serde(default, deny_unknown_fields)]
pub struct Settings {
    /// The name of the API, which is not the name of this binary.
    pub title: String,
    /// The version of the contract, not of the crate.
    pub version: String,
}

impl Default for Settings {
    fn default() -> Self {
        Self {
            title: "My Blog".to_string(),
            version: "0.1.0".to_string(),
        }
    }
}

pub struct OpenApi {
    settings: Settings,
    /// Built at `configure` and not in `new`, because deriving a document can
    /// fail and `Plugin::new` cannot.
    served: Option<OpenApiPlugin>,
}

impl Plugin for OpenApi {
    type Config = Settings;
    type Provides = NoContext;
    const NAME: &'static str = "openapi";

    fn new(config: Settings) -> Self {
        Self {
            settings: config,
            served: None,
        }
    }

    fn configure(&mut self, lc: &mut Lifecycle<'_>) -> Result<(), PluginError> {
        // The same function `src/app.rs` builds the live table with, so the
        // document describes what this binary actually dispatches.
        let router = crate::pages::router().map_err(|e| {
            PluginError::new(format!("[openapi] the route table does not build: {e}"))
        })?;
        let mut spec = SpecBuilder::new(self.settings.title.clone(), self.settings.version.clone())
            .routes_from_router(&router)
            .hide(ROUTE, Verb::Get);

        // What each page takes and gives. `pages!` read it off the handler
        // signatures, so the document says what the code does rather than
        // what somebody remembered to write down.
        for route in crate::pages::described() {
            if let Some(node) = &route.takes {
                spec = spec.describing_node(node);
            }
            if let Some(node) = &route.gives {
                spec = spec.describing_node(node);
            }
            let takes = route.takes.as_ref().and_then(|node| node.name());
            let gives = route.gives.as_ref().and_then(|node| node.name());
            spec = spec.describe(route.path, route.verb, move |mut op| {
                if let Some(name) = takes {
                    op = op.json_body(Schema::reference(name));
                }
                if let Some(name) = gives {
                    // A description is required and may not be empty, which
                    // the document's own validator says before anything is
                    // served. One sentence, the same for every operation,
                    // and `.describe(..)` is where a better one goes.
                    op = op.json_response(200, "The answer", Schema::reference(name));
                }
                op
            });
        }

        let document = spec
            .build()
            .map_err(|e| PluginError::new(format!("[openapi] deriving the document: {e}")))?;

        // The crate's own plugin, held rather than registered. Its
        // `configure` is what makes an invalid document a build failure
        // instead of a 500 found during an incident, and it renders both
        // bodies once.
        let mut served = OpenApiPlugin::new(OpenApiConfig::new(document).json_route(ROUTE));
        served.configure(lc)?;
        let _ = RENDERED.set((
            served.body(Format::Json).to_string(),
            served.body(Format::Yaml).to_string(),
        ));
        self.served = Some(served);
        Ok(())
    }

    fn register(&mut self, lc: &mut Lifecycle<'_>) -> Result<(), PluginError> {
        // Publishes the document through `Services`, so a command or another
        // plugin can read it with `brocanth_openapi::document_of`.
        match &mut self.served {
            Some(served) => served.register(lc),
            None => Ok(()),
        }
    }
}
===== src/plugins/policy/mod.rs =====
//! Authorisation, as this application writes it.
//!
//! A rule is a predicate and a check is a witness. `PostPolicy` below is the
//! first; `Verified` is the second; the two are not alternatives.
//!
//! `brocanth-policy` is in `Cargo.toml` because this file is here, and through
//! no other route. Delete the registration in `src/plugins/mod.rs` and
//! delete this file, and the application has no rule algebra and links
//! nothing to have one with.
//!
//! # The bug this file cannot have
//!
//! PocketBase validates a rule against an empty request object, so a
//! misspelled field in a negated rule becomes `'' IS NOT 'banned'`, which is
//! true, which grants access -- silently, for ever. Two things here make that
//! impossible, and both are visible below:
//!
//! 1. **A field name is not a string.** `Pred::eq` takes a `PostColumn`, so
//!    `PostColumn::Autor` is a compile error. There is no
//!    `Pred::eq("author_id", ..)` to fall back to and no string-to-filter
//!    parser anywhere in this framework.
//! 2. **An absent value is a word somebody wrote.** An `Option` does not turn
//!    into a `Bound`. The author writes `Bound::or_deny`, `or_allow` or
//!    `or_null`, and the word is in the diff for a reviewer to disagree with.
//!
//! # Where the rules go
//!
//! `Post` is here so that this file compiles into something that runs. Move
//! it, and whatever follows it, into `src/policies/` once there is more than
//! one -- what must stay here is the plugin, because that is what reads the
//! dialect and puts it on the context.
//!
//! The column enum is written by hand on purpose: the `policy!` macro is
//! deliberately late, after a real application has used the algebra, so that
//! the syntax is informed rather than imitated.
//!
//! # Listing, and then writing
//!
//! A list is a `WHERE` fragment, so the paginator keeps its totals:
//!
//! ```ignore
//! let rules = ctx.provided::<Rules, _>().expect("a dispatched request");
//! let condition = PostPolicy.list(&visitor).condition::<Post>(rules.dialect)?;
//! // "SELECT .. FROM posts WHERE " + condition.sql(), bound to condition.binds()
//! ```
//!
//! A write is the same predicate carried into the statement, which is what
//! closes the window between the check and the change:
//!
//! ```ignore
//! let admission = PostPolicy.update(&visitor).admission::<Post>(rules.dialect, id)?;
//! let rows = /* run admission.query(), count the rows */;
//! let verified = admission.resolve(rows)?;          // zero rows is a denial
//! let write = verified.update(rules.dialect, [(PostColumn::Status, "published".into())])?;
//! // run write.query(); then:
//! write.applied(affected)?;                          // zero affected is RaceLost
//! ```
//!
//! `Verified` is taken by value everywhere it is used, and a debug build fails
//! the test that checked one and then forgot to spend it.
//!
//! # This project also has the broker
//!
//! `Pred::audience` is the join, and it is why `brocanth-realtime`
//! depends on this crate. A predicate you can inspect inverts into an
//! audience -- `author_id = 7` becomes the key `(author_id, 7)` -- so the
//! broker files a subscriber under that key and one write is a map lookup
//! rather than one query per subscriber. A rule that is a string can only
//! be executed, which is why PocketBase runs the query.

use brocanth::plugin_api::{Lifecycle, Plugin, PluginError};
use brocanth::plugin_context::{ContextBuilder, ContextItem, ProvidesContext};
use brocanth_policy::{Bound, Column, Dialect, Entity, EntityPred, FieldMask, Ident, Policy, Pred};

// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
// An entity
// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

/// The columns of `posts`, as an enum, because a rule is written in them.
#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, PartialEq, Eq, Hash)]
pub enum PostColumn {
    /// Primary key.
    Id,
    /// Who wrote it.
    AuthorId,
    /// `draft` or `published`.
    Status,
    /// The text.
    Body,
}

impl Column for PostColumn {
    /// Required rather than derived: a changed-field name that is not one of
    /// these is rejected instead of quietly permitted.
    const ALL: &'static [Self] = &[Self::Id, Self::AuthorId, Self::Status, Self::Body];

    fn name(&self) -> &'static str {
        match self {
            Self::Id => "id",
            Self::AuthorId => "author_id",
            Self::Status => "status",
            Self::Body => "body",
        }
    }
}

/// The table the rules below are about.
pub struct Post;

impl Entity for Post {
    type Column = PostColumn;
    /// Checked at compile time, because a table name is program text and the
    /// only honest moment to reject one is while the program is being built.
    const TABLE: Ident = Ident::must("posts");
    const PRIMARY_KEY: Self::Column = PostColumn::Id;
}

// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
// A subject, and the rules about it
// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

/// Whoever is asking. `brocanth-policy` never looks inside it.
///
/// An anonymous visitor is this application's own `None`, turned into a
/// decision at the point a rule reads it.
#[derive(Clone, Debug, Default)]
pub struct Visitor {
    /// The signed-in account, when there is one.
    pub account_id: Option<i64>,
    /// Whether this one may see everything.
    pub admin: bool,
}

impl Visitor {
    /// Nobody. What a request with no session carries.
    pub fn anonymous() -> Self {
        Self::default()
    }
}

/// The rules for `posts`.
///
/// Only `list` is required, because list authorisation is the one that cannot
/// be done per object: a paginator that filters after reading lies about its
/// totals. Everything else defaults to deny, which is what makes shipping this
/// file as it stands safe rather than lucky.
#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, Default)]
pub struct PostPolicy;

impl Policy for PostPolicy {
    type Entity = Post;
    type Subject = Visitor;

    fn list(&self, who: &Visitor) -> EntityPred<Post> {
        if who.admin {
            // `Pred::is_always` reports this, so a test can assert which
            // subjects see everything.
            return Pred::Always;
        }
        Pred::any([
            Pred::eq(PostColumn::Status, "published"),
            // `or_deny` is the word that says what an anonymous visitor's
            // missing id means: they own nothing.
            Pred::eq(PostColumn::AuthorId, Bound::or_deny(who.account_id)),
        ])
    }

    fn create(&self, who: &Visitor) -> EntityPred<Post> {
        Pred::eq(PostColumn::AuthorId, Bound::or_deny(who.account_id))
    }

    fn update(&self, who: &Visitor) -> EntityPred<Post> {
        if who.admin {
            return Pred::Always;
        }
        Pred::eq(PostColumn::AuthorId, Bound::or_deny(who.account_id))
    }

    fn writable(&self, who: &Visitor) -> FieldMask<PostColumn> {
        if who.admin {
            FieldMask::except([PostColumn::Id])
        } else {
            // The author edits the body, never the status. `AuthorId` is
            // here because a create writes it, and `create` above admits
            // only the author's own id. The mask is the other half of the
            // rule: `update` says which rows, this says which columns.
            FieldMask::only([PostColumn::Body, PostColumn::AuthorId])
        }
    }
}

// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
// The plugin
// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

/// What this plugin is built from.
///
/// It derives `Deserialize` because this application reads it from
/// `[policy]` in a TOML file. Drop the config plugin and the derive goes
/// with it: `Plugin::Config` carries no bounds the core imposes.
#[derive(Debug, serde::Deserialize)]
#[serde(default, deny_unknown_fields)]
pub struct Settings {
    /// `postgres` or `sqlite`. A string here and an enum below: a config file
    /// is text, and the translation is checked at startup where a typo can
    /// still be answered.
    pub dialect: String,
}

impl Default for Settings {
    fn default() -> Self {
        Self {
            dialect: "postgres".to_string(),
        }
    }
}

/// What this plugin puts on the context.
///
/// The dialect, and only the dialect. Every compile entry point still takes
/// one as an argument -- the call site says which it meant -- and this is
/// where the call site gets it, so one database is named once.
///
/// The policies are not here. `PostPolicy` is a unit struct and a value, not
/// a service: a handler writes `PostPolicy.list(&who)` and has nothing to
/// look up. What a handler cannot work out on its own is which dialect this
/// deployment is, so that is what the context carries.
pub struct Rules {
    /// Which dialect this application's conditions are compiled for.
    pub dialect: Dialect,
}

impl ContextItem for Rules {}

pub struct Policies {
    settings: Settings,
    /// Read at `configure` and not in `new`, because turning a string into a
    /// dialect can fail and `Plugin::new` cannot.
    dialect: Option<Dialect>,
}

impl Plugin for Policies {
    type Config = Settings;
    type Provides = ProvidesContext<Rules>;
    const NAME: &'static str = "policy";

    fn new(config: Settings) -> Self {
        Self {
            settings: config,
            dialect: None,
        }
    }

    fn configure(&mut self, _lc: &mut Lifecycle<'_>) -> Result<(), PluginError> {
        let dialect = match self.settings.dialect.as_str() {
            "postgres" => Dialect::Postgres,
            "sqlite" => Dialect::Sqlite,
            other => {
                return Err(PluginError::new(format!(
                    "[policy] dialect is {other:?}; write postgres or sqlite"
                )))
            }
        };

        // Compile one rule at startup. It costs a string and it answers the
        // only question this file can get wrong without anybody noticing: a
        // column whose name is not a bare identifier fails the query rather
        // than escaping into it, and finding that out here is better than
        // finding it out on the first list request.
        let condition = PostPolicy
            .list(&Visitor::anonymous())
            .condition::<Post>(dialect)
            .map_err(|e| PluginError::new(format!("[policy] posts: {e}")))?;
        let sql = condition.sql();
        crate::plugins::say!(info, "[policy] posts, anonymous: {sql}"; sql);

        self.dialect = Some(dialect);
        Ok(())
    }

    fn provide(&self, cx: &mut ContextBuilder) {
        // `configure` runs before the server is handed the application, so
        // the dialect is here by the time any request builds a context.
        if let Some(dialect) = self.dialect {
            cx.insert(Rules { dialect });
        }
    }
}
===== src/plugins/realtime/mod.rs =====
//! The realtime broker, as this application registers it.
//!
//! A subscription is an index entry, not a query. `brocanth-policy` is a
//! value rather than a string, so "the owner" inverts into `(author_id, 7)`,
//! the broker files the subscriber under that key, and a write becomes a map
//! lookup. PocketBase runs one `SELECT` per subscriber instead, which is
//! about 180 ms on Postgres at six hundred of them.
//!
//! `brocanth-realtime` is in `Cargo.toml` because this file is here, and
//! through no other route. Delete the registration in `src/plugins/mod.rs`
//! and delete this file, and the application broadcasts nothing and links
//! nothing to do it with.
//!
//! # Publishing
//!
//! A handler that just wrote a row has the row, so it hands it over and the
//! broker reads nothing:
//!
//! ```ignore
//! // In a page handler: `Ctx::provided` reads the plugin context.
//! let realtime = ctx.provided::<Broadcast, _>().expect("a dispatched request");
//! let row = Row::new().with("author_id", author);
//! realtime
//!     .broker
//!     .publish_with_row(&RowChange::created("posts", id), &row);
//! ```
//!
//! `src/app.rs` extracts it once at startup and prints the client limit,
//! which is there to be deleted: it exists so a brand-new project reads the
//! value it was given rather than warning that nothing does.
//!
//! # Reading
//!
//! The broker stops one layer short of the socket. Four things read a
//! mailbox:
//!
//! 1. **A browser, over server-sent events.** The `realtime-sse` module
//!    writes `GET /realtime/events?topics=posts/*` and the stream behind it
//!    (`src/plugins/realtime_sse/mod.rs`): one `Client` per connection, a
//!    heartbeat, `lagged` when frames were dropped, and an end on shutdown.
//!    `brocanth add realtime-sse` adds it. `realtime-websocket` is the
//!    duplex form: `GET /realtime/socket`, frames out and `subscribe`
//!    commands in.
//! 2. **In process.** `Broker::connect`, one subscription, and a thread on
//!    `Mailbox::recv_blocking`. This is how a cache invalidator or an audit
//!    log is written, and it is the reason to have a broker before there is a
//!    transport at all.
//! 3. **Long polling.** Keep the `Client` in the session; each request calls
//!    `Mailbox::recv_timeout` for a few seconds, then `Mailbox::drain`, and
//!    returns the frames as one finished body. Every hook runs exactly as
//!    specified. It costs a request per interval and a little latency.
//! 4. **Nothing.** The index costs what it holds, and an application that
//!    publishes without subscribers pays for a map lookup that finds no one.

use std::sync::Arc;

use brocanth::plugin_api::{Lifecycle, Plugin, PluginError};
use brocanth::plugin_context::{ContextBuilder, ContextItem, ProvidesContext};
use brocanth_realtime::{Broker, Config, Overflow};

/// What this plugin is built from.
///
/// It derives `Deserialize` because this application reads it from
/// `[realtime]` in a TOML file. Drop the config plugin and the derive goes
/// with it: `Plugin::Config` carries no bounds the core imposes.
#[derive(Debug, serde::Deserialize)]
#[serde(default, deny_unknown_fields)]
pub struct Settings {
    /// How many connections the broker will hold. At the limit `connect`
    /// refuses rather than evicting, so a new socket cannot displace an
    /// established one.
    pub max_clients: usize,
    /// How many topics one client may hold, so a client cannot spend the
    /// server's memory by asking.
    pub max_subscriptions_per_client: usize,
    /// How many frames a mailbox holds before the overflow policy fires.
    pub queue: usize,
    /// `drop-oldest`, `drop-newest` or `disconnect`. A string here and an
    /// enum below: the config file is text, and the translation is checked
    /// at startup where a typo can still be answered.
    pub overflow: String,
}

impl Default for Settings {
    fn default() -> Self {
        Self {
            max_clients: 1000,
            max_subscriptions_per_client: 64,
            queue: 32,
            overflow: "drop-oldest".to_string(),
        }
    }
}

/// What this plugin puts on the context.
///
/// A wrapper and not `Arc<Broker>` itself, because `ContextItem` is
/// brocanth's trait and `Broker` is `brocanth-realtime`'s type. That crate
/// deliberately does not depend on the core -- nothing in it touches a
/// request, a response or a route -- so the impl that joins the two belongs
/// in the one crate that can see both, which is this one.
pub struct Broadcast {
    /// The broker. Shared, so a clone is a pointer and every request sees
    /// the same index.
    pub broker: Arc<Broker>,
}

impl ContextItem for Broadcast {}

pub struct Realtime {
    settings: Settings,
    /// Built at `configure` and not in `new`, because reading the overflow
    /// policy out of a string can fail and `Plugin::new` cannot.
    broker: Option<Arc<Broker>>,
}

impl Plugin for Realtime {
    type Config = Settings;
    type Provides = ProvidesContext<Broadcast>;
    const NAME: &'static str = "realtime";

    fn new(config: Settings) -> Self {
        Self {
            settings: config,
            broker: None,
        }
    }

    fn configure(&mut self, _lc: &mut Lifecycle<'_>) -> Result<(), PluginError> {
        let overflow = match self.settings.overflow.as_str() {
            "drop-oldest" => Overflow::DropOldest,
            "drop-newest" => Overflow::DropNewest,
            "disconnect" => Overflow::Disconnect,
            other => {
                return Err(PluginError::new(format!(
                    "[realtime] overflow is {other:?}; write drop-oldest, \
                     drop-newest or disconnect"
                )))
            }
        };
        if self.settings.queue == 0 {
            return Err(PluginError::new(
                "[realtime] queue must hold at least one frame",
            ));
        }
        if self.settings.max_clients == 0 {
            return Err(PluginError::new(
                "[realtime] max_clients is 0, so every connection is refused",
            ));
        }
        // Synchronous, allocating a few maps, needing no runtime. Unlike a
        // pool, there is nothing here to open, so there is no `before_start`.
        self.broker = Some(
            Broker::builder()
                .config(Config {
                    max_clients: self.settings.max_clients,
                    max_subscriptions_per_client: self.settings.max_subscriptions_per_client,
                    queue: self.settings.queue,
                    overflow,
                })
                .build(),
        );
        Ok(())
    }

    fn provide(&self, cx: &mut ContextBuilder) {
        // `configure` runs before the server is handed the application, so
        // the broker is here by the time any request builds a context.
        if let Some(broker) = &self.broker {
            cx.insert(Broadcast {
                broker: broker.clone(),
            });
        }
    }
}
===== src/plugins/realtime_sse/mod.rs =====
//! `realtime-sse`: the broker's frames, written to a browser as server-sent
//! events.
//!
//! The transport is here, in the application, and not in
//! `brocanth-realtime`, because that crate does not depend on `brocanth`:
//! nothing in it touches a request, a response or a route. A transport is a
//! response body, and a body belongs to the crate that owns the routes, which
//! is this one. `src/pages/realtime/events.rs` is the route.
//!
//! # What the stream writes
//!
//! ```text
//! : connected 7              first, so the headers leave at once
//! event:lagged               first, when a reconnect may have missed frames
//! id:12 event:posts/* data:  each frame the broker addressed to this client
//! event:lagged data:3        the mailbox was full and 3 frames were dropped
//! :                          a heartbeat, after HEARTBEAT with nothing to say
//! event:close                the server is stopping; the browser reconnects
//! ```
//!
//! `lagged` means "what you show may be stale: fetch it again". It is the
//! mailbox's `Event::Lagged`, written for the browser.
//!
//! # Back-pressure
//!
//! The broker never waits for this stream. The mailbox is bounded, and
//! `[realtime] overflow` says what a full one does. `drop-oldest` and
//! `drop-newest` drop frames, and the stream writes `lagged`. `disconnect`
//! closes the mailbox, the stream ends, and the browser reconnects with
//! `Last-Event-ID` and is told `lagged`.
//!
//! # Ending
//!
//! * The browser leaves. The server drops this stream, which drops the
//!   `Client`, which unsubscribes it and closes its mailbox. The server
//!   learns that a socket is gone only when a write fails, so the heartbeat
//!   is also what frees a dead subscriber, within one period.
//! * The server stops. The stream gate calls `finish`. The stream writes the
//!   frames already queued, then `event:close`, and ends, so a deploy does
//!   not wait for a subscriber.

use std::task::{Context, Poll};
use std::time::Duration;

use brocanth::plugin_body::{BodyStream, Chunk};
use brocanth_realtime::{Client, Event};
use tokio::time::{Instant, Interval, MissedTickBehavior};

/// How long a stream stays silent before it writes a heartbeat.
///
/// Shorter than the idle timeout of any proxy in front of this server, and
/// the longest a dead subscriber holds its mailbox.
pub const HEARTBEAT: Duration = Duration::from_secs(15);

/// One subscriber's frames, as a response body.
pub struct Frames {
    /// The broker's handle for this connection. Dropped with the stream, and
    /// that drop is the disconnect.
    client: Client,
    /// Written before the first frame.
    preamble: Option<Vec<u8>>,
    /// What `finish` left to write: the queued frames and `event:close`.
    tail: Option<Vec<u8>>,
    ended: bool,
    /// Built on the first idle poll: a timer needs the runtime, and the
    /// server's poll is certain to have one.
    ticker: Option<Interval>,
}

impl Frames {
    /// A stream over `client`'s mailbox.
    ///
    /// `missed` is true when the browser reconnected and frames were
    /// published since the last one it read. The broker keeps no history, so
    /// the stream cannot replay them; it says `lagged` instead.
    pub fn new(client: Client, missed: bool) -> Self {
        let mut preamble = format!(": connected {}\n\n", client.id()).into_bytes();
        if missed {
            preamble.extend_from_slice(&lagged(None));
        }
        Self {
            client,
            preamble: Some(preamble),
            tail: None,
            ended: false,
            ticker: None,
        }
    }
}

/// `event:lagged`, with how many frames were dropped when that is known.
fn lagged(skipped: Option<u64>) -> Vec<u8> {
    match skipped {
        Some(n) => format!("event:lagged\ndata:{n}\n\n").into_bytes(),
        None => b"event:lagged\ndata:\n\n".to_vec(),
    }
}

/// What a mailbox event looks like on the wire.
fn encoded(event: Event) -> Vec<u8> {
    match event {
        // Encoded once by the broker and shared by every subscriber.
        Event::Frame(frame) => frame.bytes().to_vec(),
        Event::Lagged { skipped } => lagged(Some(skipped)),
    }
}

impl BodyStream for Frames {
    fn poll_chunk(&mut self, cx: &mut Context<'_>) -> Chunk {
        if let Some(preamble) = self.preamble.take() {
            return Poll::Ready(Some(Ok(preamble)));
        }
        if let Some(tail) = self.tail.take() {
            self.ended = true;
            return Poll::Ready(Some(Ok(tail)));
        }
        if self.ended {
            return Poll::Ready(None);
        }
        match self.client.mailbox().poll_recv(cx) {
            Poll::Ready(Some(event)) => {
                // A frame is traffic, so the next heartbeat is a full
                // period away.
                if let Some(ticker) = &mut self.ticker {
                    ticker.reset();
                }
                return Poll::Ready(Some(Ok(encoded(event))));
            }
            // This stream holds the client, so only the overflow policy
            // closes its mailbox: `disconnect` fired. End; the browser comes
            // back with `Last-Event-ID` and is told `lagged`.
            Poll::Ready(None) => return Poll::Ready(None),
            Poll::Pending => {}
        }
        let ticker = self.ticker.get_or_insert_with(|| {
            let mut ticker = tokio::time::interval_at(Instant::now() + HEARTBEAT, HEARTBEAT);
            ticker.set_missed_tick_behavior(MissedTickBehavior::Delay);
            ticker
        });
        match ticker.poll_tick(cx) {
            Poll::Ready(_) => Poll::Ready(Some(Ok(b":\n\n".to_vec()))),
            Poll::Pending => Poll::Pending,
        }
    }

    /// The server is stopping: write what is queued, then `event:close`.
    fn finish(&mut self) {
        let mut tail = Vec::new();
        for event in self.client.mailbox().drain() {
            tail.extend(encoded(event));
        }
        tail.extend_from_slice(b"event:close\ndata:\n\n");
        self.tail = Some(tail);
    }
}

#[cfg(test)]
mod tests {
    use brocanth_events::RowChange;
    use brocanth_realtime::{Broker, Gate, Subscription, Topic};

    use super::*;

    async fn next(frames: &mut Frames) -> Option<String> {
        let chunk = std::future::poll_fn(|cx| frames.poll_chunk(cx)).await?;
        Some(String::from_utf8(chunk.expect("no error")).expect("utf-8"))
    }

    fn subscribed(broker: &std::sync::Arc<Broker>) -> Client {
        let client = broker.connect().expect("room");
        client
            .subscribe(Subscription::new(
                Topic::collection("posts").expect("a topic"),
                Gate::everyone(),
            ))
            .expect("subscribed");
        client
    }

    #[tokio::test]
    async fn a_published_change_is_written_as_an_event() {
        let broker = Broker::new();
        let mut frames = Frames::new(subscribed(&broker), false);
        assert_eq!(
            next(&mut frames).await.as_deref(),
            Some(": connected 1\n\n")
        );

        broker.publish_change(&RowChange::created("posts", 42i64));
        let frame = next(&mut frames).await.expect("a frame");
        assert!(frame.starts_with("id:1\nevent:posts/*\n"), "{frame}");
        assert!(frame.contains("data:id=42\n"), "{frame}");
    }

    #[tokio::test]
    async fn a_reconnect_that_missed_frames_is_told_so() {
        let broker = Broker::new();
        let mut frames = Frames::new(subscribed(&broker), true);
        let preamble = next(&mut frames).await.expect("the preamble");
        assert!(preamble.ends_with("event:lagged\ndata:\n\n"), "{preamble}");
    }

    #[tokio::test]
    async fn finish_writes_the_queue_then_close_then_ends() {
        let broker = Broker::new();
        let mut frames = Frames::new(subscribed(&broker), false);
        let _ = next(&mut frames).await;
        broker.publish_change(&RowChange::deleted("posts", 7i64));

        frames.finish();
        let tail = next(&mut frames).await.expect("the tail");
        assert!(tail.contains("data:id=7\n"), "{tail}");
        assert!(tail.ends_with("event:close\ndata:\n\n"), "{tail}");
        assert_eq!(next(&mut frames).await, None);
    }

    #[tokio::test]
    async fn dropping_the_stream_frees_the_subscriber() {
        let broker = Broker::new();
        let frames = Frames::new(subscribed(&broker), false);
        assert_eq!(broker.clients(), 1);
        drop(frames);
        assert_eq!(broker.clients(), 0);
    }
}
===== src/plugins/realtime_websocket/mod.rs =====
//! `realtime-websocket`: the broker's frames over a WebSocket, and commands
//! back.
//!
//! The transport is here, in the application, and not in
//! `brocanth-realtime`, because that crate does not depend on `brocanth`:
