//! nothing in it touches a request, a response or a route.
//! `src/pages/realtime/socket.rs` is the route; it checks the handshake and
//! the Origin, authorises the first topics, and hands the socket to [`run`].
//!
//! # What the server sends
//!
//! One text message per record, in the same format as server-sent events,
//! so one parser reads both transports:
//!
//! ```text
//! id:12 event:posts/* data:..   each frame the broker addressed to this client
//! event:lagged data:3           the queue was full and 3 frames were dropped
//! event:lagged data:            first, when a reconnect may have missed frames
//! event:subscribed data:posts/42      the answer to `subscribe posts/42`
//! event:unsubscribed data:posts/42    the answer to `unsubscribe posts/42`
//! event:refused data:posts/42: why    a command that was not obeyed
//! ```
//!
//! A frame is the broker's bytes as it encoded them, shared by every
//! subscriber; nothing is encoded twice.
//!
//! # What the browser sends
//!
//! `subscribe <topic>` and `unsubscribe <topic>`, as text. Each topic passes
//! the same `authorise` as the topics in the URL. A binary message, or one
//! larger than `websocket::MAX_MESSAGE` (64 KiB), ends the connection.
//!
//! # Back-pressure
//!
//! The broker never waits for this socket. Each send waits until the frame
//! is written, so a browser that reads slowly holds this loop, the loop
//! stops taking from the mailbox, and `[realtime] overflow` decides what is
//! lost: `drop-oldest` and `drop-newest` drop frames and the browser reads
//! `lagged`; `disconnect` closes the mailbox, and this loop closes the
//! socket with code 1013.
//!
//! # Ending
//!
//! * The browser closes the socket, or stops answering pings for one
//!   `HEARTBEAT`. The loop ends, which drops the `Client`, which unsubscribes
//!   it and closes its mailbox.
//! * The server stops. The loop sends the frames already queued, then a
//!   close frame with code 1001, and ends, so a deploy does not wait for a
//!   subscriber. The browser reconnects with `?last=<id>`.

use std::collections::HashMap;
use std::time::Duration;

use brocanth_realtime::{Client, Event, Subscription, SubscriptionId, Topic};
use brocanth_server::websocket::{self, Incoming, WebSocket};
use tokio::time::{Instant, MissedTickBehavior};

/// How often the socket pings the browser. A browser that has not answered
/// by the next ping is gone, so this is also the longest a dead subscriber
/// holds its mailbox.
pub const HEARTBEAT: Duration = Duration::from_secs(15);

/// Who may follow what: the page's `authorise`, with what it read from the
/// request already captured.
pub type Authorise = Box<dyn Fn(Topic) -> Result<Subscription, String> + Send>;

/// The topics this socket follows, by name, so `unsubscribe` can find them.
pub type Filed = HashMap<String, SubscriptionId>;

/// Serves one socket until the browser leaves or the server stops.
///
/// `client` holds the subscriptions the page filed, and `filed` names them.
/// `missed` is true when the browser reconnected and frames were published
/// since the last one it read; the broker keeps no history, so the socket
/// says `lagged` instead.
pub async fn run(
    mut socket: WebSocket,
    client: Client,
    authorise: Authorise,
    mut filed: Filed,
    missed: bool,
) {
    if missed && socket.send_text(lagged(None)).await.is_err() {
        return;
    }
    let mut ticker = tokio::time::interval_at(Instant::now() + HEARTBEAT, HEARTBEAT);
    ticker.set_missed_tick_behavior(MissedTickBehavior::Delay);
    loop {
        tokio::select! {
            event = client.mailbox().recv() => match event {
                Some(event) => {
                    if socket.send_text(encoded(event)).await.is_err() {
                        return;
                    }
                }
                // This loop holds the client, so only the overflow policy
                // closes its mailbox: `disconnect` fired.
                None => {
                    return socket
                        .close(websocket::TRY_AGAIN_LATER, "too far behind; reconnect")
                        .await;
                }
            },
            incoming = socket.recv() => match incoming {
                Incoming::Text(command) => {
                    let answer = obey(&client, &authorise, &mut filed, &command);
                    if socket.send_text(answer).await.is_err() {
                        return;
                    }
                }
                Incoming::Binary(_) => {
                    return socket.close(websocket::POLICY, "send text commands").await;
                }
                Incoming::Stopping => {
                    for event in client.mailbox().drain() {
                        if socket.send_text(encoded(event)).await.is_err() {
                            return;
                        }
                    }
                    return socket
                        .close(websocket::GOING_AWAY, "the server is stopping")
                        .await;
                }
                Incoming::Closed(_) => return,
            },
            _ = ticker.tick() => {
                if socket.keepalive().await.is_err() {
                    return;
                }
            }
        }
    }
}

/// One command from the browser, obeyed, and the record that answers it.
fn obey(client: &Client, authorise: &Authorise, filed: &mut Filed, command: &str) -> String {
    let (verb, name) = command
        .trim()
        .split_once(' ')
        .unwrap_or((command.trim(), ""));
    let name = name.trim();
    let topic = match Topic::parse(name) {
        Ok(topic) => topic,
        Err(e) => return record("refused", &format!("{name}: {e}")),
    };
    match verb {
        "subscribe" if filed.contains_key(name) => record("subscribed", name),
        "subscribe" => {
            let subscription = match authorise(topic) {
                Ok(subscription) => subscription,
                Err(why) => return record("refused", &format!("{name}: {why}")),
            };
            match client.subscribe(subscription) {
                Ok(subscribed) => {
                    filed.insert(name.to_string(), subscribed.id);
                    record("subscribed", name)
                }
                Err(e) => record("refused", &format!("{name}: {e}")),
            }
        }
        "unsubscribe" => {
            if let Some(id) = filed.remove(name) {
                client.unsubscribe(id);
            }
            record("unsubscribed", name)
        }
        other => record(
            "refused",
            &format!(
                "{other:?} is not a command; send `subscribe <topic>` or `unsubscribe <topic>`"
            ),
        ),
    }
}

/// A record with no id: an event name and one data line.
fn record(event: &str, data: &str) -> String {
    let data = data.replace(['\r', '\n'], " ");
    format!("event:{event}\ndata:{data}\n\n")
}

/// `event:lagged`, with how many frames were dropped when that is known.
fn lagged(skipped: Option<u64>) -> String {
    match skipped {
        Some(n) => format!("event:lagged\ndata:{n}\n\n"),
        None => "event:lagged\ndata:\n\n".to_string(),
    }
}

/// What a mailbox event looks like on the wire.
fn encoded(event: Event) -> String {
    match event {
        // Encoded once by the broker and shared by every subscriber.
        Event::Frame(frame) => String::from_utf8_lossy(frame.bytes()).into_owned(),
        Event::Lagged { skipped } => lagged(Some(skipped)),
    }
}

#[cfg(test)]
mod tests {
    use std::sync::Arc;

    use brocanth_events::RowChange;
    use brocanth_realtime::{Broker, Gate};
    use brocanth_server::websocket::Handshake;
    use brocanth_server::{Request, Server, Stopper};

    use super::*;

    /// A server whose one route is this loop, with every topic public.
    async fn open(broker: &Arc<Broker>) -> (WebSocket, Stopper) {
        let server = Server::bind("127.0.0.1:0").await.expect("a port");
        let addr = server.local_addr().expect("an address");
        let stopper = server.stopper();
        let broker = Arc::clone(broker);
        tokio::spawn(server.run(move |request: Request| {
            let broker = Arc::clone(&broker);
            async move {
                let handshake =
                    Handshake::read(|name| request.header(name), &[]).expect("a handshake");
                let client = broker.connect().expect("room");
                let everyone: Authorise =
                    Box::new(|topic| Ok(Subscription::new(topic, Gate::everyone())));
                handshake.response(move |socket| run(socket, client, everyone, Filed::new(), false))
            }
        }));
        let socket = websocket::connect(addr, "/", None).await.expect("open");
        (socket, stopper)
    }

    async fn next(socket: &mut WebSocket) -> Incoming {
        tokio::time::timeout(Duration::from_secs(5), socket.recv())
            .await
            .expect("a message in time")
    }

    #[tokio::test]
    async fn a_subscribed_socket_reads_a_published_change_and_a_close_frees_it() {
        let broker = Broker::new();
        let (mut socket, stopper) = open(&broker).await;
        socket.send_text("subscribe posts/*").await.expect("sent");
        assert_eq!(
            next(&mut socket).await,
            Incoming::Text("event:subscribed\ndata:posts/*\n\n".into())
        );

        broker.publish_change(&RowChange::created("posts", 42i64));
        let Incoming::Text(frame) = next(&mut socket).await else {
            panic!("a frame");
        };
        assert!(frame.starts_with("id:1\nevent:posts/*\n"), "{frame}");
        assert!(frame.contains("data:id=42\n"), "{frame}");

        socket.close(websocket::NORMAL, "done").await;
        for _ in 0..500 {
            if broker.clients() == 0 {
                break;
            }
            tokio::time::sleep(Duration::from_millis(10)).await;
        }
        assert_eq!(broker.clients(), 0, "the loop ended and dropped the client");
        stopper.stop();
    }

    #[tokio::test]
    async fn a_bad_command_is_refused_and_the_socket_stays_open() {
        let broker = Broker::new();
        let (mut socket, stopper) = open(&broker).await;
        socket.send_text("follow posts/*").await.expect("sent");
        let Incoming::Text(answer) = next(&mut socket).await else {
            panic!("an answer");
        };
        assert!(answer.starts_with("event:refused\n"), "{answer}");
        socket
            .send_text("unsubscribe posts/*")
            .await
            .expect("still open");
        assert_eq!(
            next(&mut socket).await,
            Incoming::Text("event:unsubscribed\ndata:posts/*\n\n".into())
        );
        stopper.stop();
    }

    #[tokio::test]
    async fn a_stopping_server_sends_going_away() {
        let broker = Broker::new();
        let (mut socket, stopper) = open(&broker).await;
        socket.send_text("subscribe posts/*").await.expect("sent");
        let _ = next(&mut socket).await;
        stopper.stop();
        assert_eq!(
            next(&mut socket).await,
            Incoming::Closed(Some(websocket::GOING_AWAY))
        );
    }
}
===== src/plugins/resource/mod.rs =====
//! Typed CRUD routes over your tables, with the policy in the `WHERE` clause.
//!
//! Written once by `brocanth create`. This file is yours.
//!
//! [`mount`] is the list of resources this application serves, and it starts
//! empty: a resource is one table, and a new project has none. Add one line
//! per resource. A resource is a `brocanth_resource::Resource`: an entity, a
//! policy over its rows (there is no default, so a resource without one does
//! not compile), a data source and three conversions. That crate's own
//! documentation has the worked example.
//!
//! `src/pages/mod.rs` calls [`mount`] at `/api`, so every route lands in the
//! one table. A page and a resource that claim one path are a build error.
//!
//! The three impls below make this project's `Ctx`, `Response` and `Error`
//! the page triple `brocanth_resource::pages` is written against. A resource
//! also implements `PageSubject<Ctx>`, which reads who is asking off a
//! request.

use brocanth::routing::{BuildError, RouterBuilder};
use brocanth_resource::pages::{PageCtx, PageError, PageResponse};

use crate::pages::{Ctx, Error, Handler, Response};

/// Every resource, under `prefix`.
///
/// Seven routes each: the five REST routes, and `POST /{id}` and
/// `POST /{id}/delete` for an HTML form, which can send nothing but GET and
/// POST. `brocanth_resource::pages::mount_api` mounts the five alone.
///
/// ```ignore
/// brocanth_resource::pages::mount(routes, &format!("{prefix}/posts"), Arc::new(Posts::new()))?;
/// ```
pub fn mount(routes: &mut RouterBuilder<Handler>, prefix: &str) -> Result<(), BuildError> {
    let _ = (routes, prefix);
    Ok(())
}

impl PageCtx for Ctx {
    fn query(&self) -> &str {
        &self.query
    }

    fn body(&self) -> &[u8] {
        &self.body
    }

    fn param(&self, name: &str) -> Option<&str> {
        Ctx::param(self, name)
    }

    fn header(&self, name: &str) -> Option<&str> {
        Ctx::header(self, name)
    }
}

impl PageResponse for Response {
    fn page_response(status: u16, body: String, headers: Vec<(String, String)>) -> Self {
        Response {
            status,
            body,
            headers,
            ..Default::default()
        }
    }
}

impl PageError for Error {
    fn page_error(status: u16, message: String) -> Self {
        Error::new(status, message)
    }
}
===== src/plugins/robots/mod.rs =====
//! The robots.txt, as this application answers it.
//!
//! Rendered once at startup from `Settings`, then served by
//! `src/pages/robots.txt.rs`. A rule path that does not start with `/`, or
//! a sitemap that is not a full URL, stops the build here: a crawler would
//! ignore that line, and the page it was meant to hide would be indexed.
//!
//! `index = false` answers `User-agent: *` / `Disallow: /` and nothing
//! else. That is the answer for development and staging.
//!
//! Only the root `/robots.txt` of a host is read. Under a base path, as on
//! a GitHub Pages project site, crawlers never ask for this file.

use std::sync::OnceLock;

use brocanth::plugin_api::{Lifecycle, Plugin, PluginError};
use brocanth::plugin_context::NoContext;
use brocanth_robots::{Group, Robots as Text};

/// The rendered text, set once at startup.
static RENDERED: OnceLock<String> = OnceLock::new();

/// The text, or `None` when this plugin was never registered.
pub fn text() -> Option<&'static str> {
    RENDERED.get().map(String::as_str)
}

/// What this plugin is built from.
///
/// It derives `Deserialize` because this application reads it from
/// `[robots]` in a TOML file. Drop the config plugin and the derive goes
/// with it: `Plugin::Config` carries no bounds the core imposes.
#[derive(Debug, serde::Deserialize)]
#[serde(default, deny_unknown_fields)]
pub struct Settings {
    /// Let crawlers read this site.
    pub index: bool,
    /// Paths no crawler may read.
    pub disallow: Vec<String>,
    /// Paths a crawler may read under a disallowed one.
    pub allow: Vec<String>,
    /// Where the sitemap is, as a full URL.
    pub sitemap: Option<String>,
    /// Groups for named crawlers, written after the `*` group. File-only:
    /// `[[robots.groups]]`.
    pub groups: Vec<CrawlerGroup>,
}

/// One `[[robots.groups]]` table.
#[derive(Debug, Clone, Default, serde::Deserialize)]
#[serde(default, deny_unknown_fields)]
pub struct CrawlerGroup {
    /// The crawlers this group names, by product token: "GPTBot".
    pub agents: Vec<String>,
    /// Paths these crawlers may read under a disallowed one.
    pub allow: Vec<String>,
    /// Paths these crawlers may not read.
    pub disallow: Vec<String>,
}

impl Default for Settings {
    /// Open: a production server with no `[robots]` section is indexed.
    fn default() -> Self {
        Self {
            index: true,
            disallow: Vec::new(),
            allow: Vec::new(),
            sitemap: None,
            groups: Vec::new(),
        }
    }
}

pub struct Robots {
    settings: Settings,
}

impl Robots {
    /// The builder these settings describe.
    fn text(&self) -> Result<Text, PluginError> {
        if !self.settings.index {
            return Ok(Text::closed());
        }
        let mut text = Text::new();
        // The admin, where src/modules/admin.rs mounts it. Turn it off with
        // `integrate.admin` in src/modules/robots.rs.
        if crate::modules::robots::MODULE.integrate.admin {
            text = text.disallow(crate::modules::admin::MODULE.mount_at);
        }
        for path in &self.settings.disallow {
            text = text.disallow(path.as_str());
        }
        for path in &self.settings.allow {
            text = text.allow(path.as_str());
        }
        for named in &self.settings.groups {
            let Some((first, rest)) = named.agents.split_first() else {
                return Err(PluginError::new(
                    "[robots] a [[robots.groups]] table names no crawler\n\
                     help: give it agents = [\"GPTBot\"], or delete the table",
                ));
            };
            let mut group = Group::new(first.as_str());
            for agent in rest {
                group = group.agent(agent.as_str());
            }
            for path in &named.disallow {
                group = group.disallow(path.as_str());
            }
            for path in &named.allow {
                group = group.allow(path.as_str());
            }
            text = text.group(group);
        }
        // Unset, the line names the sitemap this application serves.
        let own = self.settings.sitemap.as_deref();
        let sitemap = own.or(crate::plugins::sitemap::location());
        if let Some(url) = sitemap {
            text = text.sitemap(url);
        }
        Ok(text)
    }

    /// The text these settings describe, or why it cannot be served.
    fn rendered(&self) -> Result<String, PluginError> {
        self.text()?
            .render()
            .map_err(|e| PluginError::new(format!("[robots] {e}")))
    }
}

impl Plugin for Robots {
    type Config = Settings;
    type Provides = NoContext;
    const NAME: &'static str = "robots";

    fn new(config: Settings) -> Self {
        Self { settings: config }
    }

    // After `configure`: the sitemap plugin sets its URL there, and every
    // plugin finishes one stage before any plugin starts the next.
    fn after_config(&mut self, _lc: &mut Lifecycle<'_>) -> Result<(), PluginError> {
        let _ = RENDERED.set(self.rendered()?);
        Ok(())
    }
}

#[cfg(test)]
mod tests {
    use super::*;

    fn ai_crawlers() -> CrawlerGroup {
        CrawlerGroup {
            agents: vec!["GPTBot".to_string(), "CCBot".to_string()],
            disallow: vec!["/".to_string()],
            ..CrawlerGroup::default()
        }
    }

    #[test]
    fn closed_settings_answer_disallow_everything_and_nothing_else() {
        let robots = Robots::new(Settings {
            index: false,
            groups: vec![ai_crawlers()],
            ..Settings::default()
        });
        assert_eq!(robots.rendered().unwrap(), "User-agent: *\nDisallow: /\n");
    }

    #[test]
    fn open_settings_write_each_rule_then_each_group() {
        let robots = Robots::new(Settings {
            disallow: vec!["/private".to_string()],
            allow: vec!["/private/help".to_string()],
            sitemap: Some("https://example.com/sitemap.xml".to_string()),
            groups: vec![ai_crawlers()],
            ..Settings::default()
        });
        let text = robots.rendered().unwrap();
        assert!(text.contains("Disallow: /private\n"), "{text}");
        assert!(text.contains("Allow: /private/help\n"), "{text}");
        assert!(
            text.contains("\nUser-agent: GPTBot\nUser-agent: CCBot\nDisallow: /\n"),
            "{text}"
        );
        assert!(
            text.ends_with("Sitemap: https://example.com/sitemap.xml\n"),
            "{text}"
        );
    }

    #[test]
    fn a_group_with_no_crawler_stops_the_build() {
        let robots = Robots::new(Settings {
            groups: vec![CrawlerGroup::default()],
            ..Settings::default()
        });
        let error = robots.rendered().unwrap_err().to_string();
        assert!(error.contains("help:"), "{error}");
    }
}
===== src/plugins/roles/mod.rs =====
//! Roles: named sets of permissions, given to accounts.
//!
//! Written once by `brocanth create`. This file is yours.
//!
//! A permission is a string. The admin asks for `<model>.view`, `.add`,
//! `.change` and `.delete` for a model, and `<panel>.view` and `.act` for a
//! tab. A page of yours may ask for any other. A superuser holds every
//! permission. Only a superuser changes roles, in the admin's Roles tab.
//!
//! Who may see a page is a role question:
//!
//! ```ignore
//! let staff = crate::plugins::roles::require(ctx, "report.view").await?;
//! ```
//!
//! Which rows is a row question, and it goes into the query through
//! `brocanth-policy`. `Staff::gate` turns a permission into a predicate, so
//! both travel in one `condition()`:
//!
//! ```ignore
//! fn update(&self, who: &Visitor) -> EntityPred<Post> {
//!     // Editors change every post; everybody else changes their own.
//!     who.staff.gate("post.change").or(Pred::eq(PostColumn::AuthorId, who.id))
//! }
//! ```

use brocanth_auth::Staff;

use crate::pages::{Ctx, Error};

/// The signed-in member of staff, when it holds `permission`.
///
/// # Errors
/// 401 when nobody on the staff is signed in, 403 when the account lacks
/// the permission.
#[allow(dead_code)] // for your pages
pub async fn require(ctx: &Ctx, permission: &str) -> Result<Staff, Error> {
    let Some(staff) = crate::plugins::superuser::staff(ctx).await? else {
        return Err(Error::new(401, "sign in with a staff account"));
    };
    if !staff.allows(permission) {
        return Err(Error::new(
            403,
            format!("this needs the permission {permission}"),
        ));
    }
    Ok(staff)
}
===== src/plugins/sessions/mod.rs =====
//! Cookie sessions, and the one value they put on the context.

use brocanth::plugin_api::{Lifecycle, MiddlewareCatalog, Plugin, PluginError};
use brocanth::plugin_context::{ContextBuilder, ContextItem, ProvidesContext};
use brocanth::plugin_routes::BoxFuture;
use brocanth::routing::AsyncNext;

use crate::pages::{Ctx, Response};

/// What this plugin is built from.
///
/// It derives `Deserialize` because this application reads it from
/// `[sessions]` in a TOML file. Drop the config plugin and the derive goes
/// with it: `Plugin::Config` carries no bounds the core imposes.
#[derive(Debug, serde::Deserialize)]
#[serde(default, deny_unknown_fields)]
pub struct Settings {
    pub cookie: String,
    /// `Secure`, so the cookie never leaves over plain HTTP. False in
    /// development and nowhere else.
    pub secure: bool,
}

impl Default for Settings {
    fn default() -> Self {
        Self {
            cookie: "_my_blog_session".to_string(),
            secure: true,
        }
    }
}

/// What this plugin puts on the context.
///
/// One provider owns one type, so there is no string name to misspell and no
/// `responds_to?` guard to forget. A handler that asks for a `Session` in an
/// application that never registered this plugin does not compile.
#[derive(Debug, Default)]
pub struct Session {
    pub cookie: String,
}

impl ContextItem for Session {}

pub struct Sessions {
    cookie: String,
    secure: bool,
}

impl Plugin for Sessions {
    type Config = Settings;
    type Provides = ProvidesContext<Session>;
    const NAME: &'static str = "sessions";

    fn new(config: Settings) -> Self {
        Self {
            cookie: config.cookie,
            secure: config.secure,
        }
    }

    fn configure(&mut self, _lc: &mut Lifecycle<'_>) -> Result<(), PluginError> {
        if self.cookie.is_empty() {
            // A missing or ill-typed key fails at startup, not at first use,
            // and the plugin never quietly chooses a name the operator did not.
            return Err(PluginError::new("[sessions] cookie must not be empty"));
        }
        Ok(())
    }

    fn configure_router(&mut self, catalog: &mut MiddlewareCatalog<'_>) -> Result<(), PluginError> {
        catalog.offer(
            "session",
            "reads the cookie and puts a Session on the context",
        );
        Ok(())
    }

    fn before_start(&mut self, _lc: &mut Lifecycle<'_>) -> Result<(), PluginError> {
        if !self.secure {
            crate::plugins::say!(warn, "[sessions] Secure is off: development only");
        }
        Ok(())
    }

    fn provide(&self, cx: &mut ContextBuilder) {
        cx.insert(Session {
            cookie: self.cookie.clone(),
        });
    }
}

/// The layer `session` mounts. Reading the cookie off the request and writing
/// `Set-Cookie` on the way out both need the request type that arrives with the
/// HTTP server; the seam is here and it is already the right shape.
///
/// A layer may wait, which is what a session store wants: the lookup is
/// awaited here and holds no thread.
pub fn layer<'a>(ctx: &'a mut Ctx, next: AsyncNext<'a, Ctx, Response>) -> BoxFuture<'a, Response> {
    next.run(ctx)
}
===== src/plugins/sitemap/mod.rs =====
//! The sitemap.xml, as this application answers it.
//!
//! Rendered once at startup from the page table and `Settings`, then
//! served by `src/pages/sitemap.xml.rs`. Every GET page with no parameter
//! is listed, except a file (a last segment with a dot, such as
//! `/robots.txt`) and the paths under `exclude`. A route with a parameter
//! is many pages: list the ones that exist in `extra`.
//!
//! A base URL that is not a full `http(s)` address stops the build here: a
//! crawler ignores every relative `<loc>`.
//!
//! To give a page a `<lastmod>`, call `path_modified` in `sitemap` below.

use std::sync::OnceLock;

use brocanth::plugin_api::{Lifecycle, Plugin, PluginError};
use brocanth::plugin_context::NoContext;
use brocanth::routing::Verb;
use brocanth_robots::sitemap::{listed, under, Sitemap as Xml};

/// The rendered XML, set once at `configure`.
static RENDERED: OnceLock<String> = OnceLock::new();

/// The full URL of `/sitemap.xml`, set once at `configure`.
static LOCATION: OnceLock<String> = OnceLock::new();

/// The XML, or `None` when this plugin was never registered.
pub fn text() -> Option<&'static str> {
    RENDERED.get().map(String::as_str)
}

/// The full URL of `/sitemap.xml`, for the `Sitemap:` line of a
/// robots.txt. `None` before `configure`.
#[allow(dead_code)]
pub fn location() -> Option<&'static str> {
    LOCATION.get().map(String::as_str)
}

/// What this plugin is built from.
///
/// It derives `Deserialize` because this application reads it from
/// `[sitemap]` in a TOML file. Drop the config plugin and the derive goes
/// with it: `Plugin::Config` carries no bounds the core imposes.
#[derive(Debug, serde::Deserialize)]
#[serde(default, deny_unknown_fields)]
pub struct Settings {
    /// The site's address: scheme, host, and base path if any.
    pub base_url: String,
    /// More paths to list.
    pub extra: Vec<String>,
    /// Path prefixes kept out of the sitemap.
    pub exclude: Vec<String>,
}

impl Default for Settings {
    fn default() -> Self {
        Self {
            base_url: "http://localhost:3000".to_string(),
            extra: Vec::new(),
            exclude: vec!["/status".to_string(), "/realtime".to_string()],
        }
    }
}

pub struct Sitemap {
    settings: Settings,
}

impl Sitemap {
    /// The builder these settings and these routes describe.
    fn sitemap<'a>(&self, routes: impl IntoIterator<Item = (Verb, &'a str)>) -> Xml {
        let kept = |path: &str| !self.settings.exclude.iter().any(|p| under(path, p));
        let mut xml = Xml::new(self.settings.base_url.as_str());
        for (verb, pattern) in routes {
            if verb == Verb::Get && listed(pattern) && kept(pattern) {
                xml = xml.path(pattern);
            }
        }
        for path in &self.settings.extra {
            if kept(path) {
                xml = xml.path(path.as_str());
            }
        }
        xml
    }
}

impl Plugin for Sitemap {
    type Config = Settings;
    type Provides = NoContext;
    const NAME: &'static str = "sitemap";

    fn new(mut config: Settings) -> Self {
        // The admin, where src/modules/admin.rs mounts it: no page a
        // visitor reads.
        let admin = crate::modules::admin::MODULE.mount_at.to_string();
        if !config.exclude.contains(&admin) {
            config.exclude.push(admin);
        }
        Self { settings: config }
    }

    fn configure(&mut self, _lc: &mut Lifecycle<'_>) -> Result<(), PluginError> {
        let routes = crate::pages::handlers()
            .iter()
            .map(|(verb, pattern, _)| (*verb, *pattern));
        let xml = self.sitemap(routes);
        let text = xml
            .render()
            .map_err(|e| PluginError::new(format!("[sitemap] {e}")))?;
        let _ = RENDERED.set(text);
        let _ = LOCATION.set(xml.location());
        Ok(())
    }
}

#[cfg(test)]
mod tests {
    use super::*;

    const ROUTES: &[(Verb, &str)] = &[
        (Verb::Get, "/"),
        (Verb::Get, "/about"),
        (Verb::Post, "/signup"),
        (Verb::Get, "/users/{id}"),
        (Verb::Get, "/robots.txt"),
        (Verb::Get, "/drafts/one"),
    ];

    fn rendered(settings: Settings) -> Result<String, String> {
        let plugin = Sitemap::new(settings);
        plugin
            .sitemap(ROUTES.iter().copied())
            .render()
            .map_err(|e| e.to_string())
    }

    #[test]
    fn pages_with_no_parameter_are_listed_and_the_rest_are_not() {
        let xml = rendered(Settings {
            base_url: "https://example.com".to_string(),
            extra: vec!["/users/7".to_string()],
            exclude: vec!["/drafts".to_string()],
        })
        .unwrap();
        for present in ["/</loc>", "/about</loc>", "/users/7</loc>"] {
            let loc = format!("<loc>https://example.com{present}");
            assert!(xml.contains(&loc), "{loc} in {xml}");
        }
        for absent in ["/signup", "{id}", "robots.txt", "/drafts"] {
            assert!(!xml.contains(absent), "{absent} in {xml}");
        }
    }

    #[test]
    fn a_relative_base_url_stops_the_build_with_the_fix() {
        let error = rendered(Settings {
            base_url: "example.com".to_string(),
            ..Settings::default()
        })
        .unwrap_err();
        assert!(error.contains("help:"), "{error}");
    }
}
===== src/plugins/static_files/mod.rs =====
//! The static files, before the router: a server wrap.
//!
//! Not a page, and not routed. A page returns a `Response` whose body is a
//! `String`, which a font is not, so a static file answers before the router
//! is consulted and never touches the page contract. `src/plugins/mod.rs`
//! composes this wrap with any other.

use crate::plugins::{Answer, Handler, ServerWrap};

/// Names this wrap in `src/plugins/mod.rs`.
pub struct StaticFiles;

impl ServerWrap for StaticFiles {
    fn wrap(app: &std::sync::Arc<crate::app::App>, next: Handler) -> Handler {
        let _ = app;
        std::sync::Arc::new(move |request: brocanth_server::Request| -> Answer {
            match serve_asset(&request) {
                Some(file) => Box::pin(std::future::ready(file)),
                None => next(request),
            }
        })
    }
}

/// The file a request named, or `None` to let the router answer.
///
/// Checked before the route table, which is also the order of surprise: a
/// page at the same path as a file would be shadowed, and a path under
/// `/assets` or `/public` is not a route anybody writes by hand.
fn serve_asset(request: &brocanth_server::Request) -> Option<brocanth_server::Response> {
    if !matches!(
        request.verb,
        brocanth::routing::Verb::Get | brocanth::routing::Verb::Head
    ) {
        return None;
    }
    let asset = crate::assets::lookup(&request.path)?;

    // The ETag is the content hash, so it is strong and a match means the
    // bytes are identical. 304 is the whole point of serving `public/`
    // without a fingerprint in the name.
    if request.header("if-none-match") == Some(asset.etag) {
        return Some(
            brocanth_server::Response::new(304, asset.content_type, &[][..])
                .with_header("etag", asset.etag)
                .with_header("cache-control", asset.cache_control()),
        );
    }

    Some(
        brocanth_server::Response::new(200, asset.content_type, asset.body)
            .with_header("etag", asset.etag)
            .with_header("cache-control", asset.cache_control()),
    )
}
===== src/plugins/storage/mod.rs =====
//! File storage, as this application registers it.
//!
//! An object is named by what is in it. Thirty-two bytes rendered as
//! sixty-four hex characters is the whole address, so `..`, a slash and a
//! drive letter are not values the type can hold and no filename a client
//! sent ever becomes a path component. The original name is kept as metadata,
//! defanged for the `Content-Disposition` header it ends up in, and never
//! touches the filesystem.
//!
//! `brocanth-storage` is in `Cargo.toml` because this file is here, and
//! through no other route. Delete the registration in `src/plugins/mod.rs`
//! and delete this file, and the application stores no files and links
//! nothing to store them with.
//!
//! # Uploading
//!
//! There is no upload route here, on purpose: `brocanth-storage` parses no
//! multipart body and knows nothing about HTTP, and whether *this*
//! application lets *this* request store a file is a policy question a
//! generator cannot answer. A handler that has already asked it writes:
//!
//! ```ignore
//! let files = ctx.provided::<FileStore, _>().expect("a dispatched request");
//! let about = ObjectInfo::new()
//!     .with_filename(sent_name)
//!     .with_declared_type(sent_type);
//! let key = files.store.put(&bytes, &about).await?;
//! ```
//!
//! and keeps `key.to_hex()` on the record. The media type is taken from the
//! bytes rather than from `sent_type`, because a client that labels a file
//! `text/html` is choosing what this origin serves back to the next visitor.
//!
//! # Serving
//!
//! Also the application's, and for a sharper reason: a file token says "the
//! bearer may read this object" and carries no identity at all. Mint one only
//! for a request that has already passed the policy, because after that the
//! token is the only thing between the object and whoever the URL reaches.
//!
//! ```ignore
//! let token = files.tokens.issue(&key);           // after the policy said yes
//! let key = files.tokens.verify(presented)?;      // on the download route
//! let meta = files.store.meta(&key).await?;
//! ```
//!
//! `ObjectMeta::content_type` answers `is_safe_to_inline`, which is the bit
//! the download route needs to force an attachment for everything that is not
//! a recognised image.
//!
//! # Thumbnails
//!
//! `get_variant` and `put_variant` are here already, with the `variants`
//! feature off: moving derived bytes needs no codec, so this build can serve
//! a variant another process computed and cannot compute one itself. Turning
//! the feature on in `Cargo.toml` adds `derive_variant`, and adds png, jpeg,
//! gif and webp decoders pointed at stranger-supplied bytes. An application
//! that stores documents should leave it off.
//!
//! # One secret, two keys
//!
//! `secret` below is used twice and the two uses cannot be confused for each
//! other: `HashKey` and `FileTokenKey` derive from it under different domain
//! separators, so an address key cannot sign a token and a token key cannot
//! address an object. Rotating the secret invalidates every outstanding token
//! at once, which is the break-glass, and it also changes the address new
//! uploads are stored at -- objects already on disk keep the address they
//! were written under, so rotate it with a plan rather than on a Friday.

use std::sync::Arc;

use brocanth::plugin_api::{Lifecycle, Plugin, PluginError};
use brocanth::plugin_context::{ContextBuilder, ContextItem, ProvidesContext};
use brocanth_storage::{FileTokenKey, FileTokens, HashKey, LocalDisk, Storage};

/// What this plugin is built from.
///
/// It derives `Deserialize` because this application reads it from
/// `[storage]` in a TOML file. Drop the config plugin and the derive goes
/// with it: `Plugin::Config` carries no bounds the core imposes.
#[derive(Debug, serde::Deserialize)]
#[serde(default, deny_unknown_fields)]
pub struct Settings {
    /// Where the objects live, relative to the working directory or absolute.
    /// Created if it is not there.
    pub root: String,
    /// The deployment secret. It signs file tokens and keys object addresses;
    /// see the module header for what each of those means.
    pub secret: String,
    /// How many seconds a file token lives. A token cannot be revoked, so
    /// this is the leak window.
    pub token_ttl: i64,
}

impl Default for Settings {
    fn default() -> Self {
        Self {
            root: "var/uploads".to_string(),
            secret: "development only: replace before anything is uploaded in anger".to_string(),
            token_ttl: 120,
        }
    }
}

/// What this plugin puts on the context.
///
/// A wrapper and not the two values loose, because `ContextItem` is
/// brocanth's trait and both of these are `brocanth-storage`'s types. That
/// crate deliberately does not depend on the core -- it has no routes, parses
/// no multipart body and knows nothing about HTTP -- so the impl that joins
/// the two belongs in the one crate that can see both, which is this one.
pub struct FileStore {
    /// The store, behind the trait rather than as a `LocalDisk`, so swapping
    /// the backend is a change in this file and nowhere else.
    ///
    /// Allowed to be unread, and only until one thing changes. Every
    /// `Storage` method is `async` and the startup line below is not, so
    /// nothing the generator writes can touch this field — a handler is
    /// what uses it, and a handler cannot reach the per-request context
    /// yet, because `Ctx` in `src/pages/mod.rs` has no extractor. Delete
    /// this attribute the moment you write the first upload route; it is
    /// a note about today, not about the field.
    #[allow(dead_code)]
    pub store: Arc<dyn Storage>,
    /// Mints and verifies the short-lived tokens a download route checks.
    pub tokens: FileTokens,
}

impl ContextItem for FileStore {}

pub struct Files {
    settings: Settings,
    /// Built at `configure`, because opening a store can fail and
    /// `Plugin::new` cannot.
    store: Option<Arc<dyn Storage>>,
    tokens: Option<FileTokens>,
}

impl Plugin for Files {
    type Config = Settings;
    type Provides = ProvidesContext<FileStore>;
    const NAME: &'static str = "storage";

    fn new(config: Settings) -> Self {
        Self {
            settings: config,
            store: None,
            tokens: None,
        }
    }

    fn configure(&mut self, lc: &mut Lifecycle<'_>) -> Result<(), PluginError> {
        if self.settings.root.is_empty() {
            return Err(PluginError::new(
                "[storage] root is required; there is no default directory",
            ));
        }
        if self.settings.secret.is_empty() {
            // An empty secret is not a weaker store, it is two different
            // stores: addresses stop being keyed, and a token key of no bytes
            // signs tokens anybody can forge.
            return Err(PluginError::new(
                "[storage] secret is required; it signs file tokens and keys object addresses",
            ));
        }
        if self.settings.token_ttl <= 0 {
            return Err(PluginError::new(
                "[storage] token_ttl must be at least one second, or no token is ever valid",
            ));
        }
        // Synchronous: `std::fs`, a `create_dir_all` and a `canonicalize`.
        // Unlike a pool there is nothing to open, so there is no
        // `before_start`.
        let disk = LocalDisk::open(&self.settings.root)
            .map_err(|e| {
                PluginError::new(format!("[storage] opening {:?}: {e}", self.settings.root))
            })?
            .with_hash_key(HashKey::from_secret(self.settings.secret.as_bytes()));
        let store: Arc<dyn Storage> = Arc::new(disk);
        let tokens = FileTokens::new(FileTokenKey::from_secret(self.settings.secret.as_bytes()))
            .with_ttl(self.settings.token_ttl);
        // On the services too, where a plugin registered after this one
        // reads them at its own `configure`: the upload and download routes
        // of `storage-routes` do.
        lc.services_mut().insert(Arc::clone(&store));
        lc.services_mut().insert(tokens.clone());
        self.store = Some(store);
        self.tokens = Some(tokens);
        Ok(())
    }

    fn provide(&self, cx: &mut ContextBuilder) {
        // `configure` runs before the server is handed the application, so
        // both are here by the time any request builds a context.
        if let (Some(store), Some(tokens)) = (&self.store, &self.tokens) {
            cx.insert(FileStore {
                store: store.clone(),
                tokens: tokens.clone(),
            });
        }
    }
}
===== src/plugins/storage_routes/mod.rs =====
//! The upload and download routes, as this application answers them.
//!
//! `POST /files` takes a `multipart/form-data` form, stores its `file` part
//! and answers `303` to the form's `return_to` field, with `?file=` and
//! `&token=` added, or `201` with the address. `GET /files/{address}` serves
//! the file to a request whose `?token=` opens it; add `&thumb=300x200` for
//! a thumbnail. The path, `public` and `thumbnails` are in
//! `src/modules/storage_routes.rs`.
//!
//! A plain form works with no script. The CSRF check reads no multipart
//! body, so the `lift` hook copies the form's `_csrf_token` field into the
//! header the check reads, before it runs:
//!
//! ```html
//! <form method="post" action="/files" enctype="multipart/form-data">
//!   <input type="hidden" name="return_to" value="/posts/new">
//!   <input type="file" name="file">
//!   <button>Upload</button>
//! </form>
//! ```
//!
//! A page shows a stored file with a link it mints after its own policy
//! allowed the read:
//!
//! ```ignore
//! let files = ctx.provided::<crate::plugins::storage::FileStore, _>().expect("dispatched");
//! let token = files.tokens.issue(&key);
//! let src = format!("/files/{}?token={}", key.to_hex(), token.as_str());
//! ```

use std::sync::Arc;

use brocanth::plugin_api::{Lifecycle, Plugin, PluginError};
use brocanth::plugin_context::NoContext;
use brocanth::plugin_hooks::{HookRegistrar, HookSource, OnAsyncRequest, Request, RequestState};
use brocanth::plugin_order::Order;
use brocanth_storage::routes::{FileRoutes, RoutesConfig};
use brocanth_storage::{FileTokens, Storage};

/// Whether this request may store a file.
///
/// It answers no, so nobody can upload until you say who may. The request
/// has passed the session and the CSRF check by now; read the signed-in
/// account from `state` and ask your policy. A "yes" for everyone is a free
/// disk for every stranger.
fn may_upload(_request: &Request, _state: &RequestState) -> bool {
    false
}

/// What this plugin is built from.
///
/// It derives `Deserialize` because this application reads it from
/// `[storage_routes]` in a TOML file. Drop the config plugin and the derive goes
/// with it: `Plugin::Config` carries no bounds the core imposes.
#[derive(Debug, serde::Deserialize)]
#[serde(default, deny_unknown_fields)]
pub struct Settings {
    /// The largest file accepted, in bytes.
    pub max_bytes: usize,
    /// The form field that carries the CSRF token: `[csrf] field`.
    pub csrf_field: String,
    /// The header the CSRF check reads: `[csrf] header`.
    pub csrf_header: String,
}

impl Default for Settings {
    fn default() -> Self {
        Self {
            max_bytes: 2_097_152,
            csrf_field: "_csrf_token".to_string(),
            csrf_header: "x-csrf-token".to_string(),
        }
    }
}

pub struct StorageRoutes {
    settings: Settings,
    /// Built at `configure`, from the store the storage plugin published.
    routes: Option<FileRoutes>,
}

impl Plugin for StorageRoutes {
    type Config = Settings;
    type Provides = NoContext;
    const NAME: &'static str = "storage_routes";
    const INSTALLS_HOOKS: bool = true;

    fn new(config: Settings) -> Self {
        Self {
            settings: config,
            routes: None,
        }
    }

    fn configure(&mut self, lc: &mut Lifecycle<'_>) -> Result<(), PluginError> {
        let store = lc.services().get::<Arc<dyn Storage>>().cloned();
        let tokens = lc.services().get::<FileTokens>().cloned();
        let (Some(store), Some(tokens)) = (store, tokens) else {
            return Err(PluginError::new(
                "[storage_routes] no file store is registered\n\
                 help: register the storage plugin before this one in src/app.rs",
            ));
        };
        let config = RoutesConfig {
            path: crate::modules::storage_routes::MODULE.path.to_string(),
            max_bytes: self.settings.max_bytes,
            public: crate::modules::storage_routes::MODULE.public,
            thumbnails: crate::modules::storage_routes::MODULE.thumbnails,
            csrf_field: self.settings.csrf_field.clone(),
            csrf_header: self.settings.csrf_header.clone(),
            ..RoutesConfig::default()
        };
        self.routes = Some(FileRoutes::new(store, tokens, config, may_upload)?);
        Ok(())
    }
}

impl HookSource for StorageRoutes {
    type Families = OnAsyncRequest;

    fn install_hooks(&self, registrar: &mut HookRegistrar<'_>) -> Result<(), PluginError> {
        if let Some(routes) = &self.routes {
            registrar.request_async(
                "lift",
                Order::new().before_if_present("csrf.check"),
                routes.csrf_lift(),
            );
            // After the CSRF check, so an upload from another site stops
            // there, and after the proxy reader and the rate limiter.
            registrar.request_async(
                "answer",
                Order::new()
                    .after_if_present("csrf.check")
                    .after_if_present("proxy-headers.read")
                    .after_if_present("rate_limit.count"),
                routes.clone(),
            );
        }
        Ok(())
    }
}

#[cfg(test)]
mod tests {
    use brocanth::routing::Verb;
    use brocanth_server::Request;

    // Where src/modules/storage_routes.rs puts the routes, so a moved path
    // is tested where it went.
    const PATH: &str = crate::modules::storage_routes::MODULE.path;

    #[tokio::test]
    async fn nobody_may_upload_until_may_upload_says_who() {
        let app = crate::app::build().expect("the application builds");
        let mut request = Request::new(Verb::Post, PATH)
            .with_header("content-type", "multipart/form-data; boundary=b");
        request.body = b"--b--\r\n".to_vec().into();
        let response = crate::app::handle(&app, request).await;
        assert_eq!(response.status, 403);
    }

    #[tokio::test]
    async fn a_file_is_served_only_with_a_token() {
        let app = crate::app::build().expect("the application builds");
        let address = "0".repeat(64);
        let request = Request::new(Verb::Get, format!("{PATH}/{address}"));
        let response = crate::app::handle(&app, request).await;
        assert_eq!(response.status, 403);
    }
}
===== src/plugins/superuser/mod.rs =====
//! Superusers: operator accounts that pass every check of the admin.
//!
//! Written once by `brocanth create`. This file is yours.
//!
//! A superuser is a flag on an account. An operator sets it, never a form a
//! visitor can reach:
//!
//! * at start, from `[superuser] email` and the password in the variable
//!   that `password_env` names, when `email` is set;
//! * with `createsuperuser --email <address>`, when the accounts are in
//!   Postgres.
//!
//! Both are idempotent. An account that exists is promoted and keeps its
//! password, so a second run does not reset a password somebody changed.
//!
//! [`staff`] reads the signed-in member of staff off a request: a
//! superuser, or an account that holds a role. The admin uses it as its
//! principal. A page of yours can too:
//!
//! ```ignore
//! let Some(staff) = crate::plugins::superuser::staff(ctx).await? else {
//!     return Err(Error::new(403, "staff only"));
//! };
//! ```

use std::sync::{Arc, LazyLock, OnceLock};

use brocanth::plugin_api::{Lifecycle, Plugin, PluginError};
use brocanth::plugin_context::NoContext;
use brocanth_auth::{ensure_superuser, Staff, SuperuserOutcome};

use crate::pages::{Ctx, Error};
use crate::plugins::auth::{Services, Store};

/// The superuser the server makes at start: `[superuser]` in the config
/// file, or `$<APP>_SUPERUSER__<KEY>`.
///
/// It derives `Deserialize` because this application reads it from
/// `[superuser]` in a TOML file. Drop the config plugin and the derive goes
/// with it: `Plugin::Config` carries no bounds the core imposes.
#[derive(Debug, serde::Deserialize)]
#[serde(default, deny_unknown_fields)]
pub struct Settings {
    /// The superuser's address. `None` makes no superuser at start.
    pub email: Option<String>,
    /// The *name* of the variable that holds its password, not the
    /// password.
    pub password_env: String,
}

impl Default for Settings {
    fn default() -> Self {
        Self {
            email: None,
            password_env: "MY_BLOG_SUPERUSER_PASSWORD".to_string(),
        }
    }
}

/// The accounts store, once the auth plugin has built it. The admin's
/// roles tab reads it: the admin is built with the route table, before any
/// plugin starts.
static STORE: LazyLock<Arc<OnceLock<Arc<Store>>>> = LazyLock::new(Default::default);

/// The cell [`STORE`] fills at startup.
#[allow(dead_code)] // read by the roles tab, with `roles`
pub fn store() -> Arc<OnceLock<Arc<Store>>> {
    Arc::clone(&STORE)
}

/// The signed-in member of staff, or `None`: no session, a pre-login one,
/// or an account that is neither a superuser nor holds a role.
///
/// # Errors
/// A store that fails is a 500. A missing or forged cookie is `None`.
#[allow(dead_code)] // read by the admin, with `admin`
pub async fn staff(ctx: &Ctx) -> Result<Option<Staff>, Error> {
    let (Some(auth), Some(cookies)) = (ctx.provided::<Services, _>(), ctx.header("cookie")) else {
        return Ok(None);
    };
    Staff::from_cookie_header(auth, cookies)
        .await
        .map_err(|e| Error::internal(format!("superuser: {e}")))
}

pub struct Superuser {
    settings: Settings,
    /// Read in `configure`, used once in `start`.
    password: Option<String>,
}

impl Plugin for Superuser {
    type Config = Settings;
    type Provides = NoContext;
    const NAME: &'static str = "superuser";

    fn new(config: Settings) -> Self {
        Self {
            settings: config,
            password: None,
        }
    }

    fn configure(&mut self, _lc: &mut Lifecycle<'_>) -> Result<(), PluginError> {
        if self.settings.email.is_none() {
            return Ok(());
        }
        let name = &self.settings.password_env;
        match std::env::var(name) {
            Ok(password) if !password.is_empty() => self.password = Some(password),
            // This project's own tests build the application with no secret
            // in the environment, and make no superuser.
            _ if cfg!(test) => {}
            _ => {
                return Err(PluginError::new(format!(
                    "[superuser] email is set and ${name} is not\n\
                     help: export {name}=<a long passphrase>, or unset [superuser] email"
                )));
            }
        }
        Ok(())
    }

    fn before_start(&mut self, lc: &mut Lifecycle<'_>) -> Result<(), PluginError> {
        let services = lc.require_service::<Services>()?;
        let _ = STORE.set(Arc::clone(services.store()));
        Ok(())
    }

    fn start(&mut self, lc: &mut Lifecycle<'_>) -> Result<(), PluginError> {
        let (Some(email), Some(password)) = (self.settings.email.clone(), self.password.take())
        else {
            return Ok(());
        };
        let services = lc.require_service::<Services>()?.clone();
        // `start` runs after the startup gate, so the auth tables exist.
        tokio::spawn(async move {
            let outcome = ensure_superuser(
                services.accounts(),
                services.store().as_ref(),
                &email,
                &password,
            )
            .await;
            match outcome {
                Ok(SuperuserOutcome::Created(_)) => {
                    crate::plugins::say!(info, "superuser: created {email}"; %email);
                }
                Ok(SuperuserOutcome::Promoted(_)) => {
                    crate::plugins::say!(info, "superuser: promoted {email}"; %email);
                }
                Ok(SuperuserOutcome::Unchanged(_)) => {}
                Err(e) => {
                    crate::plugins::say!(warn, "superuser: {email}: {e}"; %email, error = %e);
                }
            }
        });
        Ok(())
    }
}
===== src/plugins/validation/mod.rs =====
//! Request validation, as this application registers it.
//!
//! The shape of a schema is checked when this crate compiles; the data is
//! checked when a request arrives, because a request body is bytes that did
//! not exist at compile time. What the derive buys is that the check and the
//! description come from one declaration, so a form, an OpenAPI document and
//! the check on the way in cannot drift apart.
//!
//! `brocanth-schema` is in `Cargo.toml` because this file is here, and
//! through no other route. Delete the registration in `src/plugins/mod.rs`
//! and delete this file, and the application validates nothing and links
//! nothing to validate with.
//!
//! # The proof, and why a handler asks for it
//!
//! `Valid<T>` has a private field and exactly one constructor:
//! `Validation::check`, a method on the context item this plugin provides.
//! A handler that takes a `Valid<SignUp>` therefore has proof the body was
//! checked, and a handler in an application that never registered this plugin
//! does not compile. Forgetting to wire validation is a build that does not
//! finish rather than a bug that reaches production.
//!
//! ```ignore
//! let checks = ctx
//!     .provided::<brocanth_schema::Validation, _>()
//!     .expect("a dispatched request");
//! match checks.check(SignUp { username, email }) {
//!     Ok(body) => create(body),
//!     Err(issues) => Response {
//!         status: 422,
//!         body: checks.render(&issues),
//!         ..Default::default()
//!     },
//! }
//! ```
//!
//! # Where schemas go
//!
//! `SignUp` is here so that this file compiles into something that runs. Move
//! it, and whatever follows it, into `src/schemas/` once there is more than
//! one. Nothing registers a schema: a type with `#[derive(Validate)]` is
//! reachable from wherever it is written.
//!
//! # The middleware this does not mount
//!
//! `ValidationPlugin` offers a middleware called `validation.report`, and
//! this plugin does not pass the offer on. The offer is a name; the crate
//! ships no function behind it, because turning an unhandled report into a
//! 422 means knowing what this application's `Response` is. Mounting a name
//! with no layer behind it stops the application at startup, so the offer is
//! withheld until there is something to mount. Write the layer in this file,
//! add `"validation.report" => Some(validation::layer),` to
//! `src/plugins/mod.rs`, and forward `configure_router` to `self.inner`.

use brocanth::plugin_api::{Lifecycle, Plugin, PluginError};
use brocanth::plugin_context::{ContextBuilder, ProvidesContext};
use brocanth_schema::{ReportStyle, Validate, ValidationConfig, ValidationPlugin};

// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
// A schema
// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

/// What somebody may send to sign up.
///
/// A constraint that cannot mean anything for a field's type does not
/// compile, and neither does a rule naming a field this struct does not have:
/// the derive reads the field list from the struct, so there is no string to
/// misspell.
#[derive(Debug, Validate)]
/// The shape a sign-up body has to have.
///
/// Allowed to be unconstructed: a handler is what builds one, and a
/// handler cannot reach the per-request context yet — `Ctx` in
/// `src/pages/mod.rs` has no extractor, so `Valid<SignUp>` has nowhere to
/// be asked for. Delete this attribute with the first route that takes a
/// body.
#[allow(dead_code)]
pub struct SignUp {
    /// Three to thirty-two characters.
    #[validate(string(min_length = 3, max_length = 32))]
    pub username: String,
    /// Checked against the shape of an address, not against a mail server.
    #[validate(string(email))]
    pub email: String,
}

// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
// The plugin
// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

/// What this plugin is built from.
///
/// It derives `Deserialize` because this application reads it from
/// `[validation]` in a TOML file. Drop the config plugin and the derive goes
/// with it: `Plugin::Config` carries no bounds the core imposes.
#[derive(Debug, serde::Deserialize)]
#[serde(default, deny_unknown_fields)]
pub struct Settings {
    /// `json` or `form`. A string here and an enum below: the config file is
    /// text, and the translation is checked at startup where a typo can still
    /// be answered.
    pub report: String,
}

impl Default for Settings {
    fn default() -> Self {
        Self {
            report: "json".to_string(),
        }
    }
}

pub struct Validation {
    report: String,
    /// Built at `configure` and not in `new`, because reading the report
    /// style out of a string can fail and `Plugin::new` cannot.
    inner: Option<ValidationPlugin>,
}

impl Plugin for Validation {
    type Config = Settings;
    type Provides = ProvidesContext<brocanth_schema::Validation>;
    const NAME: &'static str = "validation";

    fn new(config: Settings) -> Self {
        Self {
            report: config.report,
            inner: None,
        }
    }

    fn configure(&mut self, _lc: &mut Lifecycle<'_>) -> Result<(), PluginError> {
        let report = match self.report.as_str() {
            "json" => ReportStyle::Json,
            "form" => ReportStyle::Form,
            other => {
                return Err(PluginError::new(format!(
                    "[validation] report is {other:?}; write json or form"
                )))
            }
        };
        // The crate's own plugin, held rather than registered: it is the only
        // thing that can build the context item, because `Validation`'s field
        // is private and this file is not that crate.
        self.inner = Some(ValidationPlugin::new(ValidationConfig { report }));
        Ok(())
    }

    fn provide(&self, cx: &mut ContextBuilder) {
        // `configure` runs before the server is handed the application, so
        // the inner plugin is here by the time any request builds a context.
        if let Some(inner) = &self.inner {
            inner.provide(cx);
        }
    }
}
===== src/plugins/webhooks_in/mod.rs =====
//! Incoming webhooks, as this application receives them.
//!
//! A delivery is a POST to `{path}/{source}`. The hook in this file answers
//! it before the CSRF check, and only after the signature is valid and the
//! timestamp is inside the replay window. Then the body decodes into the
//! source's event type, the idempotency key is claimed, and the handler
//! runs. A copy of a delivery that already ran is answered 200 and runs
//! nothing. A handler that fails gives the key back, and the sender's retry
//! runs it again.
//!
//! The idempotency keys are rows in the `db` module's database, so every
//! instance sees the same keys and a restart forgets none.
//!
//! # Adding a source
//!
//! A source is a name, a scheme and a handler. Add it in `configure`:
//!
//! ```ignore
//! .source("stripe", Scheme::stripe(&stripe_secret)?, Payments)?
//! .source("github", Scheme::github(&github_secret)?, Pushes)?
//! ```
//!
//! `Scheme::standard` is Standard Webhooks (svix, and what
//! `brocanth-webhooks` sends), `Scheme::stripe` is Stripe, `Scheme::github`
//! is GitHub, and `Scheme::hmac_sha256` is any sender that puts a hex MAC of
//! the body in one header. A scheme with no timestamp has no replay window;
//! only the idempotency key stops a replay there.

use std::sync::Arc;

use brocanth::plugin_api::{Lifecycle, Plugin, PluginError};
use brocanth::plugin_context::NoContext;
use brocanth::plugin_hooks::{HookRegistrar, HookSource, OnAsyncRequest};
use brocanth::plugin_order::Order;
use brocanth_webhooks::{Delivery, Handler, Incoming, Receiver, Rejection, Scheme};

// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
// A source
// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

/// What the `example` source sends: a Standard Webhooks envelope. Replace
/// it with the event your sender documents. Fields it does not name are
/// ignored.
#[derive(Debug, serde::Deserialize)]
pub struct ExampleEvent {
    /// The event type: `invoice.paid`.
    #[serde(rename = "type")]
    pub kind: String,
}

/// Handles the `example` source.
pub struct Example;

impl Handler for Example {
    type Event = ExampleEvent;

    async fn handle(&self, event: ExampleEvent, delivery: &Delivery) -> Result<(), Rejection> {
        // Do the work, or record it, before the `Ok`: the sender stops
        // retrying at the 200.
        let (kind, id) = (event.kind, &delivery.id);
        crate::plugins::say!(info, "webhook {kind} received ({id})"; %kind, %id);
        Ok(())
    }
}

// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
// The plugin
// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

/// What this plugin is built from.
///
/// It derives `Deserialize` because this application reads it from
/// `[webhooks_in]` in a TOML file. Drop the config plugin and the derive goes
/// with it: `Plugin::Config` carries no bounds the core imposes.
#[derive(Debug, serde::Deserialize)]
#[serde(default, deny_unknown_fields)]
pub struct Settings {
    /// Where deliveries arrive: `{path}/{source}`.
    pub path: String,
    /// Seconds a signed timestamp may be off: the replay window.
    pub tolerance: i64,
    /// The largest delivery accepted, in bytes.
    pub max_bytes: usize,
    /// The `example` source's Standard Webhooks secret, `whsec_...`.
    pub secret: String,
    /// Create the idempotency table on the first readiness check.
    pub migrate: bool,
}

impl Default for Settings {
    fn default() -> Self {
        Self {
            path: "/webhooks".to_string(),
            tolerance: 300,
            max_bytes: 1_048_576,
            secret: "whsec_ZGV2ZWxvcG1lbnQgb25seTogcmVwbGFjZSBtZQ==".to_string(),
            migrate: true,
        }
    }
}

pub struct WebhooksIn {
    settings: Settings,
    /// Built at `configure`, because a secret can be wrong and
    /// `Plugin::new` cannot fail.
    receiver: Option<Receiver>,
}

impl Plugin for WebhooksIn {
    type Config = Settings;
    type Provides = NoContext;
    const NAME: &'static str = "webhooks_in";
    const INSTALLS_HOOKS: bool = true;

    fn new(config: Settings) -> Self {
        Self {
            settings: config,
            receiver: None,
        }
    }

    fn configure(&mut self, _lc: &mut Lifecycle<'_>) -> Result<(), PluginError> {
        let example = Scheme::standard(&self.settings.secret)
            .map_err(|e| PluginError::new(format!("[webhooks_in] secret: {e}")))?;
        let receiver = Incoming::new(&self.settings.path)
            .tolerance(self.settings.tolerance)
            .max_bytes(self.settings.max_bytes)
            .source("example", example, Example)?
            .build()?;
        self.receiver = Some(receiver);
        Ok(())
    }

    fn before_start(&mut self, lc: &mut Lifecycle<'_>) -> Result<(), PluginError> {
        // The pool the `db` plugin opened. It is lazy, so nothing here
        // waits; the first readiness check creates the table.
        let (Some(receiver), Some(db)) = (
            &self.receiver,
            lc.services().get::<brocanth_db::Db>().cloned(),
        ) else {
            return Ok(());
        };
        let seen = Arc::new(brocanth_webhooks::PgSeen::new(db));
        receiver.set_seen(seen.clone());
        if self.settings.migrate {
            let migrated = Arc::new(tokio::sync::OnceCell::new());
            lc.ready_when(move || {
                let seen = Arc::clone(&seen);
                let migrated = Arc::clone(&migrated);
                async move {
                    migrated
                        .get_or_try_init(|| seen.migrate())
                        .await
                        .map(drop)
                        .map_err(|e| {
                            format!(
                                "creating the webhook keys table: {e}\n\
                                 help: check `[db] url`; with `migrate = false`, run \
                                 `brocanth_webhooks::PgSeen::SCHEMA` in a migration"
                            )
                        })
                }
            });
        }
        Ok(())
    }
}

impl HookSource for WebhooksIn {
    type Families = OnAsyncRequest;

    fn install_hooks(&self, registrar: &mut HookRegistrar<'_>) -> Result<(), PluginError> {
        if let Some(receiver) = &self.receiver {
            // Before the CSRF check, which would refuse a POST with no
            // token. After the proxy reader and the rate limiter, so the
            // client's address is read and a flood is refused first.
            registrar.request_async(
                "receive",
                Order::new()
                    .before_if_present("csrf.check")
                    .after_if_present("proxy-headers.read")
                    .after_if_present("rate_limit.count"),
                receiver.clone(),
            );
        }
        Ok(())
    }
}

#[cfg(test)]
mod tests {
    use brocanth::routing::Verb;
    use brocanth_server::Request;

    #[tokio::test]
    async fn an_unsigned_delivery_is_refused() {
        let app = crate::app::build().expect("the application builds");
        let mut request = Request::new(Verb::Post, "/webhooks/example")
            .with_header("content-type", "application/json");
        request.body = br#"{"type":"invoice.paid"}"#.to_vec().into();
        let response = crate::app::handle(&app, request).await;
        assert_eq!(response.status, 401);
    }
}
===== src/plugins/webhooks_out/mod.rs =====
//! Outgoing webhooks, as this application sends them.
//!
//! An endpoint is a URL, a secret and the events it wants. An event goes to
//! every active endpoint that wants it: one row in the delivery log and one
//! job each. The job posts the event, signed with Standard Webhooks, and the
//! queue retries a failure with backoff, eight times over about a day.
//!
//! A page reaches it through the context:
//!
//! ```ignore
//! let hooks = ctx.provided::<brocanth_webhooks::Outgoing, _>().expect("dispatched");
//!
//! // Once, when a partner registers. Show the secret to them, once.
//! let endpoint = hooks
//!     .add_endpoint("https://partner.example/hooks", &["invoice.*"], "billing")
//!     .await?;
//!
//! // Whenever something happens.
//! hooks.emit("invoice.paid", &serde_json::json!({ "id": 42 })).await?;
//! ```
//!
//! A row change becomes `<collection>.created`, `.updated` or `.deleted`
//! through `hooks.sink(&["invoices"])`, a `ChangeSink` the model layer
//! writes through. A change sink cannot fail the write, so an event that must
//! not be lost is emitted by the code that wrote the row.
//!
//! The deliveries are retried on the `webhooks` queue by a worker this
//! plugin runs, over the `db` module's pool. With the admin, they are a tab
//! at `/admin/webhooks`.

pub use brocanth_webhooks::{OutgoingConfig as Settings, OutgoingPlugin as WebhooksOut};
