===== AGENTS.md =====
<!-- Written by `brocanth`. Do not edit -- `brocanth add` and `brocanth remove` rewrite this file. -->
<!-- Notes of your own go in a file of your own, beside this one. -->
# Working in My Blog

A brief for a coding agent. Written by `brocanth`, so the lists
below are this project's and not the framework's in general.

## Check your work

Run these after every change, in this order. They are ordered by how
fast they fail.

```
cargo test
cargo clippy --all-targets -- -D warnings
cargo fmt --check
```

The route table is `pages::router()` and the test beside it asserts the
shape of the directory. Read it rather than guessing what covers a
request.

## What this binary contains

- `core`  the router, the context, the plugin seams
- `observability`  a request id on every request and a SIGTERM that fails /readyz first; logs, probes, metrics and traces below
- `observability-logs`  the production log: JSON on stdout in production, text elsewhere, with request id, duration and spans
- `observability-health`  /up for liveness, /readyz runs every plugin's check and fails first on SIGTERM
- `observability-metrics`  Prometheus text on /metrics: requests by route and status, in-flight, job queues
- `observability-tracing`  an OTLP span per request and per job, W3C traceparent in and out; needs a collector
- `analytics`  page views counted on the server: no cookie, no script, no third party; rows in your Postgres
- `config`  config/<env>.toml, merged; without it nothing links a TOML parser
- `sessions`  a cookie, and a `Session` the context hands a handler
- `db`  one Postgres pool and sea-query statements, read as ctx.db(); without it no SQL driver
- `model`  SeaORM, validations, migrations, over the `db` pool
- `cache`  a cache on an unlogged Postgres table, not Redis; without it no cache at all
- `cache-redis`  the cache on Redis or Valkey instead of Postgres; one more server to run
- `jobs`  a queue in Postgres with retries and a dead letter; a worker in this process
- `jobs-memory`  the queue in this process's memory; for development and tests, gone at restart
- `flags`  feature flags: on for all, a percentage, users or groups; rows in your Postgres
- `audit`  change history: who changed which row, when, and what; rows in your Postgres
- `mail`  send mail through SMTP, or to the log or a folder in development
- `auth`  argon2id accounts and signed sessions; no provider, so no HTTP client
- `auth-oauth`  sign-in with GitHub, Google, Microsoft or GitLab at /oauth; links an HTTP client
- `auth-screens`  sign-up, sign-in, sign-out, reset, verify and TOTP pages under /account
- `auth-lockout`  locks an account for minutes after too many wrong passwords
- `superuser`  operator accounts that pass every admin check, and a createsuperuser command
- `roles`  roles that grant permissions, stored with the accounts, for staff and the admin
- `policy`  typed predicates over an entity's columns; no ORM, no driver, no core
- `storage`  uploads on a local disk, addressed by content hash; no object store and no S3
- `storage-routes`  POST /files and GET /files/{address}?token=: uploads your guard allows, downloads behind a file token, thumbnails
- `input`  a form or a JSON body, read into a struct, with every fault at once
- `client`  a subcommand that calls this application's own API, built from its routes
- `middleware`  proxy headers, method override, canonical host, content types, CORS
- `middleware-csrf`  a double-submit cookie, checked on every verb that is not a read
- `middleware-security-headers`  HSTS, nosniff, a referrer policy, no framing, a CSP that runs dp-swap by hash
- `middleware-rate-limit`  a fixed window per address, session or user, per group of routes; 429 with Retry-After
- `middleware-conditional-get`  a weak ETag over every page, and 304 for If-None-Match or If-Modified-Since
- `middleware-compression`  gzip and brotli per Accept-Encoding on text responses, with Vary; never a stream
- `flash`  one-shot messages across a redirect, in a signed cookie, drawn into the layout
- `gtag`  Google Analytics 4 behind a consent banner; nothing leaves before the visitor accepts
- `openapi`  an OpenAPI 3.1 document derived from src/pages, served at /openapi.json
- `validation`  a zod-shaped vocabulary; a handler takes Valid<T> or does not compile
- `resource`  typed CRUD routes over one table, with the policy in the WHERE clause
- `admin`  a Django-shaped backoffice at /admin, locked until you say who may in
- `admin-model`  the admin's rows over SeaORM, one line per entity
- `admin-actions`  "Delete selected" on every admin list, with a confirmation page
- `webhooks`  signed webhooks: verified deliveries in, signed events out; pick a direction below
- `webhooks-in`  POST /webhooks/{source}: signature, replay window and idempotency key, then your typed handler
- `webhooks-out`  signed outgoing webhooks: endpoints and a delivery log in Postgres, retries through jobs
- `realtime`  one write is a map lookup, not a query per subscriber
- `realtime-sse`  one long response per subscriber, frames written as they are made
- `realtime-websocket`  a duplex socket per subscriber, frames both ways
- `commands`  this project's own command line, `serve` plus whatever you add
- `rsx`  `.rsx` templates compiled into the binary; without it a page builds a String
- `jt`  `.jt` templates compiled into the binary; a JSON answer without a struct per shape
- `rsx-tailwind`  a CSS build over the class names in your templates; needs node
- `rsx-htmx`  a click swaps what changed; the server renders from the shared layout down
- `sanitize`  an allow-list HTML sanitizer and a SafeHtml type; links html5ever
- `robots`  a robots.txt from your settings, served at /robots.txt; closed outside production
- `sitemap`  a sitemap.xml of every page with no parameter, served at /sitemap.xml
- `pagination`  offset and keyset pages, with links that keep the query; no dependency
- `filters`  typed filters and a sort from the query string, ANDed with the policy's rule in the WHERE
- `search`  Postgres full-text and trigram search: a site index, its migration, a page at /search
- `i18n`  translations checked at build time; a locale per request from the query, a cookie or Accept-Language
- `i18n-routing`  a locale segment in the path (/fr/about), a redirect from a path without one
- `meta`  a title, a description, a canonical URL and Open Graph tags for each page
- `errors`  HTML 404 and 500 pages in your layouts, the cause in a debug build, a reporter hook
- `docker`  a cargo-chef Dockerfile: slim Debian, a user that is not root, a HEALTHCHECK on /up
- `docker-kubernetes`  deploy/kubernetes.yaml: a Deployment and a Service whose probes and grace period match the drain
- `ci`  fmt, clippy and the tests on every push; pick the provider below
- `ci-github`  .github/workflows/ci.yml: fmt, clippy, tests, a Postgres service when there is a database, the image

**Absent:** `logging`, `beacon`, `cache-memory`, `static` — not compiled, not linked, absent from `Cargo.lock`. Do not write code that uses them; add the dependency and name the plugin in `main` first.

That is the framework's central rule: a capability nobody asked for is
not compiled and not linked. If you need one, add it to `Cargo.toml`
*and* name its plugin in `src/main.rs`. Nothing registers itself.

## Adding a route

A route is a file. `src/pages/about.rs` is `/about`;
`src/pages/users/[id].rs` is `/users/{id}`. **Write the file. That is
the whole of it** -- no `mod` line, no row to register, because there is
no list. `brocanth_pages::pages!` in `src/pages/mod.rs` reads this
directory while the crate compiles and writes both.

The verb comes off the handler's name: `handle` is GET, then
`handle_head`, `handle_post`, `handle_put`, `handle_patch`,
`handle_delete`, `handle_options`. A `pub fn handle_*` outside that
list is a build error naming the list, rather than a handler nothing
reaches; a page with no handler is a build error too. A private
`fn handle_*` is a helper and is left alone.

A handler may be `pub async fn` when it waits on something, such as a
database: the table awaits it and the wait holds no thread. A page that
waits on nothing stays a plain `fn`. An async handler is dropped at an
`.await` when the client goes away, so do not leave a write half done
across one.

**Adding a file does not rebuild on its own** -- nothing referenced it
yet. Run `touch src/pages/mod.rs` after creating a page or a layout.
Editing or deleting one is noticed.

A handler returns a value and never writes a response itself. That is
what makes a half-written body on an error impossible rather than
unlikely.
## Jobs in memory

The queue lives in this process (`jobs-memory`). Jobs survive no restart,
and two processes do not share them. It is for development and tests:
`brocanth check --deploy` refuses it. Run `brocanth remove jobs-memory`
before production; the queue then uses Postgres.

## Reading a body

A handler never parses `ctx.body` itself. It declares a struct with
`#[derive(zod::Entry)]`, puts the rules on the fields, and calls
`ctx.body_as()` or `ctx.read()`. The rules are values: a `const`
shared by two structs is one rule, and `zod::accepts!` makes the
compiler check a literal against it.

Report every fault, never the first. `Faults` already holds them all
and `by_name()` groups them per field; a handler that returns after
the first one makes a person submit the form three times.

Do not write `?` on a `zod::Faults`. The blanket `From` on `Error`
turns any `std::error::Error` into a 500, and a bad body is a 422.
`body_as` is the call that gets the status right.

## Edge middleware

`src/plugins/mod.rs` holds five commented registrations from
`brocanth-middleware`. Each encodes a fact about the deployment --
which proxy to trust, which host is canonical, which origins may call
-- so each is turned on by somebody who knows that fact. Do not
enable them as a set, and do not invent an address or an origin to
make one compile. To turn one on, copy it to `build()` in
`src/app.rs` and widen `type Core` by its hook families; the comment
above the five shows the type.

## Google Analytics

`brocanth_gtag::Gtag` is registered in `src/plugins/mod.rs`.
Its hook puts a consent banner and Google's tag into every HTML page,
before `</body>`. Nothing reaches Google before the visitor accepts.

Do not add a second Google tag, and do not send a page view yourself:
the hook scrubs the URL first. Never put an email address, a name or a
user id in a URL, a page title or an event: Google's terms forbid it,
and the scrubber only catches what it can recognise. A link with
`data-consent-manage` reopens the banner.

The choice lives in the visitor's browser. To keep a record of it on
the server, set `consent_log` to a file: each choice is one JSON line
with no address and no user agent.

The banner speaks the request's locale. Its words are the
`[gtag.banner]` keys of `locales/*.toml`; change them there, in every
catalog. The `banner.*` settings are only the fallback.

## The admin

`src/plugins/admin/mod.rs` mounts the backoffice at `/admin`. It ships
locked: the policy is `DenyAll` and `principal` returns an anonymous
principal, so every screen answers 403. Do not open it with
`AllowAllUnsafeForDevelopment` outside a test.

A model reaches the admin through `AdminConfig::resource(..)`: a
`brocanth_admin::AdminResource` with a `brocanth_policy::Policy` over
the model's rows. A model with no resource answers 403. Never filter
rows after a read; the row policy goes into the query.

## Admin actions

An action over selected rows belongs to one model, in
`src/plugins/admin/mod.rs`:
`.model("post", |m| m.action(BulkAction::set("publish", "Publish selected", [("published", true)])))`.
`BulkAction::set` writes fixed values; the admin refuses a column the
model lacks when it starts. `BulkAction::custom` runs your function and
writes through `cx.update` and `cx.delete`, which keep the row policy.
Never write to the database directly in an action: the policy and the
journal would not see the write.

## Webhooks

A webhook secret is a password. It goes in the config file or the
environment, never in code and never in a flag. Verify every incoming
delivery through `brocanth_webhooks::Scheme` before you read its body;
do not compare a signature with `==`, which leaks how many bytes matched.

## Realtime over server-sent events

`GET /realtime/events?topics=posts/*,posts/42` streams the broker's frames
to a browser. `src/plugins/realtime_sse/mod.rs` is the stream;
`src/pages/realtime/events.rs` is the route.

- Authorise every topic in `authorise` in `src/pages/realtime/events.rs`.
  Nothing is public until it is listed in `PUBLIC` or gated there by a
  policy (`Subscription::from_policy`). Never filter frames after delivery.
- Publish from a page after the write, with the row you already hold:

  ```rust
  let realtime = ctx.provided::<Broadcast, _>().expect("a dispatched request");
  let row = brocanth_realtime::Row::new().with("author_id", author);
  realtime.broker.publish_with_row(&brocanth_events::RowChange::created("posts", id), &row);
  ```

- Read it in the browser. The event name is the topic. `lagged` means the
  view may be stale: fetch it again.

  ```js
  const events = new EventSource("/realtime/events?topics=posts/*");
  events.addEventListener("posts/*", (e) => console.log(e.data));
  events.addEventListener("lagged", () => location.reload());
  ```

## Realtime over a WebSocket

`GET /realtime/socket?topics=posts/*` upgrades to a WebSocket that carries
the broker's frames. `src/plugins/realtime_websocket/mod.rs` is the loop;
`src/pages/realtime/socket.rs` is the route.

- Authorise every topic in `authorise` in `src/pages/realtime/socket.rs`.
  It runs for the topics in the URL and for each `subscribe <topic>` the
  browser sends later, so read what it needs (the user id) into `Viewer`
  when the socket opens. Never filter frames after delivery.
- A page on another site is refused by its `Origin`. List a second site of
  your own in `ALLOWED_ORIGINS`; never turn the check off.
- Each message is one record in the server-sent-events format: `id:`,
  `event:` (the topic) and `data:` lines. `lagged` means the view may be
  stale: fetch it again. A close with code 1001 means the server is
  stopping: reconnect with `?last=<id>`.

  ```js
  const socket = new WebSocket(`ws://${location.host}/realtime/socket?topics=posts/*`);
  socket.onmessage = (e) => console.log(e.data);
  socket.onopen = () => socket.send("subscribe posts/42");
  ```

## HTML from users

HTML a user wrote reaches a template only as a
`brocanth_sanitize::SafeHtml`. Make one with a policy:
`brocanth_sanitize::comment(&row.body)` for a comment,
`brocanth_sanitize::rich_text(&row.body)` for editor output, or a
`Policy` built once in a `static` for anything else. Write it with
`{! value }`.

Never write a `String` with `{! }`, and never widen a policy with
`style`, `class`, `id`, an `on*` attribute or a `javascript:` URL.
Store what the user wrote and clean it when the page renders, so a
stricter policy also covers old rows.

## Pagination

A list reads its page with `ctx.page_params()?` and builds a
`brocanth_pagination::Page` from the count. Draw it with `<PageNav pager={&pager} />` from
`src/pages/parts/pager.rsx`. The links come
from `ctx.links()`, which keeps the filters and the sort.

- Put the rule, the filters and the search in the `WHERE` of the
  count and of the page. Never drop rows after the page is read: page
  two then overlaps page one, and the total is wrong.
- Order by a unique key last. Without it, two pages can share a row.
- A long list or a feed uses a keyset page (`after=<cursor>`), not an
  offset: it reads one index seek, not every row before it.
- Build the keyset `WHERE` with `brocanth_pagination::Keyset`:
  `rule.and(keyset.after(params.after.as_ref()).map_err(|e|
  Error::new(e.status(), e.to_string()))?)`, compiled once, so a
  forged cursor only moves the window inside the rule. The column
  enum implements `brocanth_policy::Sortable`, so a cursor key of the
  wrong type is a 400 and not a database error. Keep the `map_err`:
  a bare `?` makes it a 500.
- A `before=<cursor>` page (the previous link) reads with
  `keyset.before(&cursor)` (the same `map_err`) and
  `keyset.reversed().order_by()`, then
  `KeysetPage::before` puts the rows back in order.

## Translations

Text a visitor reads lives in `locales/<tag>.toml`, one file per
locale. `locales/en.toml` is the default and has every key. `build.rs`
compiles the files: each key is a function, `t::welcome(locale)`, so a
misspelt key or a missing argument does not compile. Do not write
visible text as a literal in a page: add the key to `en.toml`, then to
each translation. A key a translation lacks is a build warning, and
that locale shows the English text.

A page reads the locale with `crate::plugins::i18n::locale(ctx)`. The
hook in `src/plugins/i18n/mod.rs` chose it from `?locale=`, the
`locale` cookie, then `Accept-Language`. A link with `?locale=fr` is a
language switcher: the hook remembers the choice in the cookie.

A hook in another crate cannot name `Locale`. It reads
`brocanth_i18n::Current` from the request state and calls
`current.text("key.path")`, which answers keys with no argument.

In an `.rsx` page, take the locale as a parameter (`locale: Locale`)
and write `{t::welcome(locale)}`: rsx escapes it like any value. The
layout cannot see the request, so it says `lang="en"`; put
`lang={locale}` on the page's own root element.

In a `.jt` template, `"title": t::welcome(locale)` writes the
translation as a JSON string.

The consent banner's text is the `[gtag.banner]` table of each catalog.
The `banner.*` settings of `gtag` are the fallback for a key no catalog
has. Change the wording in the catalogs.

## One URL per locale

Every page answers under a locale segment: `/fr/about`, `/en/about`.
The `i18n` hook takes the segment off, so `src/pages/about.rs` still
answers `/about` and `ctx.path` is `/about`. A GET to `/about` gets a
307 to the locale the visitor asks for. Do not add a page under
`src/pages/fr/`: the segment is not a directory.

Write a link with `ctx.href("/about")`, which keeps the page's
locale. A language switcher links to `/fr/about`, and the hook
remembers the choice in the cookie. A path under an `unprefixed`
prefix of `[i18n]` (`/api`), the routes of other modules (the admin,
the file routes) and a file such as `/robots.txt` keep no segment.

## Page metadata

Every page has a title and share tags. A page sets its own with
`ctx.meta()` before it renders: `ctx.meta().title("About")`, then
`.description(..)`, `.image(..)`, `.canonical(..)`, `.kind("article")`
or `.noindex()`. What a page leaves out comes from `[meta]`. Do not
write a `<title>` or a `<meta>` tag in a page: the root layout writes
them from `ctx.head()`. Set `base_url` in production, or no page has a
canonical URL.

A page that builds its own HTML writes `ctx.head()` in its `<head>`:
its `Display` is the tags, escaped. A JSON answer (`.jt`) takes
`jt::Raw(&ctx.head().to_json())`.

The root layout declares `meta: &Head`, and `html!` fills it with
`ctx.head()`. It writes `<title>{meta.title()}</title>` and `{! meta }`.
Only a layout that writes `<head>` declares `meta`.

## The database

`src/plugins/db/mod.rs` opens one Postgres pool at startup from `[db] url`.
A page reads it with `ctx.db()`, a `&brocanth_db::Db`. Build a statement
with `brocanth_db::sea_query`. Do not format values into SQL text: sea-query
sends each value as a bound parameter.

```rust
use brocanth_db::sea_query::{Expr, ExprTrait, Order, Query};
use brocanth_db::Exec;

use crate::pages::{Ctx, Error, IntoResponse};

pub async fn handle(ctx: &mut Ctx) -> Result<impl IntoResponse, Error> {
    let q = Query::select()
        .columns(["id", "title"])
        .from("posts")
        .and_where(Expr::col("published").eq(true))
        .order_by("id", Order::Desc)
        .limit(10)
        .to_owned();
    let posts: Vec<(i64, String)> = ctx.db().all(&q).await?;
    Ok(posts
        .iter()
        .map(|(id, title)| format!("{id} {title}\n"))
        .collect::<String>())
}
```

`ctx.db().json(&q).await?` returns each row as a `serde_json::Value`,
encoded by Postgres. Writes that belong together go in one transaction.
`Ok` commits, and `Err` rolls back:

```rust
ctx.db()
    .transact(async |tx| {
        tx.execute(&debit).await?;
        tx.execute(&credit).await?;
        Ok(())
    })
    .await?;
```

A `brocanth_db::DbError` goes through `?` as a 500. Its message goes to the
log, never to the client.

## The client

`src/commands/api.rs` is a command line for this application's own
API. It is built from `crate::pages::described()` at run time, not
generated, so there is nothing to regenerate and nothing to keep in
step: add a page with a typed signature and the client grows a
subcommand with the right flags.

Do not add validation to it. The server checks the body and answers
422 with every fault; a client that checked as well would be a second
opinion, and the two would disagree the first time a rule changed.

## CSRF

`brocanth_middleware::Csrf` is registered in `src/plugins/mod.rs`. It
is a double-submit cookie: the hook mints a token, sets it as its own
cookie, and refuses any verb that is not GET, HEAD, OPTIONS or TRACE
unless the request sends the same value back -- in the `_csrf_token`
field, or in the `X-CSRF-Token` header.

So every form that posts carries the hidden field. In a page, read
the token with `ctx.hooked::<brocanth_middleware::CsrfToken>()` and
write `token.hidden_field()` inside the `<form>`. For HTML this
project did not template, `token.stamp_forms(html)` adds the field to
every posting form; the admin uses it.

A 403 from this hook is a form that lost its token, or a caller that
is not a browser. Add that path to `exempt`; do not remove the
registration.

## CSS

```
brocanth css         # the CSS build; -W rebuilds on each change
```

The stylesheet is embedded, so a new one is a new binary. `css` rewrites
`src/assets.rs` to say so, which is what makes cargo notice.

## Analytics

`brocanth_analytics::Analytics` is registered in `src/plugins/mod.rs`.
It counts every GET answered with `text/html`, except paths under
`[analytics] exclude`, bots and prefetches. Nothing to add to a page:
no script, no cookie, no banner.

Do not put personal data in a URL. The path and the allowed query
parameters are stored; an email-shaped segment is replaced, but a name
or an account number is not. Do not add a column to
`brocanth_analytics_views` that identifies a person.

With the admin, the numbers are on its `analytics` tab, behind the
admin's policy like every tab, and the admin's own pages are never
counted.

## Feature flags

Declare every flag as a `Flag` constant in `src/plugins/flags/mod.rs`
and add it to `ALL`. Read it with
`crate::plugins::flags::enabled(ctx, crate::plugins::flags::NAME)`, never
with a string key: a constant cannot be misspelt.

A flag with no stored rule is off. Ship the code first, then turn the
flag on. When a flag is on for everyone for good, delete the constant
and the branch that reads it; the admin tab then lists the stored rule
as not declared, so it can be removed.

## Provider sign-in

`src/plugins/auth_oauth/mod.rs` mounts the OAuth flow at `/oauth`. A
sign-in button is a form that posts to `/oauth/<provider>`, never a
link that starts the flow: a GET that starts a sign-in can be fired by
any page on the web.

Never link a provider to an account because the addresses match. The
flow links only on a POST from a session that is signed in as that
account, so a provider that reports somebody else's address cannot
take the account over.

A client secret goes in `$<APP>_AUTH_OAUTH__<PROVIDER>_CLIENT_SECRET`,
never in a committed config file.

## Account lockout

`src/plugins/auth_lockout.rs` builds the lockout with the policy in
`src/modules/auth_lockout.rs`. Sign in through `lockout(&auth)?.login(..)`,
not `auth.accounts().login(..)`, or wrong passwords are not counted.
Render `Attempt::Refused` and `Attempt::JustLocked` with the same words
and status; mail the owner on `JustLocked`. Call `clear` after a password
reset.

## Superusers

`src/plugins/superuser/mod.rs` reads the signed-in member of staff with
`staff(ctx)`. A superuser passes every check of the admin. Never set the
superuser flag from a form or a page: an operator sets it, with
`[superuser] email` at start or the `createsuperuser` command.

## File routes

Who may upload is `may_upload` in `src/plugins/storage_routes/mod.rs`. It
answers no until you change it; ask the policy there, not in a page. A
download needs a token: mint it with `FileStore::tokens` after your policy
allowed the read, and never log the URL. Do not write an upload or
download page beside these routes.

## Security headers

`brocanth_middleware::SecurityHeaders` is registered in
`src/plugins/mod.rs`. Its Content-Security-Policy
allows inline scripts by hash only. To inline a new script, add
`Csp::default().inline_script(TEXT)` with the exact text between the
tags. Do not add `'unsafe-inline'` to `script-src`. A value that
depends on the request goes in a `data-` attribute or a `<meta>` tag,
not in the script.

HSTS is sent only over HTTPS. Set `behind_tls` when a TLS terminator
is in front. Set `include_subdomains` or `preload` only when the
person who owns the domain says so.

## Incoming webhooks

A source is a name, a `Scheme` and a `Handler`, added in `configure` in
`src/plugins/webhooks_in/mod.rs`. Do not add a page for a webhook, and do
not add its path to the CSRF exempt list: the hook answers before the CSRF
check and only for a verified delivery. A handler returns `Err` to have
the sender retry; it runs once per idempotency key.

## Views

The markup for a route is a `.rsx` file **next to the page that renders it**:
`src/pages/index.rsx` beside `src/pages/index.rs`. There is no second tree and
nothing to register -- `rsx::load_dir!("src/pages")` in `src/pages/mod.rs`
compiles them all into the binary. One file is one module, under `views`, so
`index.rsx` is `views::index` and `users/[id].rsx` is `views::users::_id_`.

### Layouts

`layout.rsx` wraps every page in its directory **and every directory below**,
and a page never names it: which layout wraps a page is a property of where
the page is. That is the Crystal page_router's rule for `layout.cr`.

They stack root to leaf, and **the chain is not written down anywhere**.
`html!` is a macro: it knows which file it was written in, walks up to
`src/`, and wraps the page in every `layout.rsx` it passes. So
`src/pages/users/layout.rsx` wraps the pages under `users/` inside the root
layout, and deleting that file removes exactly that layer with no other edit.

Every page uses the same import and the same call, whatever directory it is
in. Do not add a chain of your own; if a page is wrapped wrongly, the
directory tree is what to change.

A layout takes `children: Element`, and may also take `layout: &str`, which
`html!` fills with the URL prefix that layout covers. Those two are the only
parameters it fills, because it cannot know what a third one would mean.
A value every page shares comes from a `const` -- the root layout reads
`pages::TITLE` that way. A value belonging to one request belongs on the page
component, which the handler builds and which already has the context.

For a fragment that is not a document -- an htmx swap, a partial -- call
`render` instead of `html!`: the same thing with no shell.

**Adding a `layout.rsx` where there was none does not rebuild anything**,
because nothing referenced the file yet. Run `touch src/pages/mod.rs` after
creating one. Editing or deleting an existing layout is noticed.

A component is a function returning `Element`. Its parameters are its contract
with the handler, and a caller fills them as struct fields, so a field the
component does not declare is a compile error rather than a blank in the page.
A parameter of type `Element` receives children, which is all a layout is.

Reach a template from a handler with `html!`, and with nothing else:

```rust
pub fn handle(_ctx: &mut Ctx) -> Result<impl IntoResponse, Error> {
    html!(ctx, views::index::Index { title: TITLE })
}
```

It returns the handler's own `Result`, so there is no `Ok(..)` to write and a
template that bails propagates with `?`. For another status or a header,
compose with the other return shapes: `Ok((html!(ctx, tpl)?, 404))`.

Adding a page with markup is two files in one directory -- `<name>.rs` and
`<name>.rsx` -- and nothing else anywhere. Neither needs a line in
`src/pages/mod.rs`, because the directory is the registry.

Not every response is a page. A redirect is `Response::empty(303)` with a
header and no template anywhere near it.

An interpolated value is escaped on the way out. Templates are read at
**compile** time, so an edit needs a rebuild, and a template that does not
parse is a build failure on its own line rather than a 500.

## Static files

Two directories, and the difference matters. `assets/` is **fingerprinted**:
`site.css` is published at `/css/site.<hash>.css` and cached for a year.
`public/` is served **verbatim**, for names that cannot move -- `robots.txt`,
`favicon.ico`, anything linked to from outside.

Never write either URL by hand. `crate::assets::url("css/site.css")` takes the
logical path and returns the published one, and a name nothing publishes
panics there rather than 404ing in a browser.

A basename starting with `_` is an input and is never published, which is what
`assets/css/_tailwind.css` is.

Both directories are embedded in the binary. **Adding a file where there was
none does not rebuild anything**; run `touch src/assets.rs` after creating one.

## Partial navigation

Link to a route with the `Link` component, never a bare `<a>`:

```
<Link to={&crate::pages::routes::users::id::path(7)}>User 7</Link>
```

`to` takes what a route helper returns, so a link to a route that does not
exist is a compile error. It renders a real `<a href>` with `dp-boost` on it,
so the page still works with no JavaScript.

A layout marks what a swap may replace with `dp-layout={layout}`, on the
element wrapping **exactly** the children. Anything inside that element is
replaced with them, so a sidebar or a scroll position belongs outside it.

A request carrying `DP-Partial: n` drops the outermost `n` layouts, and
`html!` resolves that into a `match` over the chain. A handler never reads the
header and never changes for it.

## Search

`/search` ranks the rows of `search_documents`, the site index.
`migrations/m20260101_000000_search_documents.rs` creates the table. Run `migrate sync`
once so `migrations/mod.rs` lists it, then `migrate up`.

- Write a row with `brocanth_search::documents::upsert(ctx.db(), kind,
  key, title, body, url)` when a thing changes, in the same
  transaction or job, and `documents::remove` when it goes.
- Every row is public. Index only what any visitor may read, or put
  the rule in `src/pages/search.rs`: it goes into the `WHERE`, so a
  hidden row is never ranked, counted or shown.
- Never filter hits after the page is read. The total and the pages
  then disagree.
- To search one table instead, declare a `brocanth_search::Index`
  over its entity and write `index.migration()` into a migration.

## Change history

Publish every write of the model layer to
`crate::plugins::audit::sink(ctx)`: `insert_published`,
`update_published`, `delete_published`. A write that is not published
is not in the history. Fill `actor` in `src/plugins/audit/mod.rs` once
the project has users, so each change names who made it.

Do not put a secret in a column you write through the sink with the
admin: the admin records the values before and after.

## Mail

`src/plugins/mail/mod.rs` registers the mail plugin. A page reads the
`Outbox` with `ctx.provided::<crate::plugins::mail::Outbox, _>()` and
builds a message with `outbox.message()`, which sets `From`.

Use `outbox.deliver_later(message)` in a request, not `send`: with the
`jobs` module the message goes on a queue and a slow relay does not hold
the response. Never build an `Smtp` in a page, and never put the SMTP
password in a file that is in git or on a command line.

In development `[mail] transport` is `log`: each message is in the log,
and nothing is sent.

## Roles

Check a permission with `crate::plugins::roles::require(ctx, "<name>")`.
A role answers which people; it does not answer which rows. Put a row
rule in a `brocanth_policy::Policy`, and combine it with a permission
through `Staff::gate`. Never filter rows after a paginated load.

## Rate limit

`brocanth_middleware::RateLimit` is registered in
`src/plugins/mod.rs` with one example rule (`login`, 5 per 60 s).
The numbers are an example. Do not invent limits: ask for the limit
and the window of each route group.

A request whose key cannot be found is not counted. Behind a proxy,
the address comes from `ProxyHeaders` or from `ip_header`. Never set
`ip_header` to a header the client can send, such as
`X-Forwarded-For`, unless the edge overwrites it.

## Outgoing webhooks

Send an event with `Outgoing::emit`, from the code that changed the data.
Do not post to a partner's URL from a page: the delivery log, the
signature and the retries come from `emit`. Show an endpoint's secret
once, to the person who registered it, and never log it.

## Errors

A page fails with `Err(Error::new(status, message))` or `?`, and
`src/plugins/errors/mod.rs` turns the failure into a page. Do not build
an error page in a handler. A 404 is `Error::new(404, "no user 7")`. A
4xx message is shown to the visitor; a 5xx message is logged and never
shown, so put the cause in it.

An error with a `content-type` header, and a client that does not
accept HTML, keep the plain answer: an API stays an API.

To hear about every 5xx, implement `brocanth_errors::Reporter` and add
it in a plugin's `after_config`:
`lc.services().get::<brocanth_errors::Reporters>()`. A reporter must
not wait.

`not-found.rsx` answers a 404 and `error.rsx` every other failure, for
the pages in its directory and below it, inside the layouts above it.
The nearest file wins. The components are `NotFound` and `ErrorPage`,
and each may declare `caught: &Caught`. After you add one where there
was none, run `touch src/pages/mod.rs`.

## Conditional GET

`brocanth_middleware::ConditionalGet` is registered in
`src/plugins/mod.rs`. It tags every `200` answer to a GET with a weak
ETag over the body and answers `304` when the browser already has it.
So a page must render the same bytes for the same content: no
timestamp or random value in the markup, or every tag differs and no
request is ever a `304`.

A page that knows when its data changed sets `Last-Modified` as an
IMF-fixdate (`Sun, 06 Nov 1994 08:49:37 GMT`). A page whose answer is
for one user only sets `Cache-Control: private`; one that must not be
kept sets `no-store`, and gets no tag.

## Flash

`brocanth_middleware::FlashPlugin` is registered in
`src/plugins/mod.rs`. After a form POST, write a message and redirect:

```rust
if let Some(flash) = ctx.hooked_mut::<brocanth_middleware::Flash>() {
    flash.success("Saved.");
}
Ok(Response::empty(303).with_header("location", "/"))
```

The next HTML page shows it inside `<div id="flash">`, which the root
layout carries. Keep that element in the layout. A page that draws a
form again without a redirect uses `flash.now(level, text)`. The text
is plain: the hook escapes it. Never put HTML or a secret in a message.

## Filters

A list declares its filters once with `brocanth_filters::Filters`,
over the entity's column enum, and reads them with
`ctx.filters(&declared)?`.

- Compile with `applied.condition::<E>(policy.list(&who), dialect)`.
  It ANDs the filters with the rule, so a filter narrows what the
  rule admits and never widens it.
- Give the count and the page the same condition. Never filter rows
  after a paginated load: the total is then wrong and pages overlap.
- Order with `applied.order_by::<E>(key)`: the primary key comes last,
  so two pages never share a row.
- Do not parse a filter from the query string by hand. A name that is
  not declared names no column, and that is the guarantee.

- Write page links with `ctx.links()`: each keeps the filters and
  the sort.

## Account screens

`src/pages/account/` holds the sign-up, sign-in, sign-out, reset,
verify and TOTP pages; their markup is `src/pages/account/screens.rsx`,
and the helpers are in `src/plugins/auth_screens/mod.rs`. Where a person
lands after signing in (`after_sign_in`) and how long a sign-in waits
for its second factor (`pending_secs`) are in
`src/modules/auth_screens.rs`.

Rules when you change them:

- Answer alike for an address with an account and one without, in the
  words and the status. Say which it was only in the mail.
- Build a link in mail with `Screens::link`, which reads
  `[auth_screens] base_url`. Never build one from the `Host` header.
- Every form carries `csrf_field(ctx)`, written with `{! }`.
- A page that changes state answers a POST, never a GET.
- Read who is signed in with `auth_screens::signed_in`. Do not trust a
  cookie value you did not unseal.

## Compression

`brocanth_middleware::Compression` is registered in
`src/plugins/mod.rs`. Its response hook runs after every hook that
reads or rewrites the body, so a page never compresses its own
output. Do not set `Content-Encoding` in a page unless the body is
already encoded; the hook then leaves it alone.

A stream is never compressed. To keep a buffered body as it is, set
`Cache-Control: no-transform`. A hook of your own that rewrites the
body goes in `runs_after`, so compression waits for it.

## Tasks

A command is a `clap::Args` struct with `#[cmd]` on it, in its own file under
`src/commands/`, plus a `pub mod` line at the end of `src/commands/mod.rs`,
below the line that starts `// Your commands`. `brocanth add` rewrites the
lines above it and keeps yours. The command becomes a subcommand
automatically — registration happens at link time.

```
brocanth --help      # lists this project's commands too
brocanth serve
```

## Decisions that belong to the person

The modules in this project are the person's choice. Do not add or
remove one on your own. Before you run `brocanth add`, show the person
the question: `brocanth add --questions` lists each module with its
default, what it needs and what it recommends. Give your
recommendation, and wait for the answer.

## House style

- A comment says **why**, next to the decision, and what was chosen
  against. Not what the line does.
- A test name is a sentence describing the guarantee it protects.
- Prefer a type that cannot be built wrong over a check that can be
  forgotten.
- State a limit where someone would look for it rather than leaving it
  to be discovered.

## Framework reference

`docs/llm-authoring.md` in the brocanth checkout is the full brief: what
cannot compile and can therefore be relied on, what can still go wrong
and therefore needs a test, and the crate map.
